L950: ## Adequacy of the Safety Database
L951: A significant dropout rate was observed during the 52-week treat-through trial APD334-301. The most common reason for study discontinuation was disease worsening, which was predefined in the protocol to ensure subjects who were not receiving benefit could leave the trial to receive additional treatment. Refer to Section 8.1.2 for details regarding subject disposition. Additional safety data from open label studies (ES101002, APD334-005, and APD334-303) and the Japanese study APD334-308 provided supportive safety information. This supportive safety information included longer-term data from subjects that completed 12 weeks of treatment in APD334-302 and APD334-003 and data from additional subjects enrolled in ES101002 and APD334-308. Refer to Section 7.2 for additional details.
L952: Given the duration of exposures in the trials, the safety database is deemed sufficient to characterize the safety of etrasimod in adults with moderately to severely active UC when used through 52 weeks. It is acknowledged that a pre-market database in UC contains limited data to fully assess the potential for rare events, particularly those of long latency (e.g., malignancy). Nevertheless, the size and scope of the submitted safety database is acceptable to inform a risk-benefit assessment for the proposed indication of the treatment of moderately to severely active UC.
L953: ## 8.2.3. Adequacy of Applicant's Clinical Safety Assessments
L954: ## Issues Regarding Data Integrity and Submission Quality
L955: No major issues regarding data integrity or submission quality were identified.
L956: ## Categorization of Adverse Events
L957: A treatment emergent adverse event (TEAE) was defined as any adverse event that started or worsened in severity on or after the first dose of study treatment. Terms are coded using MedDRA version 24.1. Serious adverse events (SAEs) were appropriately defined consistent with 21 CFR 312.32(a) and reported as required. The baseline period was the screening period.
L958: All AEs, including SAEs, were monitored until the subject discontinued and/or completed the study. The adverse event reporting period for safety surveillance began when the subject was initially included in the study (date of first signature of informed consent) and continued up to
L959: 30 days after the last administration of study treatment. If an adverse event was not resolved or stabilized by that time, the Applicant in consultation with the Investigator decided whether to continue to monitor the adverse event or closeout the event in the database if no further follow-up was necessary. Any SAE suspected to be related to the study treatment was reported whenever it occurred, irrespective of the time elapsed since the last administration of study treatment. Any SAE that was ongoing when the subject completed the study or discontinued the study was followed by the Investigator until the event resolved, stabilized, or returned to baseline status.
L960: All AEs were graded as mild, moderate, or severe as per the Investigator's judgement. The causal relationship between the investigational drug and the AE was characterized by the Investigator as related or not related, according to definitions outlined in the study protocol.
L961: Adverse events of special interest (AESIs) were specified as being of special interest based on prior experience with other agents acting via a similar mechanism; these included bradycardia, heart conduction abnormalities (2nd degree and higher AV block), elevated blood pressure, macular edema, serious or opportunistic infection, pulmonary effects, hepatic effects, posterior reversible encephalopathy syndrome (PRES), and malignancy. Within the category of infections, severe infections, opportunistic infections (including progressive multifocal leukoencephalopathy), and herpes simplex and herpes zoster were considered AESIs. Refer to Section 8.2.4.4 for AESI definitions used in the trials.
L962: ## Routine Clinical Tests
L963: For each subject, the Applicant collected laboratory tests, radiologic tests, electrocardiograms (ECGs), and ophthalmoscopy according to the Schedule of Activities defined in the study protocol.
L964: Screening samples for complete blood count (CBC) with differential, platelet count, lymphocyte counts, T lymphocytes, B lymphocytes, natural killer lymphocytes (TBNK), serum chemistry, virology, thyroid panel, coagulation, urinalysis, high-sensitivity C-reactive protein (hs-CRP), TB screen, and stool sample were obtained and reviewed prior to randomization. Safety endpoints included the incidence and severity of laboratory abnormalities and change from baseline in laboratory values. Refer to Table 108 in Section 16.5 of the Appendix for details on the laboratory tests performed during the trials.
L965: All laboratory tests with values considered clinically significantly abnormal during participation in the study or within 30 days after the last dose of study treatment were repeated until the values return to normal or baseline or are no longer considered clinically significant by the Investigator or Medical Monitor. AE included clinically relevant adverse changes in laboratory parameters over the course of the study.
L966: Total white blood cell (WBC), neutrophil, lymphocyte, and TBNK counts were available for review prior to randomization. Since lymphopenia is a known class effect of S1P receptor
L967: modulators, Investigators were blinded to the complete blood count lab results after randomization. An unblinded Medical Monitor assessed WBC differential lab results and alerted the Investigator if (1) ANC &lt; 1000 cells/\muL and/or (2) ALC &lt;200 cells/\muL.
L968: All laboratory assessments required by the protocol were performed by a central laboratory unless otherwise stated.

Overall, there was a higher rate of AEs in the etrasimod group vs. placebo, with most AEs in both groups being mild or moderate. The proportion of subjects who reported serious adverse events (SAEs) and AEs leading to discontinuation was similar between etrasimod and placebo.

L985: The most frequently reported SAE was “colitis ulcerative” (i.e., ulcerative colitis). Ulcerative colitis and colon perforation were the only SAEs that led to permanent treatment discontinuation, occurring in 1 subject in the etrasimod group and 1 subject in the placebo group. Infections and hepatic enzyme increased were the only SAEs that were also AESIs. Ulcerative colitis and anemia were the only SAEs that occurred in more than 1 etrasimod-treated subject. There were no SAEs that occurred in more than 1 subject in the etrasimod group and that were not reported by at least 1 subject in the placebo group.
L986: SAEs of ulcerative colitis and anemia were considered unlikely to be related to etrasimod as these likely represent worsening of the underlying disease.
L987: ## Pertinent Narratives for SAEs
L988: The following SAEs occurred only in the etrasimod group and were considered to be possibly related to etrasimod by the review team: bacterial pneumonia, arthralgia, increased intracranial pressure, and hepatic enzyme increased.
L989: - **Bacterial pneumonia:** A 55-year-old White male had an SAE of "pneumonia bacterial" on Study Day 163. On Study Day 158, the subject reported fatigue and body aches, followed by a nonserious AE of pyrexia. The subject continued to have fever and developed headache, nausea, bloating, and abdominal pain through Study Day 163. On Study Day 163, the subject was hospitalized for bacterial pneumonia where a chest X-ray showed mild infiltration on the left, lower fields. The subject was treated with antibiotics and on Study Day 168, the SAE was considered recovered/resolved. Relevant medical history included UC (since 2011) and daily cigarette smoking (since 1985). The only concomitant medication at the onset of the SAE was sulfasalazine. Study treatment was unchanged. The Investigator assessed the event to be not related to study treatment. Given that S1P receptor modulators are known to affect lymphocyte trafficking, it is possible that etrasimod may increase the risk of infection, and the SAE of bacterial pneumonia may possibly be related to etrasimod. The risk of infections will be included in the Warnings and Precautions section of the label.
- **Arthralgia:** A 48-year-old White female had an SAE of "arthralgia" on Study Day 162. On Study Day 137, the subject was placed on bedrest and started on methylprednisolone for a nonserious AE of "UC-associated arthralgia" of the wrist, elbow, and knee. On Study Day 141, the subject complained of pain in the knee, hip, and wrist joints and was hospitalized for an SAE of "arthralgia." During the hospitalization, the subject did not receive additional treatment. Laboratory analyses showed mild anemia and elevated C-reactive protein (CRP). An ultrasound did not demonstrate venous thrombosis in the lower limbs. No action was taken with the study treatment. The subject's last dose of study treatment prior to the SAE of arthralgia was on Study Day 141. The SAE of arthralgia was resolved on Study Day 145. The review team agrees with investigator's assessment that the SAE of arthralgia was likely not related to study treatment and is likely an extraintestinal manifestation of UC. However, arthralgia was reported in a greater proportion of subjects who received etrasimod compared to placebo in APD334-301 (4% etrasimod vs. 2% placebo). While arthralgia is not a known S1P receptor class effect, it has been reported in subjects with UC who received another S1P receptor, ozanimod. Additionally, arthralgia has been reported as an adverse reaction of other IBD therapeutics. Therefore, arthralgia will be included in the Adverse Reactions section of the label.
- **Increased intracranial pressure:** A 63-year-old male had an SAE of "increased intracranial pressure" first reported on Study Day 90. Relevant medical history included ulcerative colitis (since 2019), anal fissure (since 2019), and anemia (since 2019). Concomitant therapy included mesalazine. The subject reported gradual loss of eyesight on Study Day 90 that started on Study Day 58. On Study Day 96, an optical coherence tomography (OCT) was performed, which did not show macular edema. An MRI was performed on Study Day 100,
L990: ## Velsipity (etrasimod)
L991: which was reported as showing no significant abnormalities. The subject was seen by a neurologist on Study Day 105. On Study Day 110, a spinal tap and fluid analysis was performed, and the neurologist diagnosed the subject with increased ICP of unknown origins. The study treatment was interrupted from Study Day 107 to Study Day 114. On Study Day 114, the subject reported that his eyesight had improved but was not fully recovered. On the same day, study treatment resumed. On Study Day 160, an OCT was performed and revealed atrophy of the optic nerve in the left eye. The subject's visual acuity in the left eye had not improved. On Study Day 236, an OCT was performed and showed similar results as on Study Day 160. The SAE of increased intracranial pressure was considered recovered/resolved on Study Day 243, with sequelae of ocular atrophy and partial vision loss.
L992: According to the Applicant, the subject's neurologist attributed the increased ICP as more likely an episode of what could be a chronic process of rise in ICP; however, S1P receptor modulators have been associated with ocular disorders (e.g., macular edema) and disorders of the central nervous system (e.g., PRES, PML). It is also possible the increased ICP was secondary to mesalazine use, in which increased ICP has been reported as an AE. Therefore, a relationship between etrasimod and the SAE of increased intracranial pressure cannot be ruled out.
L993: - **Migraine:** A 52-year-old White female had an SAE of migraine on Study Day 80 requiring hospitalization. The subject's relevant medical history included ulcerative colitis (since 1997), migraine (since 1981), and arthralgia (2019-2020). Concomitant medication ongoing at the onset of the SAE of migraine included rizatriptan, zolmitriptan, paracetamol, and estradiol. It was reported that the subject did not have a history of recurrent migraine episodes with the same symptoms or cluster headaches, and she had not experienced any usual migraine triggers. However, 2 weeks prior to the event, the subject reported recurrent headaches that per the Investigator, were not from the usual triggers that were consistent with her usual migraine headaches; they were much more severe.
L994: The subject's last dose of study treatment (etrasimod 2 mg) prior to the SAE was on Study Day 79, and study treatment was interrupted. A magnetic resonance imaging scan was normal. The SAE was considered resolved on Study Day 81.
L995: On Study Day 89, the subject experienced nonserious AEs of mild vomiting and mild migraine. On Study Day 93, after the SAE resolved, study treatment resumed. Upon interruption, the event abated, but then reappeared after study treatment was reintroduced. On Study Day 116, the subject stopped estradiol treatment that had been initiated on Study Day 71 as hormone replacement. Per the investigator, a gynecologist had advised to stop estradiol treatment due to its side effect profile, which includes migraines. The subject continued to experience mild and moderate AEs of migraine through Study Day 174. The Investigator assessed the event to be unlikely related to study treatment; however, the review team determined that a relationship between etrasimod and the SAE of migraine cannot be ruled out. Although the subject had a history of migraines and
L996: ## Velsipity (etrasimod)
L997: migraine is a potential side effect of the concomitant estradiol, migraine was reported in a greater proportion of subjects who received etrasimod (3/289, 1%) compared to placebo (none) during APD334-301. Therefore, headache, including related terms headache, migraine, tension headache, and sinus headache, will be included as an adverse reaction in the Adverse Reactions section of the label.
L998: - **Hepatic enzyme increased:** A 34-year-old female reported SAEs of “abdominal pain” and “elevated hepatic enzymes” first reported on Study Day 196, requiring hospitalization for abdominal pain. The subject’s relevant medical history included ulcerative colitis (since 2020). Concomitant medications ongoing at the onset of the SAEs included fluticasone/formoterol and levonorgestrel. Baseline alanine aminotransferase (ALT), aspartate aminotransferase (AST), bilirubin, alkaline phosphatase, and gamma-glutamyl transferase (GGT) were normal (Study Day 1). On Study Day 195, the subject reported “intense epigastric abdominal pain that was radiating to her back” and exacerbated by eating and drinking. The subject was hospitalized on Study Day 196 for “severe abdominal pain.” The same day, the subject was found to have elevated liver tests during the hospitalization. The subject’s ALT was 3.5 x ULN, AST was 7.9 x ULN, GGT was 1.9 x ULN, and bilirubin was 1.8 x ULN. The subject’s alkaline phosphatase was normal. An ultrasound did not show gallstones or gallbladder inflammation; however, a magnetic resonance cholangiopancreatography was performed which showed stenosis of the posterior right hepatic duct with distal dilation. According to the Investigator, the findings could represent early primary sclerosing cholangitis. The subject received symptomatic treatment for abdominal pain during the hospitalization. On Study Day 202, the subject’s abdominal pain had resolved, and she was discharged from the hospital. On the same day, the subject’s AST and bilirubin had returned to normal though her ALT and GGT remained elevated 2.8 x ULN and 2.2 x ULN, respectively. No action was taken with study treatment due to the events. On Study Day 224, the subject’s hepatic enzymes were normal, and the SAE of hepatic enzyme increased was considered recovered/resolved. The investigator assessed the events to be unlikely related to study treatment; however, liver injury has been reported in other S1P receptor modulators and is an AESI for etrasimod. The risk of liver injury will be communicated in the Warnings and Precautions section of the label.
L999: In summary, the causes of SAEs were varied and the overall frequency of SAEs and SAEs leading to discontinuation were similar between the etrasimod and placebo groups. However, several SAEs were considered likely related to etrasimod due to the mechanism of action of the drug and will be described in labeling.
L1000: ## 8.2.4.3. *Discontinuations Due to Adverse Events*
L1001: The overall incidence of AEs leading to discontinuation was similar in the etrasimod and placebo groups with 12/289 (4%) of subjects treated with etrasimod experiencing at least one AE that led to discontinuation, compared to 7/144 (5%) of subjects who received placebo. Ulcerative colitis and colon perforation were the only SAEs that led to permanent treatment discontinuation, occurring in 1 subject in the etrasimod group and 1 subject in the placebo
L1002: group (refer to Section 8.2.4.2). Overall, there was no consistent safety signal or specific AE or organ class of AEs leading to discontinuation. Subjects with adverse events leading to treatment discontinuation are shown in Table 53.

L1008: There were no AEs that led to discontinuation that were reported by more than one subject who received etrasimod, except for ulcerative colitis. However, ulcerative colitis was reported in a similar proportion of subjects in the etrasimod group as in the placebo group and is most likely related to the underlying disease and lack of response to treatment, rather than an adverse event. Narratives for AEs leading to discontinuation of etrasimod that were also AESIs are described below.
L1009: ## AES Leading to Discontinuation That Were Also AESIs
L1010: - **ALT elevated:** An 18-year-old White male with a 10-year history of primary sclerosing cholangitis (PSC) was discontinued from study treatment due to elevated ALT and AST. Concomitant medications included mesalazine and ursodeoxycholic acid. At the baseline visit, mild elevations of ALT and AST (1.7 x ULN and 1.4 x ULN, respectively) were noted. On Study Day 15, both ALT and AST increased to 3.1 x ULN but on Study Day 21, both had returned to baseline levels after the study drug was held from Study Days 18-23.
L1011: ## Velsipity (etrasimod)
L1012: On Study Day 45, ALT was 8.7 x ULN, and AST was 10.6 x ULN. No treatment was given for the events and study drug was discontinued on Study Day 46. Both ALT and AST decreased significantly on Study Day 50 (2.1 and 1.0 x ULN, respectively). Alkaline phosphatase was mildly elevated throughout the study from 1.1-2.1 x ULN and there was a mild to moderate elevation of GGT (1.8-4.0 x ULN). Bilirubin was consistently normal. On Study Day 65, the events of ALT increased and AST increased were considered recovered/resolved at which time ALT was 1.8 x ULN and AST was 1.2 x ULN (i.e., similar to baseline). The review team determined that the events may have been related to study treatment given the liver enzymes returned to baseline after study drug discontinuation; however, the liver enzyme elevations could have been due to the subject's past medical history of PSC.
L1013: Recommendations for evaluation of transaminases and bilirubin prior to initiation of treatment and during treatment are listed in the Warnings and Precautions section of the label.
L1014: - **Alkaline phosphatase elevated:** A 44-year-old White female had an alkaline phosphatase 1.0 - 1.2 x ULN beginning on Study Day 171. Mild increases were noted in ALT beginning on Study Day 115 (1.5 - 2.7 x ULN) and in AST beginning on Study Day 227 (1.3 - 1.4 x ULN). No transaminase increase reached 3 x ULN. GGT was elevated at screening (5.5 x ULN) and throughout the study (maximum of 21.8 x ULN on Study Day 280). The subject reported no symptoms. Relevant medical history included ulcerative colitis and obesity. Study drug was discontinued due to the event of alkaline phosphatase increased, with the last dose taken on Study Day 293. At the time of the subject's last available report, the events were considered not recovered/not resolved. The investigator assessed the events of GGT increased, ALT increased, and blood alkaline phosphatase increased to be probably related to study treatment. Given that the subject's baseline GGT was elevated, the liver test elevations may not be related to etrasimod; however, S1PR modulators have been associated with a risk of hepatic injury. Therefore, a relationship between the AE of "alkaline phosphatase elevated" and etrasimod cannot be ruled out. The risk of liver injury will be communicated in the Warnings and Precautions section of the label.
- **Bradycardia:** A 51-year-old White male had an AE of bradycardia and dizziness reported on Study Day 2. On Study Day 1, prior to dosing of study treatment, the subject's heart rate (HR) was 72 beats per minute (bpm). On Study Day 2, the subject reported the AEs of bradycardia and dizziness, and his heart rate was 44 bpm. Five hours later, the subject's heart rate was 42 bpm. The subject's blood pressure remained stable throughout and electrocardiograms (ECGs) were normal. No treatment was administered for these events. The subject discontinued study treatment due to the AE of bradycardia and received the last dose of study treatment on Study Day 2. On Study Day 3, the subject's heart rate was 46 and 47 bpm, and the severity of the dizziness had improved. On Study Day 4, the subject's heart rate was 58 bpm, and the bradycardia and dizziness were considered recovered/resolved. Bradycardia after the first dose of S1P receptor modulators is a known class effect; therefore, the AE of bradycardia and dizziness are likely related to etrasimod. The risk of bradyarrhythmia will be described in the Warning and Precaution section of the label.
L1015: - **AV block second degree:** A 48-year-old Black or African American female had AEs of bradycardia and first-degree AV block on Study Days 1 and 2. The subject's baseline ECG was normal. At 4 hours post-dose on Study Day 1, the ECG results showed a HR of 49 bpm, and a moderate AE of bradycardia was recorded. The ECG also showed a PR interval &gt; 200 msec (258 msec). The investigator and central ECG reader reported a second-degree AV block, Mobitz type I. No symptomatic AEs were reported, and no treatment was administered for either event. The subject discontinued study treatment due to the AE of "AV block second degree." The subject's last dose of study treatment was taken on Study Day 1. On Study Day 2, repeat ECG showed a normal PR interval (172 msec) and HR (72 bpm), the events of AV block second degree and bradycardia were considered recovered/resolved. The subject had no history of cardiac events and AV conduction delays are a known risk with S1P receptor modulators; therefore, the AE of "AV block second degree" is likely related to etrasimod. The risk of AV conduction delays will be described in the Warning and Precaution section of the label.
- **Macular edema:** A 43-year-old White female had an AE of macular edema on Study Day 75. The subject reported blurred vision in the left eye on Study Day 74. On Study Day 75, ophthalmic examination showed a decrease in visual acuity and macular edema. Treatment was discontinued on Study Day 75 due to the nonserious AE of macular edema. The subject's last dose of study treatment was taken on Study Day 74. Medical history included cerebral infarction and hypertension. Concomitant medications included mesalazine, methylprednisolone, lercanidipine, and perindopril. Macular edema and decreased visual acuity were ongoing at the time of early termination visit (Study Day 81). The AE of macular edema was considered recovered/resolved on Study Day 139, and a follow-up ophthalmologic exam on Study Day 173 was normal. Macular edema has been identified as a risk in other S1P receptor modulators and was reported more frequently in subjects who received etrasimod compared to placebo (18% vs. 8%). The risk of macular edema will be communicated in the Warnings and Precautions section of the label and a baseline evaluation of the fundus near the start of treatment and monitoring during treatment will be recommended. Refer to Section 8.2.6.5 for additional analyses regarding the risk of eye disorders.
L1016: ## 8.2.4.4. Adverse Events of Special Interest
L1017: At the time of protocol development, progressive multifocal leukoencephalopathy (PML) was identified as an AESI. Additional AESIs were identified by the Applicant as the understanding of the safety profile of etrasimod evolved. The Applicant conducted a retrospective, blinded medical review to identify AESIs based on the mechanism of action of etrasimod, prior experience with other S1PR modulators, and disease-specific potential risks. The following categories and preferred terms were used to query the clinical database, and a blinded medical review of these AEs was conducted to identify which events were AESIs. Refer to Section 8.2.6 for the review team's analysis of adverse events that are of special interest based on known class effects but did not meet the Applicant's criteria for AESI.
L1018: - CardiovascularBradycardia – if one or more of the following:HR was &lt;40 bpm (with or without symptoms)
- Event was associated with clinical symptoms (e.g., lightheadedness)
- Event led to subject withdrawal
- Atrioventricular (AV) conduction delay – if one or more of the following:AV block was second degree (Mobitz Type I or Type II) or higher
- PR interval was greater than 230 ms
- Event was associated with clinical symptoms (e.g., dizziness) considered potentially related based upon medical review
- Hypertension – if one or more of the following:Sustained systolic BP elevation was greater than 160 mmHg
- Sustained diastolic BP elevation was greater than 100 mmHg
- Medication was added for BP control
- Macular edema – defined as an increase in central foveal thickness (CFT) \ge 40 \mu\text{m}, and/or with associated symptoms or clinically significant abnormal findings
- Hepatic injuryLiver transaminases increased – defined as any liver transaminase elevation &gt;5 \times \text{ULN} or a sustained elevation &gt;3 \times \text{ULN}
- Bilirubin elevation – defined as bilirubin &gt;2 \times \text{ULN} without Gilbert's syndrome
- InfectionsSevere infections
- Opportunistic infections
- Herpes simplex and herpes zoster
- PulmonaryAirflow obstruction (\text{FEV}_1, FVC) – defined as a decrease from baseline of &gt;25\% or accompanied by relevant symptoms
- Decreased gas exchange (DLCO) – defined as a decrease from baseline of &gt;25\% or accompanied by relevant symptoms
- Posterior reversible encephalopathy syndrome (PRES)
- Malignancy
L1019: The AESIs as identified by the Applicant are shown in Table 54. Overall, there were more subjects with at least one AESI in the etrasimod group compared to placebo with 26/289 (9%) of subjects treated with etrasimod experiencing at least one AESI, compared to 10/144 (7%) of subjects who received placebo.

L1031: Cardiovascular adverse events, including hypertension, bradycardia, and AV conduction delay, macular edema, and herpes infections were reported more frequently in the etrasimod group compared to placebo. The overall incidence of liver injury AESIs were balanced between the two groups. No AESIs of PML, bilirubin elevation, PRES, or malignancy were reported. AESIs that were reported more frequently in subjects who received etrasimod compared to placebo are described in detail below.
L1032: - **Cardiovascular events:** Cardiovascular events were reported more frequently in subjects who received etrasimod compared to placebo. One AE of bradycardia and 1 AE of AV block second degree led to study discontinuation; refer to Section 8.2.4.3.**AV Block First Degree:** One subject experienced AV block first degree, 4 hours post-dose on Study Day 1. The subject had no history of cardiac disease. Predose on Study Day 1, ECG results showed normal T-wave morphology, sinus rhythm, and atrial premature complexes, which were considered abnormal and clinically significant by the investigator and the central reader. At 4 hours post-dose on Study Day 1, ECG results showed PR interval &gt;200 msec (238 msec, increased from baseline 152 msec), normal T-wave morphology, sinus rhythm, and atrial premature complexes considered abnormal and clinically significant by the investigator and the central reader, and an AESI of AV block first degree was
L1033: reported. No treatment was administered for the event and no action was taken with study treatment in response to the event. On Study Day 2, ECG results were normal, and the event was considered recovered/resolved. The Investigator assessed the event to be probably related to study treatment.
L1034: - **Hypertension**: Hypertension was reported as an adverse reaction in 3% of subjects treated with etrasimod. No subjects treated with placebo met the Applicant's criteria for hypertension. The majority of the events were reported by the Applicant as mild or moderate in severity. There was a small but persistent increase in systolic blood pressure (SBP) compared to baseline in subjects treated with etrasimod. However, the mean change from baseline to Week 52 was 2.2 mmHg in the etrasimod group compared to -1.3 mmHg in the placebo group, the clinical significance of which is unclear.
L1035: One subject reported an AE of "hypertensive crisis" on Day 218. The subject's vital signs before and after this episode were all normal. This event was confirmed by the Investigator as not being a "hypertensive crisis" event based on the objective blood pressure measurements and accepted diagnostic definitions. No action was taken with study treatment and the event resolved on the same day without intervention from the Investigator.
L1036: Due to the risk of cardiovascular events observed in the etrasimod development program and the known risks described in other S1P receptor modulators, a Warnings and Precautions section will be included in the label regarding the risk of bradyarrhythmia and AV conduction delays, as well as increased blood pressure.
L1037: - **Macular edema**: Macular edema was considered an AESI because it has been identified as a risk in other S1P receptor modulators. The Applicant reported one case of a nonserious AE of macular edema in a 43-year-old female treated with etrasimod during APD334-301, which led to study discontinuation. The AE of macular edema is described in detail in Section 8.2.4.3.
- **Infections (severe, opportunistic, or herpes infections)****Herpes simplex and herpes zoster**: Herpes viral infections were reported by more subjects in the etrasimod group compared to placebo (2% vs. 1%). Herpes simplex, including preferred terms herpes simplex and oral herpes, was reported by 4/289 (1%) subjects in the etrasimod group versus 1/144 (1%) in the placebo group. Herpes zoster was reported by 2/289 (1%) subjects in the etrasimod group versus none in the placebo group.
L1038: Of the subjects who experienced herpes zoster, one was a 50-year-old White female who on Study Day 395 had a nonserious AE of herpes zoster, followed by a nonserious AE of post-herpetic neuralgia on Study Day 407. The subject
L1039: received etrasimod 2 mg up to Study Day 407, the study termination visit. The Investigator assessed the event of herpes zoster to be unlikely related to study treatment. The other subject was a 50-year-old White female who received etrasimod 2 mg up to Study Day 371. On Study Day 229, the subject had an AESI of herpes zoster. No action was taken with study treatment in response to the event. On Study Day 240, the event was considered recovered/resolved. The Investigator assessed the event to be not related to study treatment.
L1040: S1P receptor modulators are known to affect lymphocyte trafficking; therefore, etrasimod may increase the risk of infections, including herpes simplex or herpes zoster. Consistent with other S1P receptor modulators, the risk of infections will be described in the Warnings and Precautions section of the label.
L1041: ## 8.2.4.5. *Treatment Emergent Adverse Events and Adverse Reactions*
L1042: An overview of treatment emergent adverse events (TEAEs) for APD334-301 is shown in Table 51. Overall, the proportion of subjects with at least one TEAE was greater in subjects who received etrasimod 206/289 (71%) compared to placebo 81/144 (56%). The majority of TEAEs were mild or moderate in severity. The proportion of subjects with a severe TEAE was similar between etrasimod 21/289 (7%) and placebo 11/144 (8%).
L1043: Table 55 summarizes the common TEAEs reported at a frequency of at least 2% of subjects in the etrasimod group and greater than placebo during APD334-301. The most common TEAEs were headache, elevated liver tests, dizziness, and arthralgia.

L1058: TEAEs that occurred in at least 2% of subjects treated with etrasimod and more frequently than placebo are described below.
L1059: - **Headache:** Headache was the most common TEAE reported by subjects during APD334-301. There was an imbalance in the proportion of subjects who received etrasimod that reported headache (9%) compared to placebo (5%). The majority of headache AEs were mild or moderate. One headache was an SAE (preferred term “migraine”) and is described in greater detail in Section 8.2.4.2. No AEs of headache led to study discontinuation. An imbalance was also observed in the pooled data from APD334-302 and APD334-003 (refer to Section 8.2.5.2). The mechanism by which etrasimod may increase the risk of headache is unknown; however, headache is an adverse reaction observed in several other S1P receptor modulators, including Zeposia (ozanimod)4, which is approved for the treatment of moderately to severely active UC. Therefore, headache will be included in the Adverse Reactions section of the label.
- **Elevated liver tests:** A greater proportion of subjects who received etrasimod had an AE of elevated liver tests (6%) compared to placebo (5%). The majority of elevated liver test AEs were mild or moderate in severity. One AE was considered an SAE and is described in detail in Section 8.2.4.2. Two subjects were discontinued from study treatment due to an AE of elevated liver tests (one subject with ALT increased to 8.7 x ULN and one subject with blood alkaline phosphatase increased to 1.2 x ULN); refer to Section 8.2.4.3 for full details of the AEs. An imbalance was also observed in the pooled data from APD334-302 and APD334-003 (refer to Section 8.2.5.2).
L1060: 4 See Zeposia (ozanimod) at https://www.accessdata.fda.gov/scripts/cder/daf/
L1061: Liver injury is described in labeling as a Warnings and Precautions for other S1P receptor modulators, including Zeposia (ozanimod), which is approved for the treatment of moderately to severely UC. Although the mechanism by which etrasimod might cause liver injury is not known, a similar Warnings and Precautions will be included in the etrasimod label. Refer to Section 8.2.6.4 for additional analyses that support the potential of hepatotoxicity with etrasimod.
L1062: - **Dizziness:** A greater proportion of subjects who received etrasimod had an AE of dizziness (5%) compared to placebo (2%). All the AEs of dizziness were mild or moderate in severity. No AEs were considered SAEs, and none led to study discontinuation. Approximately half (7/15) of the AEs of dizziness were reported within the first 2 days of treatment with etrasimod and may be related to the transient bradycardia observed with etrasimod initiation. Dizziness has also been observed in trials of other S1P receptor modulators. Taken together, the review team recommends including dizziness in the Adverse Reactions section of the label.
- **Arthralgia:** A greater proportion of subjects who received etrasimod had an AE of arthralgia (4%) compared to placebo (2%). All the arthralgia AEs were mild or moderate in severity. One AE of arthralgia was considered an SAE and is described in detail in Section 8.2.4.2. None of the arthralgia AEs led to study discontinuation. Arthralgia can be an extra-intestinal manifestation of inflammatory bowel disease, including UC; however, arthralgia has been reported as an adverse reaction in other S1P receptor modulators, including Zeposia (ozanimod), which is approved for the treatment of moderately to severely active UC. Arthralgia will be included in the Adverse Reactions section of the label.
- **Hypertension:** A greater proportion of subjects who received etrasimod had an AE of hypertension (3%) compared to placebo (1%). Most hypertension AEs were reviewed in Section 8.2.4.4 as hypertension with a sustained systolic BP elevation greater than 160 mmHg was considered an AESI. Two additional AEs of hypertension (1 preferred term "blood pressure increased" in the etrasimod group and 1 preferred term "hypertension" in the placebo group) were identified as TEAEs but not considered AESIs by the Applicant. Both AEs were either mild or moderate and did not result in study discontinuation. Therefore, the two additional AEs of hypertension do not change the analysis of the risk of hypertension due to etrasimod as described in the previous section. A Warnings and Precautions section will be included in the label regarding the risk of increased blood pressure, and hypertension will be included in the Adverse Reactions section of the label.
- **Urinary Tract Infection:** A greater proportion of subjects who received etrasimod had an AE of urinary tract infection (UTI) compared to placebo (3% and 2%, respectively). No events of UTI were SAEs or led to study discontinuation or interruption. UTI was reported as a frequent adverse reaction in subjects with multiple sclerosis who received
L1063: S1P receptor modulators Zeposia (ozanimod)4 and Ponvory (ponesimod)5. Given that etrasimod may increase the risk of infection, UTI will be included in the Adverse Reactions section of the label.

- **Nausea:** A greater proportion of subjects who received etrasimod had an AE of nausea (3%) compared to placebo (1%). Most nausea AEs were mild in severity, with only one considered moderate. None of the AEs were SAEs, led to study discontinuation, or a pause in study treatment. An imbalance was also observed in APD334-302 and APD334-003 as described in Section 8.2.5.5. Nausea has also been reported as an adverse reaction in other S1PR modulators, including Zeposia (ozanimod), which is approved for the treatment of moderately to severely active UC. Taken together, nausea will be included in the Adverse Reactions section of the label.
- **Hypercholesterolemia:** A greater proportion of subjects who received etrasimod had an AE of hypercholesterolemia (3%) compared to placebo (0%). Most AEs were mild, only one AE was considered moderate. All subjects with hypercholesterolemia AEs had cholesterol levels that remained &lt; 2 \times ULN. Hypercholesterolemia has been reported as an adverse reaction in another S1P receptor modulator, Ponvory (ponesimod). Although the clinical significance of the degree of hypercholesterolemia is uncertain, the review team considered it important to inform patients and prescribers of this potential risk. Therefore, hypercholesterolemia will be included in the Adverse Reactions section of the label.
- **Herpes viral infection:** The AEs of herpes viral infection were considered AESI and are described in detail in Section 8.2.4.4.
- **Blood creatine phosphokinase increased:** A greater proportion of subjects who received etrasimod had an AE of increased blood creatine phosphokinase (CPK) (2%) compared to placebo (1%). All the AEs were mild or moderate, none of the AEs were SAEs, and none of the AEs led to study discontinuation. Elevation of CPK resolved in all subjects despite continued treatment with etrasimod. There were no other AEs related to rhabdomyolysis (e.g., rhabdomyolysis, myoglobinuria, chromaturia) reported in these subjects. CPK elevation has not been reported as an adverse reaction in other S1P receptor modulators. Therefore, the increased CPK is not likely to be related to etrasimod. The review team does not recommend its inclusion in labeling.
- **Muscle spasms and vomiting:** A greater proportion of subjects who received etrasimod had an AE of muscle spasms (2%) and vomiting (2%) compared to placebo (none for both). All of the AEs were mild or moderate, none of the AEs were SAEs, and none of the AEs led to study discontinuation. One AE of vomiting led to study drug interruption for 1 day. Only 1 subject who reported an AE of vomiting also reported an AE of nausea on
L1064: 5 See Ponvory (ponesimod) at https://www.accessdata.fda.gov/scripts/cder/daf/
L1065: the same day. Therefore, the AEs of muscle spasm and vomiting are likely not clinically significant, and the review team did not recommend inclusion in labeling.
L1066: ## 8.2.4.6. *Laboratory Findings, Vital Signs, and Electrocardiograms*
L1067: ## Laboratory Findings
L1068: Routine hematology and chemistry clinical laboratory testing were performed during Screening, at Baseline, and throughout the study from Weeks 2 to 52, and at 2- and 4-week follow-up visits (Weeks 54 and 56), early termination visit, and/or end of treatment visit according to the Schedule of Activities.
L1069: No clinically meaningful differences between etrasimod and placebo were observed in hematology and chemistry parameters except for lymphopenia. However, lymphopenia was anticipated based on the mechanism of action of the drug. Additional safety analyses for lymphopenia are described in detail in Section 8.2.6.2.
L1070: ## Vital Signs
L1071: Due to the anticipated effects of etrasimod on bradyarrhythmia and atrioventricular conduction delays, first-dose cardiac monitoring was implemented during APD334-301. Subjects were monitored in-clinic for at least 4 hours with the following monitoring:
L1072: - Baseline vital signs and 12-lead ECG at pre-randomization
- Subjects remained under observation at the site for at least 4 hours after the first dose of study treatment.
- At Hours 1, 2, and 3 post-dose, the HR and systolic/diastolic BP were assessed. If the subject had a HR &lt;50 bpm or if cardiovascular symptoms developed, then the subject would remain closely monitored and a 12-lead ECG would be performed.
- At Hour 4, a discharge assessment was performed including heart rate, systolic/diastolic BP, and a 12-lead ECG. Subjects could be discharged from the clinic if they met the discharge criteria (Table 56).
- If subjects did not meet the discharge criteria at Hour 4, extended cardiac monitoring was performed including:Hourly vital signs and 12-lead ECG until the subject meets the discharge criteria.
- Any subject who required extended monitoring on Day 1 must return on Day 2 for the second dose and were re-monitored as on Day 1.
L1073: **Table 56. Discharge Criteria After Cardiac Monitoring**
L1074: Subjects will be released from the clinical site after dosing on Day 1 (but no sooner than 4 hours post-dose) when they fulfill the following discharge criteria:
• Heart rate \ge 50 bpm or no more than 10 bpm lower than the pre-dose (baseline) value
• No evidence of second-degree AV block or higher
• No cardiac symptoms (eg, chest pain, dizziness, palpitations, lightheadedness, shortness of breath, or syncope)
L1075: Source: Clinical Study Report, Phase 3, 52-Week Study in UC (ELEVATE UC 52), 16.1.1 Protocol and Protocol Amendments, Table 4
L1076: APD334-301 included discontinuation criteria related to post-dose cardiac monitoring, as described in Table 57.
L1077: **Table 57. Discontinuation of Study Treatment-Related to Post-Dose Cardiac Monitoring**
L1078: Reasons for Study Treatment Discontinuation Related to Post-Dose Cardiac Monitoringa
• Subjects who have a cardiovascular treatment-related symptomatic event (eg, chest pain, dizziness, palpitations, lightheadedness, shortness of breath, or syncope) associated with reduction of the heart rate or associated with clinically relevant 12-lead ECG changes at any time during the 4-hour monitoring period on Day 1 or Day 2 (as applicable).
• Subjects who have not met the discharge criteria on Day 1 after \ge 4 hours of extended monitoring, or Day 2 by 4 hours post-dose.
L1079: Source: Clinical Study Report, Phase 3, 52-Week Study in UC (ELEVATE UC 52), 16.1.1 Protocol and Protocol Amendments, Table 4
L1080: Additionally, subjects underwent the same first-dose cardiac monitoring procedures as was performed for the original treatment initiation if study treatment dosing was interrupted for \ge 2 consecutive days within the first week of treatment or \ge 7 days after the first week of treatment.
L1081: Bradycardia was reported by a greater proportion of subjects who were treated with etrasimod compared to placebo. The impact of etrasimod on bradyarrhythmia is discussed in detail in Section 8.2.6.2. Similarly, hypertension was an AESI and observed in a greater proportion of subjects treated with etrasimod compared to placebo. Refer to Section 8.2.4.4 for additional details.
L1082: No other clinically meaningful differences between etrasimod and placebo were observed in other routine vital signs during APD334-301.
L1083: ## Electrocardiograms (ECGs)
L1084: During APD334-301, 12-lead ECGs were obtained during the first-dose cardiac monitoring as described previously. Additionally, 12-lead ECGs were obtained at Week 12, Week 52, and during unscheduled visits. Bradyarrhythmia and AV conduction delays are described in Section
L1085: 8.2.4.4 and Section 8.2.6.2. Overall, the number of abnormal and clinically significant ECGs (e.g., bradycardia, first-degree AV block, second-degree AV block, completed right bundle branch block, left bundle branch block, sinus tachycardia, atrial fibrillation) was balanced between etrasimod (13%) and placebo (14%).
L1086: ## QT
L1087: During APD334-301, no clinically significant trends for QT prolongation were observed. The QT prolongation potential of etrasimod was assessed in a thorough QT (TQT) study. The results from the TQT study indicated that etrasimod was not associated with significant QTc prolonging effect. Refer to the QT-IRT Review by Eliford Kitabi dated March 3, 2023 (DARRTS ID: 5135842).

L1098: There were no deaths during trials APD334-302 or APD334-003.
L1099: ## 8.2.5.2. Serious Adverse Events

L1101: compared to 6/288 (2%) of subjects treated with etrasimod. Ulcerative colitis was the only SAE experienced by subjects in both groups and the incidence was similar between the groups.

L1109: Two subjects that received etrasimod experienced SAEs that led to study discontinuation, both due to ulcerative colitis. SAEs of ulcerative colitis were considered unlikely to be related to etrasimod as these represent worsening of the underlying disease. Pertinent narratives for SAEs are described below.
L1110: ## Pertinent Narratives for SAEs
L1111: - **Coronary artery disease:** During APD334-302, a 62-year-old female was hospitalized for an SAE of coronary artery disease on Study Day 15. Relevant medical history included dyslipidemia (since 1975), obesity (since 1980), sleeve gastrectomy (2020), ulcerative colitis (since 2017), and hypertension (since 2021). Concomitant medications ongoing at the onset of the event of coronary artery disease included paracetamol and tramadol. The subject's last dose of study treatment prior to the onset of the SAE was on Study Day 14. On Study Day 15, the subject reported symptoms of dyspnea on exertion for last few months. The subject underwent coronary angioplasty on Study Day 15. Study treatment dosing was not changed due to the event. The event was considered recovered/resolved on Study Day 16 and the subject was discharged from the hospital. The review team considered the SAE as unlikely to be related to etrasimod given the
L1112: subject's past medical history and lack of other coronary artery disease events reported in the etrasimod development program.
L1113: - **Hepatobiliary procedural complication:** During APD334-302, a 29-year-old White male underwent an endoscopic retrograde cholangiopancreatography (ERCP) with stent placement on Study Day 9 for the nonserious adverse event of primary sclerosing cholangitis, diagnosed on Study Day -3 by magnetic resonance cholangiopancreatography. Approximately 1.5 hours post ERCP procedure, the subject reported abdominal pain (5/10 on the pain scale) and was admitted to the hospital for pain control. The subject was diagnosed with post-ERCP pancreatitis, although no laboratory values of pancreatic enzymes were provided by the Applicant. No action was taken with study treatment due to this event. On Study Day 10, the SAE was considered recovered/resolved and the subject was discharged. The subject's last dose of study treatment prior to the SAE was on Study Day 8. Relevant medical history included iron deficiency (date not available), ulcerative colitis (since 2008), and osteopenia (since 2017). Concomitant medications ongoing at the onset of the event of hepatobiliary procedural complication included mesalazine. The review team considered the SAE as unlikely to be related to etrasimod, as post-ERCP pancreatitis is a well-described potential complication of ERCP. Further, the ERCP was performed due to PSC, which was diagnosed prior to initiating study treatment.
- **Migraine:** During APD334-302, a 28-year-old White female with a history of migraine and intermittent headache reported an SAE of migraine leading to hospitalization and study treatment interruption. The event was first reported on Study Day 9 when the subject presented to the emergency department with frontal headache and associated monocular visual blurring, vomiting, a tingling sensation in her arm, and dysphasia. Computed tomography (CT) of the head and neck and magnetic resonance imaging (MRI) of the brain were normal. The last dose of study drug prior to the SAE onset was on Study Day 8. Concomitant medications at the onset of the event included drospirenone/ethinylestradiol betadex clathrate, mesalazine, and paracetamol. On Study Day 10, the SAE of severe migraine was considered recovered/resolved and study treatment was resumed on Study Day 11. The Investigator assessed the event as unlikely to be related to study treatment; however, the review team determined that a relationship between etrasimod and the SAE of migraine cannot be ruled out. Although the subject had a past medical history of migraine, an imbalance in headache AEs (including migraine) was observed for APD334-301 (refer to Section 8.2.4.5) and APD334-302 and APD334-003 (refer to Section 8.2.5.5). Therefore, headache, including related terms headache, migraine, tension headache, and sinus headache, will be included as an adverse reaction in the Adverse Reactions section of the label.
L1114: ## **8.2.5.3. Discontinuations Due to Adverse Events**

Subjects with AEs leading to treatment discontinuation are shown in Table 60.

L1120: Abbreviations: N, number of subjects in treatment arm; n, number of subjects with at least one event; AE, adverse event
L1121: The most common AE that led to study discontinuation was ulcerative colitis. Two of the AEs of ulcerative colitis were SAEs. However, AEs of ulcerative colitis are most likely related to the underlying disease and lack of response to treatment, rather than an adverse event. Four subjects discontinued from the study due to AEs that were considered AESIs, one AE of liver function test abnormal, one AV block first degree, and three AEs of bradycardia. These AEs are described in detail below.
L1122: - **Liver function test abnormal:** During APD334-302, a 36-year-old Asian female experienced both AST and ALT elevations to 3.1 x ULN and 5.8 x ULN, respectively, on Study Day 15. On Study Day 19, the subject discontinued study treatment due to the AE of liver function test abnormal. The AST remained elevated to \ge 4 \times ULN and ALT to &gt; 6 \times ULN through Study Day 28, after which both returned to normal on Study Day 36 and remained there through the final visit on Study Day 50. No elevation in bilirubin was observed. Azathioprine had been discontinued 14 days before randomization, and concomitant medications included propranolol for migraine and oral contraceptive. No
L1123: treatment was administered for the event. Given S1P receptor modulators have been associated with liver enzyme elevations and TEAEs of elevated liver tests were observed in the etrasimod development program, the review team considered this AE as likely related to study treatment. Elevated liver tests were a common adverse reaction during APD334-302 and APD334-003 and will be included in the Adverse Reactions section of the label. Additionally, a Warnings and Precautions regarding liver injury will also be included in the label.
L1124: - **Bradycardia and AV Block First-degree:** The following AEs related to bradyarrhythmia and AV conduction delays led to study discontinuation. All AEs occurred during APD334-302.**Bradycardia:** 3 subjects treated with etrasimod discontinued the study due to AESIs of bradycardia.The first subject was a 73-year-old White female with a longstanding history of hypertension. Heart rates on Study Day -28 and predose on Study Day 1 were 61 bpm and 66 bpm, respectively. Baseline ECG on Study Day -28 was interpreted as normal. On Study Day 1, the subject had a moderate AE of "sinus bradycardia" (HR of 49 bpm) and discontinued study treatment on the same day. No treatment was given for the event. There were no associated symptomatic adverse events reported and no changes to the subject's blood pressure. The subject's only dose of study treatment was taken on Study Day 1. The subject was monitored on Study Day 2 (after discontinuing treatment) where the HR was 48-53 bpm. On Study Day 3, the event was considered recovered/resolved with HR of 56 bpm. The event was assessed to be related to study treatment.
- The second subject was a 43-year-old White female who experienced a mild AE of "sinus bradycardia" 4 hours after receiving etrasimod (HR of 45 bpm) on Study Day 1. A repeat ECG showed a HR of 51 bpm on Study Day 1, and the event was considered recovered/resolved. The subject had repeat vital signs and ECG performed at the Early Termination visit on Study Day 6, which showed a HR of 60 bpm.
- The third subject was a 23-year-old Black male who experienced a mild AE of bradycardia after receiving etrasimod with a pre-dose HR of 60 bpm and a 4-hour post-dose HR of 45 bpm on Study Day 1. Two additional hours of monitoring on Day 1 had similar heart rates of 49 bpm and 50 bpm, again without symptoms. On Study Day 1, the event was considered recovered/resolved. No action was taken with study drug at that time. On Study Day 2, the subject had a mild AE of bradycardia with a pre-dose HR of 51 bpm and a 1-hour post-dose HR of 45 bpm. The subject also had nonserious mild AEs of dizziness and palpitations on Study Day 2. The AEs
L1125: of bradycardia, dizziness, and palpitations resolved the same day (Study Day 2). The last dose of study drug was taken on Study Day 2. No treatment was given for the event. At the Early Termination visit on Study Day 17, the ECG recorded a HR of 63 bpm.
L1126: - **AV block first-degree:** A 35-year-old White male with a history of hypertension was diagnosed with a moderate AE of AV block first degree with ECG results that showed AV first degree, ventricular hypertrophy, normal T-wave morphology, sinus rhythm, and a heart rate of 58 bpm at pre-dose on Study Day 1. At 4 hours post-dose on Study Day 1, the atrioventricular block first degree was again noted. On Study Day 14, the ECG was reported as abnormal with first-degree AV block. The subject discontinued study treatment due to the first-degree AV block (which had not changed from pre-dose). No treatment was administered for either event. Given the AV block was identified prior to initiating treatment with etrasimod, the AE is unlikely to be related to etrasimod.

- **ECG T wave abnormal:** During APD334-003, a 48-year-old white female experienced a non-specific T wave abnormality on ECG during the screening period. On Study Day 1, a non-specific T wave abnormality was documented 2 hours post-dose, with the consideration of ischemia. The subsequent ECG results remained unchanged throughout the rest of the day. However, the last ECG at 8 hours post-dose was considered clinically significant by the investigator and was reported as an adverse event of an ECG T wave abnormal (reported term: worsening of T wave changes). There were no symptoms recorded by the Investigator and the patient's blood pressure (113/68 mmHg) was stable. No treatment was reported for the event. The study drug was interrupted because of the abnormal ECG T wave. The subject was subsequently evaluated with a stress test, echocardiogram, cardiologist evaluation, and cardiac CT calcium score obtained between Day 15 and Day 27, which were all normal. Prior medical history included cholelithiasis, dyspepsia, gastritis, arthralgia, headache, and chronic tobacco and caffeine intake. The subject did not take any medications for UC at the time of screening. Prior medication included oral 5-aminosalicylates. The study drug was re-introduced on Day 36 with a pre-dose ECG finding of nonspecific T wave abnormality that remained constant throughout the day. At the time of the event, the patient was not taking any concomitant medications. The study drug was permanently discontinued because of the adverse event of ECG T wave abnormal. On Study Day 382, the event of ECG T wave abnormal was considered recovered/resolved. Given that the ECG T wave abnormality was identified prior to initiating treatment with etrasimod, it is unlikely that the AE was related to the study drug.
L1127: Due to the risk of cardiovascular events as observed in the etrasimod development program and the known risks described in other S1PR modulators, a Warnings and Precautions will be
L1128: included in the label regarding the risk of bradyarrhythmia and atrioventricular conduction delays, as well as increased blood pressure.
L1129: - **Neurological symptom:** During APD334-003, A 59-year-old White female with a history of pancolitis experienced an AE of “neurological symptom” described as multiple small-focal neurological symptoms by the Investigator on Study Day 85. At the time of the event, the patient was also experiencing nonserious AEs of hypertension and urinary tract infection. The patient’s last dose of study drug was taken on Study Day 84. On Study Day 125, the event of neurological symptom was considered recovered/resolved. Medical history also included affect lability, hypertension, hypercholesterolemia, chronic gastritis, duodenal papillitis, arthritis, goiter, and chorioretinal atrophy. Concomitant medications included mesalazine, alimemazine tartrate, valsartan, diltiazem, indapamide, algeldrate/magnesium hydroxide, pancreatin, levothyroxine, and ciprofloxacin. No treatment was reported for the event. The Investigator assessed the event of neurological symptom to be related to study drug. A follow up report was solicited by the Applicant and received approximately 3 years and 7 months after study completion. At that time, the subject reported ongoing “transient symptoms” (slight staggering when walking, loss of sensitivity in fingers and toes). After study completion, she was never evaluated by a neurologist and had no imaging studies. Hypertension has been controlled since medications were adjusted around the time of study completion. The review team considered that the neurological symptoms may be related to the subject’s past medical history and other concomitant medications; however, details are lacking regarding the subject’s symptoms due to unavailability of additional information. Therefore, a relationship between etrasimod and the AE of neurological symptom cannot be determined.
L1130: ## 8.2.5.4. *Adverse Events of Special Interest*
L1131: Similar to APD334-301, PML was identified as an AESI for APD334-302 and APD334-003 at the time of protocol development. APD334-302 and APD334-003 had a similar retrospective, blinded medical review to identify additional AESIs as described for APD334-301; refer to Section 8.2.4.4 for additional details. The review team performed additional analyses of adverse events that are of special interest based on known class effects but that did not meet the Applicant’s criteria for AESI; refer to Section 8.2.6.
L1132: The AESIs as identified by the Applicant for APD334-302 and APD334-302 are shown in Table 61. Overall, there were more subjects with at least one AESI in the etrasimod group compared to placebo with 15/288 (5%) of subjects treated with etrasimod experiencing at least one AESI, compared to 5/170 (3%) of subjects treated with placebo.

L1147: Cardiovascular AESIs, including hypotension, bradycardia, and AV conduction delay, and liver injury AESIs were reported more frequently in the etrasimod group compared to placebo. No AESIs of PML, severe infection, macular edema, pulmonary disorders, PRES, or malignancy were reported. Refer to Section 8.2.6 for additional analyses of AEs of special interest that did not meet the Applicant's criteria for AESI. AESIs that were reported more frequently in subjects who received etrasimod compared to placebo are described in detail below.
L1148: - **Cardiovascular events:** All AESIs of bradycardia and AV conduction delay occurred on Day 1 or Day 2. No cardiovascular events were SAEs. Cardiovascular events led to discontinuation of treatment in 4 subjects all of whom received etrasimod in APD334-302; 3 subjects with bradycardia and 1 subject with AV conduction delay. These AEs that led to discontinuation are described in detail in Section 8.2.5.3.
L1149: Due to the risk of cardiovascular events, specifically bradyarrhythmia, AV conduction delay, and hypertension, observed in the etrasimod development program and the known risks described in other S1P receptor modulators, a Warnings and Precautions will be described in the label regarding these risks.
L1150: - **Liver injury:** The overall incidence of liver injury AESIs was low at 1% in the etrasimod group; however, there were no subjects with AESIs of liver injury in the placebo group. No liver injury AESIs were SAEs and none of the AESIs were considered severe in severity. Additionally, there were no cases that raised concern for drug-induced liver injury, including no Hy's Law cases. One AESI of liver transaminase elevation (PT "liver function test abnormal") in the etrasimod group of APD334-302 led to treatment discontinuation and is described in detail in Section 8.2.5.3. A Warnings and Precautions regarding liver injury will also be included in labeling, aligned with other S1P receptor modulators.
- **Opportunistic infection:** One subject who received etrasimod in APD334-302 reported an AESI of an opportunistic infection, which was cytomegalovirus (CMV) infection. The subject was a 68-year-old Asian male who had withdrawn from the study on Study Day 20 due to "withdrawal by subject or parent/guardian." On Study Day 36, the subject reported a worsening of bowel movement frequency, and a CMV stool antigen test was performed and was positive. An AESI of CMV infection was reported on the same day. At the time of the CMV AESI, the subject had been receiving rectal budesonide since Study Day 20 (day of study discontinuation) and had just started vedolizumab on Study Day 36 (the day of the AESI). Given CMV is a common infection observed in patients with UC and AESI was reported after the subject had discontinued etrasimod, the relationship between etrasimod and the AESI is uncertain.
L1151: ## 8.2.5.5. *Treatment Emergent Adverse Events and Adverse Reactions*
L1152: An overview of treatment emergent adverse events (TEAEs) for the pooled data for APD334-302 and APD334-003 is shown in Table 58. Overall, the proportion of subjects with at least one TEAE was similar in subjects who received etrasimod (140/288, 49%) compared to placebo (81/170, 47%). The majority of TEAEs were mild or moderate in severity. The number of subjects who reported a severe TEAE was similar between etrasimod (10/288, 4%) and placebo (8/170, 4%).
L1153: TEAEs that occurred in at least 2% of subjects during APD334-302 and APD334-003 and at a higher frequency than placebo are shown in Table 62. Headache and elevated liver tests were the most common TEAEs during APD334-302 and APD334-003, which was similar to APD334-301.

L1167: TEAEs that occurred in at least 2% of subjects treated with etrasimod and more frequently than placebo are described below.
L1168: - **Headache:** Headache was the most common TEAE reported by subjects during APD334-302 and APD334-003. A greater proportion of subjects who received etrasimod (6%) reported a TEAE of headache compared to placebo (4%). The majority of headache TEAEs were mild or moderate in severity. One subject reported a severe TEAE of headache, which was considered an SAE (preferred term “migraine”), which is described in detail in Section 8.2.5.2. No headache TEAEs led to study discontinuation. An imbalance was also observed in APD334-301 (refer to Section 8.2.5.5). The mechanism by which etrasimod may increase the risk of headache is unknown; however, headache is an adverse reaction observed in several other S1P receptor modulators, including Zeposia (ozanimod), which is approved for the treatment of moderately to severely active UC. Therefore, headache will be included in the Adverse Reactions section of the label.
- **Elevated liver tests:** A greater proportion of subjects who received etrasimod had a TEAE of elevated liver tests (5%) compared to placebo (&lt;1%). All of the elevated liver test TEAEs were mild or moderate in severity. None of the TEAEs were considered an
L1169: ## Velsipity (etrasimod)
L1170: SAE. One subject was discontinued from study treatment due to a TEAE of elevated liver tests (preferred term "liver function test abnormal"); refer to Section 8.2.5.3. An imbalance was also observed in APD334-301 (refer to Section 8.2.4.5). Liver injury is a Warnings and Precautions for other S1P receptor modulators, including Zeposia, which is approved for the treatment of moderately to severely active UC. Although the mechanism by which etrasimod might cause liver injury is not known, a similar Warnings and Precautions will be included in the etrasimod label. Refer to Section 8.2.6.4 for additional hepatotoxicity analyses.
L1171: - **Colitis ulcerative:** Although a greater proportion of subjects who received etrasimod had a TEAE of "colitis ulcerative" (i.e., ulcerative colitis) (4%) compared to placebo (3%), the difference was small. Three TEAEs of ulcerative colitis were SAEs. Two SAEs and 6 non-serious TEAEs of ulcerative colitis led to study discontinuation. The review team determined that TEAEs of ulcerative colitis are likely to be related to the underlying disease/lack of response to therapy and unlikely to represent a drug effect and does not recommend inclusion in the Adverse Reactions section of the label.
- **Nausea:** A greater proportion of subjects who received etrasimod had a TEAE of nausea (4%) compared to placebo (2%). The majority of the TEAEs of nausea were mild or moderate, one TEAE was severe. None of the TEAEs of nausea were considered SAEs, led to study discontinuation, or a pause in treatment. An imbalance was also observed in APD334-301 as described in Section 8.2.4.5. Nausea has also been reported as an adverse reaction in other S1P receptor modulators, including Zeposia, which is approved for the treatment of moderately to severely active UC. Taken together, nausea will be included in the Adverse Reaction sections of the label.
- **Pyrexia:** Although a greater proportion of subjects who received etrasimod had a TEAE of pyrexia (3%) compared to placebo (2%), the difference was small. The majority of TEAEs of pyrexia were associated with other TEAEs that may explain the pyrexia (e.g., recent COVID-19 vaccination, diarrhea, upper respiratory tract infection). The review team determined that pyrexia was unlikely to be related to etrasimod and does not recommend inclusion in the Adverse Reactions section of the label.
- **Urinary tract infection:** A greater proportion of subjects who received etrasimod had a TEAE of urinary tract infection (3%) compared to placebo (0%). All of the urinary tract infection TEAEs were mild or moderate. None of the TEAEs were considered SAEs or led to study discontinuation. The mechanism of action of etrasimod (i.e., inhibition of lymphocyte trafficking) has been associated with an increased risk of infection. Aligned with other S1P receptor modulators, the label for etrasimod will include a Warnings and Precautions regarding infection. Additionally, urinary tract infections will be included in the Adverse Reactions section of the label.
- **Bradycardia:** A greater proportion of subjects who received etrasimod had a TEAE of bradycardia (3%) compared to placebo (0%). Four TEAEs of bradycardia were considered
L1172: AESIs and are described in Section 8.2.5.4. An additional four TEAEs of bradycardia were not reported as AESIs because they did not meet the Applicant's criteria. Three subjects discontinued the study due to TEAEs of bradycardia, as described in Section 8.2.5.3. The mechanism of action of etrasimod has been associated with bradyarrhythmia and a Warnings and Precautions regarding bradyarrhythmia will be included in labeling. Additionally, bradycardia will be described in the Adverse Reactions section of the label.
L1173: - **Blood creatine phosphokinase increased:** A greater proportion of subjects who received etrasimod had an AE of blood creatine phosphokinase (CPK) increased (2%) compared to placebo (1%). All of the AEs were mild or moderate, none of the AEs were SAEs, and none of the AEs led to study discontinuation. All the subjects had normal CPK following the AE of increased CPK. There were no other AEs related to rhabdomyolysis (e.g., rhabdomyolysis, myoglobinuria, chromaturia) reported in these subjects. Therefore, the increased CPK is not likely to be related to etrasimod. The review team did not recommend inclusion in labeling.
- **Vomiting, abdominal distension, and diarrhea:** Vomiting, abdominal distension, and diarrhea were each reported by more subjects who received etrasimod compared to placebo. However, the majority of TEAEs of vomiting, abdominal distension, and diarrhea were reported in association with additional TEAEs (e.g., pyrexia, abdominal pain, ulcerative colitis, gastroenteritis) that may explain the reported TEAEs. The review team determined that vomiting, abdominal distension, and diarrhea were unlikely to be related to etrasimod and does not recommend inclusion in the Adverse Reactions section of the label.
L1174: ## 8.2.5.6. *Laboratory Findings, Vital Signs, and Electrocardiograms*
L1175: ## Laboratory Findings
L1176: Routine hematology and chemistry clinical laboratory testing were performed during Screening, at Baseline, and throughout the study from Weeks 2 to 12, and at 2-week follow-up visits (Week 14), early termination visit, and/or end of treatment visit according to the Schedule of Activities.
L1177: No clinically meaningful differences between etrasimod and placebo were observed in hematology and chemistry parameters except for lymphopenia. However, lymphopenia was anticipated based on the mechanism of action of the drug. Additional safety analyses for lymphopenia are described in detail in Section 8.2.6.2.
L1178: ## Vital Signs
L1179: First-dose cardiac monitoring was performed during APD334-302, similar to APD334-301. Refer to Section 8.2.4.6, Vital Signs for further details. During APD334-003, subjects had 24-hour Holter monitoring. Bradycardia was reported by a greater proportion of subjects who were treated with etrasimod compared to placebo, as described previously in Section 8.2.5.4 and
L1180: Section 8.2.5.5. The impact of etrasimod on bradyarrhythmia is also discussed in detail in Section 8.2.6.3.
L1181: No other clinically meaningful differences between etrasimod and placebo were observed in other routine vital signs during APD334-302 and APD334-003.
L1182: ## Electrocardiograms (ECGs)
L1183: During APD334-302, 12-lead ECGs were obtained during the first-dose cardiac monitoring as described previously. Additionally, 12-lead ECGs were obtained at Week 12 and during unscheduled visits. During APD334-003, Holter monitoring was performed during the first 24-hours of treatment and 12-lead ECGs were obtained at screening, Weeks 0, 1, 2, 4, 8, and 12 and during unscheduled visits. Bradyarrhythmia and AV conduction delays are described in Section 8.2.5.4 and Section 8.2.6.2. Overall, the number of abnormal and clinically significant ECGs (e.g., bradycardia, first-degree AV block, second-degree AV block, complete right bundle branch block, left bundle branch block, sinus tachycardia, atrial fibrillation) was balanced between etrasimod (8%) and placebo (11%) for APD334-302 and no clinically meaningful changes in ECG parameters were reported for APD334-003.
L1184: ## QT
L1185: During APD334-302 and APD334-003, no clinically significant trends for QT prolongation were observed. The QT prolongation potential of etrasimod was assessed in a thorough QT (TQT) study. The results from the TQT study indicated that etrasimod was not associated with significant QTc prolonging effect. Refer to the QT-IRT Review (DARRTS ID: 5135842) by Eliford Kitabi dated March 3, 2023.
L1186: ## 8.2.6. Analysis of Submission-Specific Safety Issues
L1187: ## 8.2.6.1. Infections
L1188: S1P receptor modulators have been associated with an increased risk of infection due to the mechanism of action of the drug, which causes a reduction in peripheral blood lymphocytes. The Applicant evaluated the risk of severe infections, opportunistic infections, and herpes simplex and herpes zoster infections; refer to Section 8.2.4.4 and Section 8.2.5.4 for details of these analyses. To further characterize the potential risk of infection, the review team conducted additional safety analyses.

L1196: In summary, the overall incidence of infections was slightly higher in the etrasimod treatment group than placebo; however, most infections were mild or moderate in severity, were not serious, and did not lead to study discontinuation. However, based on the mechanism of action of the drug and the observed imbalance in infections, a Warnings and Precautions regarding infection will be included in labeling. Additionally, urinary tract infection and herpes viral infection will be described in the Adverse Reactions section of the label to inform patients and prescribers of the specific risk of these infections.
L1197: ## 8.2.6.2. Lymphopenia
L1198: Lymphopenia is a known S1P receptor modulator class effect due to the mechanism of action, which reduces lymphocyte egress from lymph nodes. Since etrasimod was expected to cause lymphopenia and may cause neutropenia, investigators were blinded to the complete blood count lab results in order to preserve the blind. An unblinded Medical Monitor assessed WBC differential lab results and alerted the Investigator if (1) ANC &lt;1000 cells/\muL and/or (2) ALC &lt;200 cells/\muL. As a result, the incidence of TEAEs of lymphopenia and neutropenia may not be an accurate reflection of lymphopenia and neutropenia during studies APD334-301, APD334-302, and APD334-003 because the TEAE threshold for the investigators was high.
L1199: An analysis of the incidence of lymphopenia and neutropenia based on laboratory data for APD334-301 and pooled data for APD334-302 and APD334-003 is shown in Table 64.
