L1207: As expected, lymphopenia was more common in the subjects who received etrasimod compared to placebo. An ALC of &lt;200 cells/\muL was observed in 16/289 (5.5%) and 2/288 (0.6%) subjects who received etrasimod during APD334-301 and APD334-302 and APD334-003, respectively versus none in placebo. Among subjects with an ALC of &lt;200 cells/\muL, rhinitis, COVID-19, and tinea each occurred once in 3 subjects, and upper respiratory tract infection occurred twice in a fourth subject; all were reported as mild in severity and no infection in a subject with ALC &lt;200 cells/\muL was serious or led to study discontinuation. As described in Section 16.3.2, the median time for peripheral blood lymphocytes to return to the normal range was 2.6 weeks, with approximately 90% of subjects in the normal range within 4.7 weeks.
L1208: Neutropenia was generally similar between subjects who received etrasimod compared to placebo, only 1 subject (0.3%) who received etrasimod had an ANC of &lt;500 cells/\muL versus none in placebo and this subject experienced no TEAEs of infection.
L1209: Although the overall incidence of infections was greater in the etrasimod group compared to placebo during APD334-301 and pooled data from APD334-302 and APD334-003, the difference between the two groups was small (25% etrasimod vs. 22% placebo in APD334-301, and 14% vs. 12% in pooled APD334-302 and APD334-003). Refer to Section 8.2.6.1 for further analysis of infection risk with etrasimod.
L1210: Based on the available data and consistent with the mechanism of action of the drug, lymphopenia, including severe lymphopenia (&lt;200 cells/\muL), was observed in subjects who received etrasimod. However, the overall rate of infection between etrasimod and placebo was only slightly higher in etrasimod compared to placebo. Additionally, the majority of infections, even for subjects with severe lymphopenia, were mild, not serious, and did not lead to study discontinuation. Therefore, to address the risk of lymphopenia due to etrasimod, the Warnings and Precautions section of the label will describe the reduction in peripheral blood lymphocyte count and the risk of infection in subjects in APD334-301 and pooled studies APD334-302 and
L1211: APD334-003. The label will include recommendations to obtain a baseline complete blood count, including lymphocyte count, and to consider interruption in treatment with etrasimod if a patient develops a serious infection. This information in the label is similar to other S1P receptor modulators.
L1212: ## **8.2.6.3. Bradyarrhythmia and Atrioventricular Conduction Delays**
L1213: Cardiovascular events including bradycardia, AV conduction delays, and hypertension are known class effects in S1P receptor modulators thought to be related to their effect on S1P receptors found on atrial myocytes and smooth muscle cells (i.e., S1P1, S1P2, and S1P3). Transient lowering of the heart rate and atrioventricular conduction delay were observed with the first dose of etrasimod and when therapy was reinitiated and occurred more frequently in subjects treated with etrasimod versus placebo. The largest heart rate reduction was observed 3 hours after the first etrasimod dose -7.2 (8.98) bpm compared to 0.4 (7.93) bpm in the placebo group. These effects were self-limited and resolved within 8 hours without the need for intervention. Refer to Section 6.3.1 for additional details regarding etrasimod's heart rate reduction effects.
L1214: The Applicant considered cardiovascular events as adverse events of special interest (AESIs) if the preferred term met specific criteria, which was reviewed by a blinded medical reviewer to determine whether the AE was considered an AESI. Refer to the description in Section 8.2.4.4 for details on the criteria used by the Applicant to define AESIs.6
L1215: The review team analyzed the available safety data to characterize the potential risk of bradyarrhythmia and AV conduction delay. These events were identified as those with preferred terms bradycardia, sinus bradycardia, heart rate decreased, atrioventricular block first degree, and atrioventricular block second degree. There was no requirement for a specific HR threshold or associated clinical symptoms, which differs from the Applicant's AESI analysis. The incidence of cardiovascular events identified by the review team during APD334-301 and APD334-302 and APD334-003 are shown in Table 65.
L1216: 6 The review team considered that the risk of hypertension was sufficiently characterized by the AESI and TEAE analyses performed for APD334-301 and pooled data from APD334-302 and APD334-003. Additional analyses in this section were not performed. Refer to the respective sections for additional details regarding hypertension.

L1225: The review team's broader criteria identified an increased number of subjects with bradycardia in the etrasimod groups of APD334-301 and pooled APD334-302 and APD334-003 compared to the number of subjects reported by the Applicant as AESIs. However, the overall trend between the review team's analysis and the Applicant's AESI analysis was similar.
L1226: - **Bradycardia:** During APD334-301, 4 subjects (1%) treated with etrasimod reported AEs of bradycardia, 3 of which occurred on Study Day 1. One subject (&lt;1%) treated with etrasimod reported bradycardia on Study Day 2. In the placebo group, no AEs of bradycardia were reported. During APD334-302 and APD334-003, 8 subjects (2.8%) treated with etrasimod reported AEs of bradycardia, which all occurred on Study Day 1. One subject (0.3%) treated with etrasimod reported bradycardia on Study Day 2 after having reported bradycardia on Study Day 1. In the placebo group, no AEs of bradycardia were reported.
L1227: As described previously, no bradycardia TEAEs were SAEs. A total of four bradycardia TEAEs led to study discontinuation (1 during APD334-301 and 3 during APD334-302/APD334-003), which are described in Sections 8.2.4.3 and 8.2.5.3.
L1228: - **AV Conduction Delay:** During APD334-301, 2 subjects (1%) in the etrasimod group had AV conduction delay (1 subject with first-degree AV block and 1 subject with second-degree AV block, Mobitz type I) compared to none in placebo. Both events occurred on Day 1. During APD334-302 and APD334-003, 2 subjects (1%) in the etrasimod group had AV conduction delay (1 subject with first-degree AV block and 1 subject with second-degree AV block, Mobitz type I) compared to none in placebo. Both events occurred on Day 1.
L1229: As described previously, no AV conduction delays were SAEs. Two subjects (1 from APD334-301 and 1 from APD334-302/APD334-003) were discontinued due to TEAEs of AV conduction delay (both first-degree AV blocks), which are described in Sections 8.2.4.3 and 8.2.5.3.
L1230: In summary, treatment with etrasimod resulted in a transient decrease in heart rate and an increased risk of AV conduction abnormalities (first-degree and second-degree [Mobitz Type I] AV block). Bradycardia after the first dose is a known class effect of S1P receptor modulators. Bradycardia and AV conduction delays were transient, mostly asymptomatic, and most episodes resolved within 8 hours. Therefore, the Warnings and Precautions section of the label will describe the risk of bradyarrhythmia and AV conduction delays, aligned with other currently approved S1P receptor modulators.
L1231: ## 8.2.6.4. *Hepatotoxicity*
L1232: Liver injury has been reported in S1P receptor modulators and a Warnings and Precautions is described in the labels of all approved S1P receptor modulators. The mechanism of liver injury has not been well described; however, liver injury may be caused by a toxic or immunogenic intermediate because of extensive metabolism in the liver. During the etrasimod development program, the Applicant defined liver injury as an AESI for TEAEs that met certain criteria. Overall, elevations in liver transaminases (AST and/or ALT) occurred more frequently in subjects who received etrasimod compared to placebo. These criteria and AESI analyses are described in detail in Section 8.2.4.4 and Section 8.2.5.4. The review team conducted additional analyses to evaluate for Hy's law cases and the risk of elevated liver tests not limited to the Applicant's definition of "hepatic injury."
L1233: As shown in Figure 14, there were no Hy's Law cases reported in the integrated summary of safety, which included data from the "All UC Pool," which included APD334-301, APD334-302, and APD334-003; the open-label extension studies APD334-303 and APD334-005; and open-label data from ES101002, APD334-301, APD334-302, APD334-003.

L1242: Analysis is subject level (i.e., each subject is counted once regardless of the number of reported events).
L1243: Proportions are adjusted for different randomization ratios of 302 and 003 by CMH weights.
L1244: Abbreviations: N, number of subjects in treatment arm; n, number of subjects with at least one event; ULN, upper limit of normal
L1245: In APD334-301, elevations in ALT &gt;3.0 x ULN, alkaline phosphatase &gt;2.0 x ULN, and GGT occurred at a higher frequency in the etrasimod group compared to placebo. Similarly, in pooled data from APD334-302 and APD334-003, elevations in ALT &gt;3.0 x ULN and GGT occurred at a higher frequency in the etrasimod group compared to placebo. One (&lt;1%) subject who received etrasimod reported an SAE of hepatic enzymes increased during APD334-301 (refer to Section 8.2.4.2). In APD334-301, the proportion of subjects that discontinued treatment due to liver enzyme elevations (i.e., ALT and alkaline phosphatase) was balanced between the etrasimod and placebo groups (refer to Section 8.2.4.3). In APD334-302 and APD334-003, there were no SAEs related to increased hepatic enzymes and 1 (&lt;1%) subject in the etrasimod group discontinued treatment due to elevated AST and ALT compared to none in the placebo group (refer to Section 8.2.5.3).
L1246: In summary, elevated liver tests was observed more frequently in the etrasimod group compared to placebo but there were no Hy's Law cases, and the majority were not serious or led to study discontinuation. Additionally, most AEs that were either serious or led to study discontinuation were considered unlikely to be related due to other underlying conditions (e.g., primary sclerosing cholangitis). However, elevations in liver enzymes and liver injury have been reported in S1P receptor modulators and will be included in etrasimod's Warnings and Precautions section, which will describe the rate of ALT &gt;3.0 x ULN, recommend testing liver enzymes and bilirubin levels before initiation of treatment and if concern for liver injury is present during treatment, and to discontinue etrasimod if liver injury is confirmed. Additionally, elevated liver tests, which includes elevated ALT, AST, GGT, alkaline phosphatase, bilirubin, transaminases, and liver function test, will be included in the Adverse Reactions section of the label.
L1247: ## 8.2.6.5. Ophthalmologic Findings
L1248: To characterize the potential risk of macular edema and other ophthalmologic findings, the review team analyzed the results of ophthalmologic examinations, including ophthalmoscopy, optical coherence tomography (OCT), visual acuity measurements, and slit lamp examinations. The analysis of the results of ophthalmologic evaluations were limited to APD334-301 because it was the only completed, 52-week, randomized comparison of etrasimod versus placebo.
L1249: Additionally, the assessment of ophthalmologic findings was limited due to missing ophthalmologic evaluations. Refer to the Ophthalmology Consult Review by Dr. Wiley Chambers for full details of the review (DARRTS ID: 5106241).
L1250: ## Macular Edema
L1251: Macular edema has been reported in other S1P receptor modulators and observed during the etrasimod development program. The mechanism by which S1P receptor modulators may cause macular edema is not well described but may be due to effects on endothelial cells. The Applicant defined macular edema as “an increase in CFT \ge 40 \mu\text{m} and/or with associated symptoms or clinically significant abnormal findings.” Based on this definition, 1 case of macular edema in a subject who received etrasimod during APD334-301 was reported, which led to study discontinuation. This AE of macular edema is described in detail in Section 8.2.4.4.
L1252: In subjects that had OCT macular thickness reported at baseline, 16/95 (17%) of subjects in the etrasimod group compared to 2/26 (8%) in the placebo group had increases of \ge 40 \mu\text{m}, which was the predefined OCT cut-off by the Applicant for macular edema. The review team did not consider a requirement for “associated symptoms” to be relevant because most cases of macular edema are asymptomatic. Furthermore, a decrease in visual acuity due to macular edema may not be noticed if it occurs in one eye.
L1253: On October 10, 2023, the Applicant submitted additional analyses of OCT findings, in which they claimed they verified the CFT data by reviewing the case report forms (CRFs) and OCT reports that were available for APD334-301. However, the Applicant did not submit the CRFs or OCT reports and several reports were missing; therefore, the review team could not verify the additional analyses. The analyses raised concerns about the integrity of the OCT data because nearly half (i.e., 41.7%) were identified by the Applicant to be inaccurate readings of CFT. Due to the data quality issues, the review team was unable to agree with the Applicant to report the rate of macular edema in the proposed label.
L1254: Given an imbalance was observed in the originally submitted OCT analyses and S1P receptor modulators are associated with macular edema, a Warnings and Precautions regarding macular edema will describe the association of etrasimod with an increased risk of macular edema and recommend that patients be evaluated near the start of therapy and periodically during treatment for macular edema. Additionally, consideration to discontinue etrasimod if macular edema develops will be included.
L1255: ## Decrease in Visual Acuity
L1256: The review team defined a decrease in visual acuity as a clinically significant decrease in visual acuity (i.e., doubling of the visual angle). For subjects with a baseline and follow-up examination, a decrease in visual acuity was reported in 4/156 (3%) of subjects who received etrasimod compared to no subjects who received placebo. Based on these findings, the decrease in visual acuity will be described in the Adverse Reactions section of the label.
L1257: ## 8.2.6.6. Respiratory Effects
L1258: S1P receptor modulators have been associated with changes to pulmonary function including forced expiratory volume over 1 second (FEV1) and forced vital capacity (FVC). The Applicant evaluated the risk of pulmonary function changes as AESIs as described in Sections 8.2.4.4 and 8.2.5.4. However, the review team conducted a separate analysis of the pulmonary function data to better characterize the potential risk of pulmonary function changes. The analysis focused on data from APD334-301 and APD334-302 because pulmonary function testing was not performed during APD334-003. Refer to the Pulmonology Consult Review by Dr. Rekha Jhamnani for full details of the review (DARRTS ID: 5186355)
L1259: During APD334-301 and APD334-302, there were no respiratory SAEs, and no respiratory TEAEs that led to treatment interruption or discontinuation in either study. In Study APD334-301 the mean difference in change from baseline in FEV1 is shown in Table 67. A greater decrease in FEV1 was observed in the etrasimod group compared to placebo at Week 12, which was nominally significant. A difference was not present at Week 52; however, there was significant dropout in both the etrasimod and placebo groups between Week 12 and Week 52 that limited the interpretation of the Week 52 results. No difference was observed between etrasimod and placebo for FVC (not shown). Asthma and chronic obstructive pulmonary disease (COPD) subjects were included in the study, but the small sample size limited conclusions on pulmonary function effects in this population.

L1264: In Study APD334-302, there were no pulmonary function tests that were significantly different between the etrasimod group and placebo. As in Study APD334-301, asthma and COPD subjects were included in the study, but the small sample size limited conclusions on pulmonary function effects.
L1265: Based on these findings the Warnings and Precautions section and the Clinical Pharmacology section of the label will reflect the observed changes in FEV1.
L1266: ## 8.2.7. Safety Results for Integrated Summary of Safety
L1267: The Applicant submitted an integrated summary of safety (ISS), which was an analysis that pooled data from controlled and uncontrolled studies of subjects with UC. The dataset included APD334-301, APD334-302, and APD334-003 as well as the open-label extension (OLE) studies APD334-303, APD334-005, and ES101002. Refer to Section 7.1 for details regarding the studies included in the ISS. Given that much of the data was uncontrolled, safety information was considered supportive, and the review team focused on the assessment for rare events, given the extended duration of the open-label studies. Although etrasimod 1 mg was evaluated in APD334-003, the review team focused on safety assessments for etrasimod 2 mg because there were a limited number of subjects who received etrasimod 1 mg (i.e., 42 subjects for 12 weeks) and there were no additional safety findings for the etrasimod 1 mg group.
L1268: Table 68 shows the duration of exposure to etrasimod 2 mg in the overall safety database used for the primary assessment of safety and ISS.

L1272: ## 8.2.7.1. Deaths
L1273: One death was reported in the ISS from APD334-303 due to an SAE of “neuroendocrine tumor of unknown primary site.” The subject was a 33-year-old White male who was enrolled in APD334-301 where he received placebo. He was subsequently enrolled in APD334-303 where he received open-label etrasimod 2 mg for a total of 24 weeks. On Study Day 170, etrasimod 2 mg was stopped due to lack of efficacy. The subject was subsequently started on IV methylprednisolone (Study Day 171) followed by vedolizumab and rectal mesalazine (Study Day 185) for the treatment of his ulcerative colitis. The subject’s last study visit was conducted on Study Day 189. However, on Study Day 194 the subject had an unscheduled visit for a laboratory abnormality (GGT, not reported) which prompted an abdominal ultrasound (US). At
L1274: the time, the subject reported abdominal pain with cramping as well as bloating, nausea, vomiting, and loose stool. The abdominal US showed a large number of abdominal lymph nodes and liver lesions. Further imaging identified multiple lesions in the liver and bones (vertebrae, pelvis, sternum, and femur). Upon further testing, the subject was diagnosed with poorly differentiated neuroendocrine carcinoma of unknown origin. The subject initiated treatment on Study Day 207 for the neuroendocrine carcinoma; however, the subject died on Study Day 212. The Investigator and Sponsor assessed the event of neuroendocrine tumor as unlikely to be related to study treatment. Given the short duration of exposure to etrasimod, the review team assessed the event as unlikely to be related. S1P receptor modulators have been associated with cases of malignancies. Therefore, the label will include a Warnings and Precautions regarding the risk of malignancies.
L1275: ## 8.2.7.2. Serious Adverse Events
L1276: In the ISS, there were 50 subjects who received etrasimod 2 mg and reported at least 1 SAE that was not previously reported as an SAE during their parent study. There were 41 unique reports of SAEs that were not previously reported during APD334-301, APD334-302, and APD334-003. Most SAEs were reported by 1 subject. The only SAEs that were reported by more than 1 subject included: ulcerative colitis, COVID-19, anemia, gastroenteritis, iron deficiency anemia, migraine, and transient ischemic attack. One SAE led to death, which is described in the previous section. SAEs that led to study discontinuation were reported by 12 subjects and were not previously reported in the parent studies. These included atrial fibrillation (1), ulcerative colitis (9), herpes simplex meningitis (1), and transient ischemic attack (1). SAEs (other than ulcerative colitis) that led to study discontinuation are described below.
L1277: - **Herpes simplex meningitis:** An SAE of herpes simplex (HSV) meningitis was reported by a 66-year-old White male who had received placebo treatment in parent study APD334-301 and then received etrasimod 2 mg up to Study Day 12 of APD334-303. On Study Day 7, the subject reported symptoms of headache, nausea, blurry vision, and hypoesthesia. On Study Day 9, the subject presented to the emergency room (ER) with similar complaints. In the ER, chest X-ray and head/brain CT were normal; however, a lumbar puncture and examination of cerebrospinal fluid (CSF) showed lymphocytes and CSF HSV 1/2 IgG mildly elevated (which may indicate past or recent HSV infection). Of note, HSV 1/2 PCR was not detected. Brain MRI and cervical spine CT did not show evidence to suggest HSV encephalitis or meningitis. In consultation with an infectious disease specialist, the subject received acyclovir. The Investigator discontinued etrasimod due to the SAE of HSV meningitis on Study Day 18 and the last dose of study treatment was taken on Study Day 12. The Investigator and Applicant assessed the SAE as unlikely to be related to study treatment and due to the open-label design of the study the results are difficult to interpret. However, etrasimod may increase the susceptibility to infections, and herpes viral infections have been reported with S1P receptor modulators. Therefore, the label will include a Warnings and Precautions regarding the risk of infections, including herpes viral infections.
L1278: - **Atrial fibrillation:** During APD334-005, a 69-year-old White male who received placebo in APD334-003 presented on Study Day 197 with complaints of chest pain and shortness of breath. The subject had a past medical history of obesity and hyperglycemia. ECG results were considered abnormal with atrial fibrillation with controlled ventricular response, and the subject was hospitalized. Cardioversion was performed successfully, and the subject was discharged from the hospital on Study Day 198. Etrasimod was permanently withdrawn due to the AE of atrial fibrillation. The Investigator and Applicant reported the AE as not related to etrasimod. Atrial fibrillation is not a known risk of S1P receptor modulators, and the effects of S1P receptor modulators on the cardiovascular system have been related to bradyarrhythmia, AV conduction delays, and hypertension. The label will include a Warnings and Precautions regarding the risk of bradycardia and AV conduction delay.
- **Transient ischemic attack (TIA):** A 54-year-old male with a history of hypertension was hospitalized for Grade 2 TIA on Study Day 18 of APD334-303. The subject received etrasimod 2 mg in parent study APD334-301 prior to enrollment in APD334-303. On Study Day 15, the subject went to the hospital with symptoms suspicious for a TIA. Concomitant medications included sulfasalazine, nebivolol, and barnidipine. On Study Day 22, brain magnetic resonance imaging and magnetic resonance angiography did not show any acute cerebrovascular lesions of recent significance and the event was considered recovered/resolved. The subject withdrew from the study and study treatment (etrasimod) was discontinued due to an SAE of moderate TIA. TIA is not a known risk of S1P receptor modulators, and the results are difficult to interpret due to the open-label study design. However, hypertension was observed in the etrasimod development program and, therefore, a Warnings and Precautions regarding hypertension will be included in the label.
L1279: Overall, the unique SAEs reported in the ISS did not identify a new safety signal. Most subjects had a past medical history that may have contributed to the SAE. The majority of SAEs were reported by one subject. Additionally, due to the open-label nature of the extension studies (from which, unique subjects were identified), the safety information is challenging to interpret without a comparator arm.
L1280: ## **8.2.7.3. Adverse Events that Led to Study Discontinuation**
L1281: In the ISS, there were 21 subjects treated with etrasimod 2 mg who reported TEAEs that led to study discontinuation. TEAEs that led to study discontinuation and occurred in more than 2 subjects were ulcerative colitis, lymphopenia, and headache. TEAEs that led to study discontinuation that were also SAEs (other than ulcerative colitis) are described in the previous section. There were no TEAEs that led to study discontinuation during the extension studies APD334-303, APD334-005, and ES101002 that were considered AESIs. Overall, the TEAEs that led to study discontinuation in the ISS were similar to APD334-301, APD334-302, and APD334-003.
L1282: ## **8.2.7.4. Treatment Emergent Adverse Events of Special Interest**
L1283: The ISS was reviewed for AESIs that have been previously described in Section 8.2.4.4. No TEAEs of PML, respiratory disorders, or PRES were reported in the ISS. Pertinent TEAEs corresponding to the AESI categories previously described, are noted below.
L1284: - **Bradyarrhythmia, AV Conduction Delay, and Hypertension:** TEAEs of AV conduction delay (AV block first degree and AV block second degree) were reported in 3 subjects in the ISS that were not reported during the parent studies. All 3 subjects were switched from placebo to etrasimod 2 mg during either APD334-303, APD334-005, or ES101002. Additionally, all 3 subjects reported the AESI on the first day of treatment with etrasimod 2 mg, which is consistent with TEAEs observed in APD334-301, APD334-302, and APD334-003. No new subjects reported AESIs of bradycardia in the ISS. Two additional subjects reported a TEAE of hypertension in the ISS; however, neither TEAEs were SAEs nor led to study discontinuation.
- **Ophthalmologic Findings:** One additional subject reported a TEAE of “cystoid macular oedema” in the ISS that was not previously reported in APD334-301, APD334-302, or APD334-003. The subject was a 58-year-old White woman who received placebo in parent study APD334-301, and etrasimod 2 mg up to study day 248 in APD334-303. On Study Day 85, the subject had a moderate AESI of “cystoid macular oedema” according to findings from retinal photographs, which were reported as abnormal and clinically significant for the left eye. On Study Day 120, the event was considered recovered/resolved. A dilated fundusoscopic examination on this day was reported as abnormal clinically significant for epiretinal fibrosis and mild hyperopia; remaining structures in that examination were deemed normal at that time. On Study Day 169, OCT and retinal photographic findings were reported as abnormal clinically significant for paramacular retinal edema of the left eye. On Study Day 248, the subject had a moderate AESI of cystoid macular edema. There were no ophthalmic examination findings reported from this day. Study drug was discontinued due to the event with the last dose on Study Day 248. At the time of the subject’s last available report, the outcome of the event of cystoid macular edema was unknown.
- **Hepatotoxicity:** There were no TEAEs that met Hy’s law criteria during the ISS. There were 27 additional subjects who reported TEAEs related to elevated liver tests (preferred terms: ALT increased, AST increased, GGT increased, alkaline phosphatase increased, elevated liver enzymes, transaminases increased, and bilirubin increased). The majority of TEAEs were mild or moderate in severity and no subjects were discontinued from the study due to the TEAEs of elevated liver enzymes.
- **Infections:** In the ISS, there were 8 severe infections in subjects that received etrasimod 2 mg, which included COVID-19 (3), pneumonia (2), appendicitis (1), gastroenteritis (1), and pyelonephritis (1). Two subjects who received etrasimod 2 mg reported TEAEs that led to study discontinuation that had not been reported previously, herpes simplex
L1285: meningitis (described in Section 8.2.7.2) and latent tuberculosis (TB). The subject with latent tuberculosis had been screened with a “tuberculosis test” and chest X-ray during screening for APD334-301; however, an Interferon Gamma Release Assay was performed on Study Day 385, prior to initiating etrasimod 2 mg for APD334-303, which was positive. The subject was asymptomatic and was treated for latent TB but was discontinued from the study due to the AE of latent TB. Overall, the types of infection-related TEAEs reported in the ISS were similar to those described in APD334-301, APD334-302, and APD334-003.
L1286: - **Malignancy:** Two subjects reported one TEAE of malignancy in the ISS, which were not previously reported. One subject that received etrasimod 2 mg in parent study APD334-301 and continued on etrasimod 2 mg in APD334-303, reported a TEAE of “malignant melanoma” from the verbatim reported term of “Melanocytic mole manifestation.” However, this TEAE was incorrectly coded and was corrected to the term melanocytic nevus (i.e., not a malignancy). The second TEAE of malignancy was a neuroendocrine tumor resulting in death, which is described in Section 8.2.7.1.
L1287: ## 8.2.7.5. *Treatment Emergent Adverse Events*
L1288: The TEAEs reported in the ISS were generally similar to those reported in APD334-301, APD334-302, and APD334-003. The majority of TEAEs were mild or moderate in severity. The most common TEAEs in the ISS (reported by at least 10 subjects who received etrasimod 2 mg) were ulcerative colitis, COVID-19, lymphopenia, headache, upper respiratory tract infection, and GGT increased.
L1289: ## 8.2.7.6. *Summary of Integrated Summary of Safety*
L1290: Overall, safety data from the ISS did not identify any new safety signals. The reported AEs were generally similar to those in the placebo-controlled studies APD334-301, APD334-302, and APD334-003; however, interpretation of the results of the ISS is limited by the lack of a comparator arm and the open-label nature of APD334-303, APD334-005, and ES101002.
L1291: ## 8.2.8. *Safety Analyses by Demographic Subgroups*

L1319: ## 8.2.9. Specific Safety Studies/Clinical Trials
L1320: No specific safety studies or clinical trials were performed.
L1321: ## 8.2.10. 120-Day Safety Update Report
L1322: A 120-day safety update report (SUR) was provided by the Applicant that incorporated safety information and additional integrated cumulative clinical study safety data from February 1, 2022, to August 30, 2022. The safety update includes cumulative safety data from ongoing studies in subjects with UC (APD334-303 and ES101002), as well as from other indications APD334-202 (a phase 2, 14-Week induction study in subjects with Crohn's disease [CD]), and APD334-205 (a phase 2, 28-week OLE study in subjects with alopecia areata). The review team focused on the data available in subjects with UC to evaluate for rare events or events that may have a longer latency period.
L1323: As shown in Table 73, during the SUR, an additional 221 subjects had at least 52 weeks of exposure and 90 subjects had at least 104 weeks of exposure to etrasimod 2 mg as compared to the ISS.

L1329: ## Deaths
L1330: There were no additional deaths reported during the SUR.
L1331: ## Serious Adverse Events
L1332: SAEs were reported in an additional 27 subjects with UC in the SUR. The most frequent SAEs were ulcerative colitis (9) and COVID-19/COVID-19 pneumonia (2). No other SAEs were reported in more than a single subject during the SUR.
L1333: ## Discontinuation Due to Adverse Events
L1334: Twenty-five additional subjects discontinued study treatment due to an AE during the SUR. The most frequently reported TEAEs occurring in ≥ 2 subjects in the etrasimod 2 mg group that led to permanent study treatment discontinuation were colitis ulcerative (13), lymphocyte count decreased (3), and weight decreased (1).
L1335: ## Treatment Emergent Adverse Events of Special Interest
L1336: The SUR included AESIs that have been previously described in Section 8.2.4.4. No TEAEs of PML, respiratory disorders, or PRES were reported in the SUR. Pertinent TEAEs corresponding to the AESI categories previously described, are noted below.
L1337: ## Bradyarrhythmia, AV Conduction Delay, and Hypertension
L1338: - **Bradycardia:** Three additional subjects not previously reported in the ISS, experienced TEAEs of sinus bradycardia on Day 1. All sinus bradycardia TEAEs were asymptomatic and none of these subjects experienced an HR &lt; 50 bpm.
- **AV Conduction Delay:** No additional subjects with UC reported TEAEs related to AV conduction delay.
- **Acute myocardial infarction:** One subject that received etrasimod 2 mg experienced an SAE of acute myocardial infarction Grade 3 on Day 27 of ES101002. Prior to receiving open-label etrasimod 2 mg in ES101002, the subject received blinded treatment in the double-blind period of the study. Medical history included alcohol and tobacco use. Study treatment was discontinued due to the AE of acute myocardial infarction.
- **Hypertension:** TEAEs of hypertension were reported in 8 additional subjects during the SUR. None of the TEAEs were SAEs or led to study discontinuation.
L1339: ## Infections and Opportunistic Infections
L1340: The most common Infection TEAEs reported by subjects treated with etrasimod 2 mg during the SUR were COVID-19, upper respiratory tract infection, nasopharyngitis, urinary tract infection, bronchitis, and gastroenteritis. There were no additional TEAEs of PML, cryptococcus, nasopharyngitis, urinary tract infection, bronchitis, or gastroenteritis that occurred in UC subjects during the SUR.
L1341: - **Opportunistic Infections:** One subject with UC that received etrasimod 2 mg reported an TEAE of opportunistic infection of esophageal candidiasis on Study Day 42. The event was preceded by an SAE of rectal hemorrhage and study treatment withdrawal on Study Day 35 prior to SDEI onset.
- **Severe Infections:** Six additional subjects in the etrasimod 2 mg group reported an TEAE of severe infection. The types of infections were varied. Previously unreported serious infection PTs included bacterial superinfection, anal abscess, colonic abscess, diverticulitis, and vestibular neuronitis.
- **Herpes Viral Infections:** One additional subject each in the etrasimod 2 mg group experienced TEAEs oral herpes and herpes zoster. Both TEAEs were not SAEs and did not lead to study discontinuation.
- **Lymphopenia:** During the SUR, 108 UC subjects treated with etrasimod 2 mg reported TEAEs of lymphopenia (PTs: lymphopenia, lymphocyte count, lymphocyte percentage decreased, and CD4 lymphocytes decreased). Three subjects were discontinued from treatment due to the TEAEs of decreased lymphocyte count.
L1342: ## Hepatic Injury
L1343: Twelve additional subjects experienced TEAEs related to hepatic injury. TEAEs reported by &gt; 1 subject were: hepatic function abnormal (6) and cholangitis sclerosing (2). One subject in the etrasimod 2 mg group reported 1 SAE of cholelithiasis that led to temporary interruption of study treatment. There were no AEs that led to study treatment discontinuation and no TEAEs that met Hy's law criteria.
L1344: ## **Treatment Emergent Adverse Events**
L1345: The most frequently reported TEAEs during the SUR were colitis ulcerative, COVID-19, lymphopenia, anemia, lymphocyte count decreased, and headache. These TEAEs are similar to those reported in the placebo-controlled studies APD334-301, APD334-302, and APD334-003 as well as the ISS.
L1346: ## **Summary of the 120-Day Safety Report**
L1347: The SUR did not identify any new safety signals; however, the interpretation of data from the SUR is limited due to the lack of a comparator arm. Overall, similar TEAEs were reported,
L1348: consistent with data from the primary safety analysis, performed with placebo-controlled data from APD334-301, APD334-302, and APD334-003.
L1349: ## 8.2.11. Human Reproduction and Pregnancy
L1350: ## Pregnancy
L1351: Pregnant women were excluded from clinical trials with etrasimod during the clinical development program. At the time of the SUR a total of 11 pregnancy exposure cases were reported with outcomes including: normal livebirth (2), preterm birth (1), ongoing (6), elective abortion (1), and spontaneous abortion (1). Overall, these limited available human data are insufficient to evaluate for a drug-associated risk of major birth defects, miscarriage, or other adverse maternal or fetal outcomes.
L1352: Etrasimod is indicated for a condition that would be expected to be seen in females of reproductive potential (including during pregnancy), and current data are insufficient to inform women regarding etrasimod use during pregnancy. Therefore, labeling will include that based on findings from animal studies, etrasimod may cause fetal harm when administered to a pregnant woman. In addition, postmarketing requirements will be issued for the Applicant to conduct a pregnancy exposure registry and a complementary study of a different design (such as a claims database study). Language regarding the pregnancy exposure registry should be added to Subsection 8.1 and Section 17 of labeling once the study protocol has been finalized and registry contact information established.
L1353: ## Lactation
L1354: Lactating women were excluded from clinical trials with etrasimod. At the time of the 120-day SUR, no lactation exposure cases were reported. Etrasimod is indicated for a condition that would be expected to be seen in females of reproductive potential (including during lactation), and there are no current data available to inform women regarding etrasimod use during lactation. Therefore, a PMR will be issued for the Applicant to conduct a clinical lactation study with etrasimod to inform labeling.
L1355: ## Females and Males of Reproductive Potential
L1356: There are no available human data on the effects of etrasimod on male or female fertility. Based on animal data, etrasimod may cause fetal harm when administered to pregnant women. Therefore, labeling will include that females of reproductive potential should be counseled on the potential for a serious risk to the fetus and the need for effective contraception during treatment with etrasimod and for one week following the last dose.
L1357: ## 8.2.12. Pediatrics and Assessment of Effects on Growth
L1358: Insufficient pediatric data were submitted as part of this BLA to support an indication in pediatric subjects or to inform an assessment of the impact of etrasimod on growth. At the
L1359: time of this review, pediatric trials were ongoing. Refer to Section 13 Postmarketing Requirements and Commitments.
L1360: ## 8.2.13. Overdose, Drug Abuse Potential, Withdrawal, and Rebound
L1361: There are no specific concerns about drug abuse potential or withdrawal with etrasimod use in UC.
L1362: ## 8.2.14. Safety in the Postmarket Setting
L1363: ## Safety Concerns Identified Through Postmarket Experience
L1364: No postmarketing experience is available because etrasimod is not currently approved in any country.
L1365: ## Expectations on Safety in the Postmarket Setting
L1366: Refer to Section 13 for details regarding postmarketing requirements and commitments agreed upon with the Applicant.
L1367: ## 8.3. Conclusions and Recommendations
L1368: The Applicant submitted the results of two adequate and well-controlled phase 3 trials (APD334-301 and APD334-302) to establish the substantial evidence of effectiveness. The review team agreed with the Applicant's conclusion that a clinically meaningful and statistically significant benefit was demonstrated on the primary endpoints and multiple secondary endpoints for APD334-301 and APD334-302.
L1369: The Applicant submitted data from the 52-week phase 3 trial APD334-301, the 12-week phase 3 trial APD334-302, and 12-week phase 2 trial APD334-003 to support the safety of etrasimod 2 mg in subjects with moderately to severely active UC. The safety profile of etrasimod 2 mg once daily for up to 52 weeks was acceptable and supports approval of the proposed dosing regimen. There are several known potential risks related to the S1P receptor modulator class which were observed in the etrasimod development program (i.e., infections, bradyarrhythmia and atrioventricular conduction delays, liver injury, macular edema, increased blood pressure, and respiratory effects), which were consistent with other S1P receptor modulators. The labeling is considered adequate to communicate these risks and provide appropriate risk mitigation.
L1370: ## 9 Advisory Committee Meeting and Other External Consultations
L1371: An Advisory Committee meeting was not held to discuss this application as the application did not raise significant safety or efficacy issues that were unexpected for a drug of this class to warrant input from an Advisory Committee.
L1372: ## 10 Pediatrics

L1374: However, only 1 adolescent subject (who received placebo) was enrolled in APD334-301, and only 2 adolescent subjects (1 placebo, 1 etrasimod) were enrolled in APD334-302. Therefore, given data were available for only one adolescent subject who received etrasimod, the Division communicated to the Applicant that (b) (4)
L1375: Postmarketing requirements under the Pediatric Research Equity Act (PREA) will be issued; refer to Section 13.
L1376: The Applicant submitted an amended agreed initial pediatric study plan (iPSP). In the amended agreed iPSP, the Applicant requested a waiver for pediatric assessment for the birth to &lt;2 years of age pediatric age group due to studies being impossible or highly impracticable. The Applicant also requested a deferral of pediatric studies because the adult studies were completed prior to completion of the pediatric studies. The review team agreed with the waiver and deferral requests.
L1377: In the amended agreed iPSP, the Applicant proposed two pediatric clinical studies. At the time of the review of the NDA submission, a one-year, phase 2, multicenter, open-label, single-arm, sentinel dose cohort study to evaluate the efficacy, PK, and safety of etrasimod in adolescent subjects 12 to &lt;18 years of age with moderately to severely active UC was ongoing (i.e., APD334-207). (b) (4)
L1378: (refer to Section 7.2).
L1379: Given the lack of data available for etrasimod in pediatric subjects with UC, the review team anticipates that data from APD334-207 may be necessary to inform the design of the future pediatric program, particularly to inform dose selection for the second pediatric study (i.e., APD334-208). Therefore, the PREA PMRs will include one PMR to finish the ongoing pediatric study APD334-207 and a second PMR to conduct a one-year study to evaluate the safety, efficacy, pharmacokinetics, and pharmacodynamics of etrasimod in pediatric patients 2 to 17 years of age with moderately to severely active ulcerative colitis. The PREA PMRs are described in Section 13.

DOSAGE AND ADMINISTRATION	
The following assessments and vaccinations prior to initiation of treatment with etrasimod were added:
Ophthalmologic assessment: A baseline evaluation of the fundus, including the macula, near the start of treatment in all subjects was added to align with other S1P receptor modulators and to monitor for macular edema, which was observed during the etrasimod development program. Refer to Sections 8.2.4, 8.2.5, and 8.2.6.5.
A recommendation to test for varicella zoster virus (VZV) antibodies in patients without a full course of vaccination against VZV and vaccinate antibody negative patients before starting treatment was added to align with other S1P receptor modulators and describe the risk of herpes viral infections.
Skin examination: A baseline skin examination was added to mitigate the potential risk of skin malignancy.

L1385: and prescribers. The following subsections had revisions that were noteworthy:

5 ADVERSE REACTIONS
Clinical Trials Experience	This section was updated to describe the results of APD334-301 (i.e., UC-1, a 52-week trial) and pooled data from APD334-302 and APD334-003 (i.e., UC-2 and UC-3, two 12-week trials). The review team did not agree with pooling data from APD334-301, APD334-302, and APD334-003, due to the different durations of exposure of the trials.

Studies UC-2 and UC-3: headache, elevated liver tests, nausea, bradycardia, and urinary tract infection. Refer to Section 8.2.5.5.

This subsection was added to describe the impact of hepatic impairment on etrasimod exposure and that use in patients with severe hepatic impairment is not recommended.
CYP2C9 Poor Metabolizers
A new subsection was added with the recommendation to avoid concomitant use of Velsipity in subjects who are poor CYP2C9 metabolizers and receiving concomitant moderate to strong CYP2C8 or CYP3A4 inhibitors because of increased etrasimod exposure.

CLINICAL PHARMACOLOGY	
Mechanism of Action
The mechanism of action section was modified to describe that etrasimod has minimal activity on S1P3 and no activity on S1P2, rather than “no activity” on S1P2 and S1P3. (b) (4)
Pharmacodynamics
The median time for peripheral blood lymphocytes to return to baseline and the normal range was updated to reflect data in subjects with UC, rather than a population PK model. Refer to Section 16.3.2.
Pharmacokinetics
L1397: Data from drug-drug interaction studies with itraconazole and gemfibrozil were added.
Pharmacogenomics
This subsection was added to describe the potential impact of genetic variants of CYP2C9 alleles on CYP2C9 activity, including the potential for decreased clearance of etrasimod in CYP2C9 intermediate and poor metabolizers when used concomitantly with moderate to strong inhibitors of CYP2C8 or CYP3A4.

CLINICAL STUDIES	Demographic information for UC-1 and UC-2 was added to describe the trial populations.
The following changes were made to the results from UC-1 and UC-2:The number of subjects in each treatment arm were updated to match the review team's analysis, which included the removal of 1 adolescent subject.
The proportion of subjects meeting efficacy endpoints were updated according to the review teams analyses. Refer to Section 8.1.2 and 8.1.4.
The endpoint of "symptomatic remission" was removed because it is not considered to provide additional, clinically meaningful information versus "clinical remission." Information regarding improvements in stool frequency and rectal bleeding are described individually below the table in the Stool Frequency and Rectal Bleeding Subscores.
The endpoint of "clinical response" at Week 12 was moved out of the efficacy endpoints table and added below the table in text. This endpoint was not multiplicity-controlled; however, given this endpoint is important to patients and prescribers, the review team agreed with including it in the label.
A description of a supplementary analysis of the primary endpoint of clinical remission in a subgroup of subjects with an mMS of 4 to 9 was removed, as this population was not consistent with the preferred
L1398: definition of moderately to severely active UC.
(b) (4)
Several different assessments of histologic-endoscopic improvement using various definitions were removed because they were not multiplicity-controlled endpoints (e.g., endoscopic remission [ES of 0] and Geboes <2.0) or were redundant (e.g., ES \le 1 and Geboes \le 3.1) to the endpoint of histologic-endoscopic mucosal improvement in the efficacy table (defined as an ES \le 1 and Geboes score <2.0).
Endpoints related to endoscopic remission (i.e., ES = 0) were permitted for Week 12 (UC-1 and UC-2) and Week 52 (UC-1). Although this endpoint was not multiplicity-controlled, it demonstrated a nominally significant effect and represents a clinically relevant endpoint that is informative to prescribers.
(b) (4)
The following changes were made for UC-1:The endpoint of “Clinical response” at Week 52 was removed because it was not viewed as clinically relevant, as patients and prescribers would expect clinical remission (i.e., improvement in symptoms and endoscopy) at such a late timepoint. Furthermore, the endpoint was not multiplicity-controlled.

L1407: ## 13 Postmarketing Requirements and Commitment
L1408: Under the Pediatric Research Equity Act (PREA) (21 U.S.C. 355c), all applications for new active ingredients (which includes new salts and new fixed-combinations), new indications, new dosage forms, new dosing regimens, or new routes of administration are required to contain an assessment of the safety and effectiveness of the product for the claimed indication in pediatric patients unless this requirement is waived, deferred, or inapplicable. NDA 216956 triggers PREA because etrasimod is a new active ingredient. The following PREA PMRs will be issued.
L1409: **4499-1** Complete the ongoing phase 2 study to evaluate the safety, efficacy, pharmacokinetics, and pharmacodynamics of Velsipity (etrasimod) in pediatric patients 12 to less than 18 years of age with moderately to severely active ulcerative colitis.
L1410: Study Completion: November 2025
L1411: Final Report Submission: May 2026
L1412: **4499-2** Conduct a one-year study to evaluate the safety, efficacy, pharmacokinetics, and pharmacodynamics of Velsipity (etrasimod) in pediatric patients 2 to less than 18 years of age with moderately to severely active ulcerative colitis.
L1413: Final Protocol Submission: February 2024
L1414: Study Completion: February 2028
L1415: Final Report Submission: August 2028
L1416: The following postmarketing requirements (PMRs) under Section 505(o)(3) of FDAAA will be issued at the time of approval of the application.
L1417: **4499-3** Perform a lactation study (milk only) in lactating women who have received Velsipity (etrasimod), regardless of indication, to assess concentrations of etrasimod in breast milk using a validated assay and to assess the effects on the breastfed infant.
L1418: Draft Protocol Submission: June 2024
L1419: Final Protocol Submission: November 2024
L1420: Study Completion: November 2026
L1421: Final Report Completion: November 2027
L1422: **4499-4** Collect data from a prospective pregnancy exposure registry, preferably a disease-based multiproduct pregnancy registry, using a cohort analysis that compares the maternal, fetal, and infant outcomes of women exposed to etrasimod regardless of indication during pregnancy with unexposed comparator
L1423: population(s) in a timely manner. Align the study protocol with protocol(s) outside the US to reach the target sample size. The registry will identify and record pregnancy complications, major and minor congenital malformations, spontaneous abortion, stillbirths, elective terminations, preterm births, small-for-gestational-age births, and any other adverse outcomes, including postnatal growth and development. Outcomes described in the protocol will be assessed throughout pregnancy. Infant outcomes, including effects on postnatal growth and development, will be assessed through at least the first year of life.
L1424: Draft Protocol Submission: May 2024
Final Protocol Submission: January 2025
Study Completion: September 2032
Final Report Submission: September 2033
L1425: **4944-5** Conduct an additional pregnancy study that uses a different design from the prospective pregnancy registry established to fulfill postmarketing requirement 4499-4 (for example a retrospective cohort study using claims or electronic medical record data or a case-control study) to assess major congenital malformations, spontaneous abortions, stillbirths, small for gestational age births, and preterm births in women exposed to etrasimod regardless of indication during pregnancy compared to an unexposed control population.
L1426: Draft Protocol Submission: May 2024
Final Protocol Submission: January 2025
Study Completion: March 2031

L1427: ## 14 Division Director (Clinical) Comments
L1428: I concur with the recommendation of the review team to approve NDA for Velsipity (etrasimod) for the treatment of moderately to severely active ulcerative colitis (UC) in adults. Etrasimod, a new molecular entity (NME), is a sphingosine 1-phosphate (S1P) receptor modulator that binds with high affinity to S1P receptors 1, 4, and 5. Other S1P modulators have been approved for UC, multiple sclerosis, psoriasis, and graft-versus-host disease. The recommended dosage is 2 mg once daily.
L1429: As discussed in detail in this multi-disciplinary review, the benefits of etrasimod outweigh the risks for the indication sought. The NDA submission included the results of two adequate and well-controlled phase 3 trials (APD334-301 and APD334-302), which formed the basis of the substantial evidence of effectiveness. The co-primary endpoints of clinical remission at Week 12 and Week 52 in APD334-301 and clinical remission at Week 12 in APD334-301, all assessed by
L1430: modified Mayo score, achieved statistical significance. Secondary endpoints also supported the primary efficacy analyses in both trials.
L1431: The safety profile was generally consistent with the known adverse event profile for S1P receptor modulators. Some of the adverse events of special interest, such as infections including herpes zoster, liver injury, and malignancies, are shared across multiple drugs used to treat UC; however, other observed or potential risks, such as bradyarrhythmia and atrioventricular conduction delays, macular edema, fetal risk, and respiratory effects, are unique to this drug class. The most common adverse reactions reported in clinical trials of patients with UC include headache, elevated liver tests, and dizziness. Product Labeling (including Contraindications and Warnings and Precautions) and Medication Guide are adequate to communicate these known and potential risks to healthcare providers and patients, respectively. A REMS will not be required.
L1432: Postmarketing required studies, under Section 505(o)(3) of the Federal Food, Drug, and Cosmetic Act, will assess the pregnancy outcomes following etrasimod exposure during pregnancy and the effect of etrasimod on lactation. In addition, the Applicant agreed to conduct postmarketing required studies, under the Pediatric Research Equity Act, to assess the safety, efficacy, pharmacokinetics, and pharmacodynamics of etrasimod in pediatric patients 12 to 17 years of age with moderately to severely active UC; and the safety, efficacy, pharmacokinetics, and pharmacodynamics of etrasimod in pediatric patients 2 to 17 years of age with moderately to severely active UC.
L1433: ## 15 Office Director (or Designated Signatory Authority) Comments
L1434: I concur with the review team's assessment of the application, labeling recommendations, and the recommendation to approve etrasimod, a sphingosine 1-phosphate (S1P) receptor modulator, for the treatment of moderately to severely active ulcerative colitis (UC) in adults, as detailed in this document.
L1435: The regulatory action is Approval of NDA 216956 for Velsipity (etrasimod) for the treatment of moderately to severely active ulcerative colitis (UC) in adults.
L1436: A REMS is not warranted at this time. Postmarketing required (PMR) studies will include those required under PREA, as well as pregnancy and lactation studies, as detailed in Section 13.

L1448: ## 16.3.1. Population PK Analysis
L1449: The Applicant's population PK (PopPK) analysis included pooled PK data from 15 phase 1 studies, 2 phase 2 studies (APD334-003 and APD334-005), and 2 phase 3 studies (APD334-301 and APD334-302) in patients with moderately to severely active UC as shown in Table 75. The demographic information of the subjects included in the population PK analysis are shown in Table 76 and Table 77.

L1462: The final PopPK dataset included a total of 16,205 quantifiable etrasimod plasma concentrations from 1079 subjects.
L1463: The basic PopPK structure model consisted of a two-compartment model with sequential zero- and first-order absorption and first-order elimination as shown in Figure 15.
L1464: **Figure 15. Schematic Representation of the Structural Population Pharmacokinetic Model of Etrasimod**
L1465: Source: Population PK Report ARE0301H-UC-PK-Report, Figure 1
L1466: Abbreviations: F1: bioavailability to the absorption compartment; D1: zero-order absorption duration; k_a: first-order absorption rate constant; CL: systemic clearance; V_2: central volume of distribution; Q: intercompartmental clearance; V_3: peripheral volume of distribution
L1467: The base model also included fixed weight effects on clearance and volume parameters:
L1468: CL_{\text{Weight}} = 0.75 \cdot \log\left(\frac{WT_i}{70}\right)
L1469: V_{\text{Weight}} = \log\left(\frac{WT_i}{70}\right)
L1470: Additionally, for covariate analyses, age, sex, patient status, ethnicity (Japanese, Chinese, or other ethnicities), race (Black or African American versus other races), baseline bilirubin, eGFR, tobacco use, and formulations were tested for their potential influence on the apparent clearance, volume of distribution, bioavailability, first-order absorption rate constant (k_a), and zero-order absorption duration (D_1) of etrasimod.
L1471: Briefly, a covariate modeling approach emphasizing parameter estimation rather than stepwise hypothesis testing was implemented as the primary covariate analysis methodology. A full model was constructed to avoid correlation or collinearity in predictors (covariates with correlation coefficients &gt; |0.6| were not simultaneously included as potential predictors). Population parameters, including fixed effects parameters (covariate coefficients and structural model parameters), and random effects parameters were estimated. An exploratory assessment of any remaining trends was conducted by graphical inspection of all covariate effects.
L1472: The final PopPK model that best described etrasimod PK was a two-compartment model with sequential zero- and first-order absorption. The PK parameter estimates from the final PopPK model are summarized in Table 78, Table 79, Table 80.

L1485: Figure 16 displays a visual predictive check of the final population PK model for etrasimod.
L1486: **Figure 16. Visual Predictive Check of the Final PopPK Model for Etrasimod**
L1487: Source: Population PK Report ARE0301H-UC-PK-Report, Figure S163
L1488: Note: Black lines represent the median (solid), 5th and 95th percentiles (dashed) of the observed data. Blue and grey shaded regions represent the 95% confidence intervals of the corresponding (i.e., 5th, 50th, and 95th) percentiles.
L1489: Overall, the PopPK model estimated the typical values of CL/F and Vss/F of etrasimod were 1.06 L/h and 70 L, respectively, for a 70 kg subject. The mean effective half-life (t_{1/2,eff}) was 33.9 hours in patients with UC. Mean etrasimod PK parameters in patients with UC estimated using PopPK analysis are summarized in Table 12.
L1490: ## Velsipity (etrasimod)
L1491: PopPK estimates that healthy subjects have slightly smaller (within 10%) mean etrasimod C_{max,ss} and AUC_{ss} compared to reference UC patient of the same body weight, following 2 mg QD dosing. Hence, disease status is not expected to have a clinically significant effect on etrasimod PK exposure in subjects with UC.
L1492: ## Effect of Body Weight on PK
L1493: Body weight was identified as a statistically significant covariate on both clearance and volume for etrasimod. Etrasimod exposures are higher in subjects with lower body weight and lower in subjects with higher body weight. Based on popPK analysis, the predicted C_{max,ss}, AUC_{ss}, and C_{trough,ss} values for etrasimod in a typical 50 kg subject are 125, 123, and 120%, respectively, of those from a reference 70 kg subject. The predicted C_{max,ss}, AUC_{ss}, and C_{trough,ss} values for etrasimod in a typical 110 kg subject are 74.1, 75.7, and 77.9%, respectively, of those from a reference 70 kg subject. Hence, the effect of body weight on PK is not considered to be clinically significant to warrant dose adjustment across the typical body weight range in adults.
L1494: ## Smoking Status
L1495: Population PK analysis estimated that steady state PK estimates (C_{max,ss}, AUC_{ss}, and C_{trough,ss}) of etrasimod in a typical tobacco user are 99.1, 98.8, and 98.5%, respectively, of those from a reference male non-user of tobacco of the same body weight.
L1496: In addition, PopPK estimates that there are no meaningful differences in the PK of etrasimod based on sex, age, race, or ethnicity in adults.
L1497: ## 16.3.2. Exposure-Response Analysis
L1498: Population PK-PD analyses were conducted to characterize the exposure-response (E-R) relationships between etrasimod and lymphocyte count, clinical efficacy endpoints (clinical remission, endoscopic improvement, symptomatic remission, and mucosal healing at Week 12), and heart rate.
L1499: The Applicant conducted population E-R analyses using data from 15 phase 1 studies in adult healthy volunteers and adults with hepatic or renal impairment, two phase 2 studies in adult patients with UC, and two phase 3 studies in adult patients with UC. Post hoc estimates of PK parameters for each subject from the final PopPK model were included in the PK-PD analysis.
L1500: ## Lymphocyte Count
L1501: Exploratory graphical evaluations of the observed lymphocyte count versus time and relationships between exposure and response were initially performed prior to analysis (Figure 17). An indirect response model with a circadian rhythm for lymphocyte egress over a 24-hour period was used to describe the etrasimod and lymphocyte relationship.
L1502: Figure 17. Base Lymphocyte Model Diagram
L1503: CL: systemic clearance

L1506: k_{out}: removal rate constant

L1508: I_{max}: maximum inhibition
L1509: V_3: peripheral volume of distribution
L1510: IC_{50}: concentration required to achieve half the maximum inhibition
L1511: k_a: first-order absorption rate constant
L1512: D_1: zero-order absorption duration
L1513: Amp_{24}: amplitude for 24-hour period
L1514: F_1: bioavailability to the absorption compartment
L1515: Ph_{24}: phase for 24-hour period
L1516: Source: Population PK-PD ARE0301H-UC-Lymphocytes Report, Figure 1
L1517: The covariates tested in the full model included age, height, body weight, body mass index (BMI), sex, race, ethnicity (Western, Japanese, and Chinese), patient status (UC versus healthy, subscores and composite scores at baseline, baseline disease activity (modified Mayo score), and duration of disease), tobacco use at baseline, and concomitant and prior medications.
L1518: Overall, visual inspection indicates that the PK-PD model for lymphocyte count is generally predictive for the central tendency of lymphocyte count since the median of the observed lymphocyte are generally within the 95% CI of the simulated data (Figure 18).

L1522: Simulations were performed to predict the typical time to return to a normal level of lymphocytes after discontinuation of etrasimod dosing at steady state (Figure 19). As expected, higher doses increased suppression of lymphocyte egress. Lymphocyte returned to near (within 10% of) baseline within one to two weeks following discontinuation of steady-state dosing.

L1533: ## Clinical Efficacy Endpoints
L1534: Etrasimod exposure-response analyses for key primary (clinical remission) and secondary efficacy endpoints (endoscopic improvement, symptomatic remission, and mucosal healing) after 12 weeks of treatment with etrasimod were performed using data from one phase 2 study (APD334-003) and two phase 3 studies (APD334-301 and APD334-302) in adult patients with
L1535: UC. Post hoc estimates of PK parameters for each subject from the final PopPK model were included in the PK-PD analysis.
L1536: Exploratory graphical evaluations were performed initially to assess possible trends in the data prior to analysis. For each endpoint, logistic regression analyses were performed to develop a model describing the relationship between the steady state exposure of etrasimod (C_{\text{avg,ss}}), and the probability of achieving a clinical endpoint.
L1537: The following covariates were tested in the full model: age, sex, race, ethnicity (Western, Japanese, and Chinese), body weight, tobacco use at baseline, naïve to biologic/JAK inhibitor therapy at study entry (Yes/No), concomitant corticosteroid use, disease status [composite scores at baseline, baseline disease activity (MMS)].
L1538: Visual inspection of the E-R models for clinical remission at Week 12 (as presented in Figure 20) and key secondary efficacy endpoints at Week 12 (not presented in this review) appear to be generally predictive.
L1539: **Figure 20. Visual Predictive Check of Clinical Remission Proportions at Week 12, Stratified by Study and Treatment Group**
L1540: Source: Population PK-PD ARE0301H-UC-Efficacy Report, Figure S37:
L1541: The red dashed lines indicate the observed endpoint proportions. Distributions of simulated proportions are represented by the histograms.
L1542: The black dashed and dash-dot lines represent the medians and 90% prediction intervals, respectively. The text annotations show the simulated medians and 90% prediction intervals, respectively, and the observed proportions.
L1543: Abbreviations: Sim = simulated; Obs = observed
L1544: The E-R relationships for the observed rates of clinical remission at Week 12 and main secondary efficacy endpoints (endoscopic improvement, symptomatic remission, and mucosal healing) at Week 12 are presented in Figure 6.
L1545: Overall, the final models suggest that there is an apparent positive E-R relationship for clinical remission at Week 12, as well as for the secondary endpoints (endoscopic improvement,
L1546: symptomatic remission, and mucosal healing) at Week 12. Across the range of exposures, there appeared to be a trend toward greater efficacy response with higher etrasimod exposure.
L1547: ## Heart Rate
L1548: An E-R analysis for heart rate was conducted using heart rate data obtained from 12-lead safety ECG and Holter monitor measurements in two phase 1 studies APD334-008 (QT study) and APD334-110 (cardiodynamic effect study). Post hoc estimates of PK parameters from the final PopPK model were included in the analysis.
L1549: Exploratory graphical evaluations of the observed heart rate versus time and relationships between exposure and response (e.g., heart rate nadirs) were performed prior to analysis. Initial modeling was conducted based on a direct effect of etrasimod concentration on heart rate with circadian rhythm and etrasimod-dependent tolerance. Subsequently, other simplified structures for the tolerance mechanism were evaluated in addition to the combination of other circadian rhythms and placebo effects. In the final model (Figure 21), etrasimod concentrations were assumed to decrease heart rate via a direct inhibitory Emax model with a maximum effect that decreased with increasing tolerance.
L1550: **Figure 21. PK-PD Model for Etrasimod Effect on Heart Rate**
L1551: Source: Population PK-PD ARE0301H-HR Report, Figure 2
L1552: Overall, visual inspection indicates that the PK-PD model for heart rate is generally predictive for the central tendency of heart rate since the median of the observed lymphocyte are generally within the 90% CI of the simulated data (Figure 22).
L1553: **Figure 22. Visual Predictive Check for Heart Rate Versus Time (a) and Time of Days (b)**
L1554: Source: Population PK-PD ARE0301H-HR Report, Figure 12
L1555: Note: The lines represent the median (solid) or 5th/95th (dashed) percentiles of the observed data. The shaded areas represent 90% prediction intervals for median (grey) or 5th/95th percentiles for data simulated under the model.
L1556: Abbreviations: HR = heart rate
L1557: Simulations were performed to compare the predicted heart rate in subjects with UC following 2 mg QD for 21 days versus 2 mg QD with dose titration regimen (i.e., 0.25 mg QD on Days 1 and 2, 0.5 mg on Day 3, 0.75 mg on Day 4, 1.25 mg on Day 5, and 2 mg QD on Days 6-21). The model estimated that the extent of maximal HR reduction was generally similar between regimens with and without dose titration although the time to maximal HR reduction was delayed with the regimen with dose titration (reached after 5 days of treatment) compared to that following 2 mg QD without titration (reached on Day 1) (Figure 3).
L1558: ## 16.3.3. Drug Interaction Studies
L1559: ## Is Etrasimod a Substrate of CYP Enzymes?
L1560: Yes. In vitro studies (Study XT164054 and Study APD334.DMPK.010) indicated that CYP2C8, CYP2C9, and CYP3A4 are the major CYPs involved in the metabolism of etrasimod with fraction metabolized of 0.38, 0.37 and 0.22, respectively, with CYP2C19 and CYP2J2 as minor contributors of 0.01.
L1561: ## Identification of In Vitro Metabolites
L1562: In vitro study (MPR-11-005) using human liver microsomes identified five metabolites, M1, M2, M3, M4, and M5. Metabolite M1 was an acyl-glucuronide of etrasimod whereas M2 and M3 were 2 mono-hydroxyl metabolites. M2 was the major metabolite produced by rCYP2C8 and rCYP2C19 whereas M3 was primarily produced by CYP2C9*1 (MPR-16-002). M2 and M3 were further metabolized to glucuronide conjugates, M4 and M5, respectively.
L1563: In in vitro study (MPR-16-003), 18 metabolites of etrasimod were identified in human hepatocytes following incubation with [^{14}C]etrasimod (20 \muM). Among them, the hydroxyl (M3) and ketone (M6) metabolites were the most abundant at 4.3% and 9.0% of total radioactivity;
L1564: while all other metabolites formed were less than 4% of total radioactivity, including the glucuronide metabolite M1 and other metabolites that included combination of oxidation and glucuronidation.
L1565: ## Identification of Human CYP Enzymes Involved in the Metabolism of Etrasimod
L1566: Based on in vitro study (MPR-16-002) using recombinant human CYP enzymes, CYP2C9*1, CYP2C19, and CYP2C8 were the primary enzymes involved in the oxidative metabolism of etrasimod. M2 was the major metabolite produced by rCYP2C8 and rCYP2C19 whereas M3 was primarily produced by CYP2C9*1; and M6 was a minor metabolite of all three rCYPs.
L1567: ## **Effect of Other Drugs on Etrasimod PK**
L1568: Three clinical DDI studies (APD334-009, APD334-111 and APD334-116) were conducted and see Section 6.3.2 above for details.
L1569: ## Concomitant Inducers
L1570: In a clinical DDI study (APD334-009), rifampin (a strong inducer of CYP3A4 and CYP2C19, and a moderate inducer of CYP2C8 and CYP2C9) reduced AUC of etrasimod by 49%.
L1571: ## Concomitant Inhibitors
L1572: In a clinical DDI study (APD334-111), fluconazole (a moderate inhibitor of CYP2C9 and CYP3A4, and a strong inhibitor of CYP2C19) increased AUC of etrasimod by up to 84%.
L1573: In a clinical drug interaction study (APD334-116, co-administration of itraconazole (a strong inhibitor of CYP3A4) 200 mg QD for 13 days with a single dose of etrasimod 1 mg had no or minimum impact on the mean C_{max} (6% increase) but resulted in an increase of 32% in mean AUC_{0-inf} of etrasimod.
L1574: ## **Is Etrasimod an Inhibitor and/or an Inducer of CYP Enzymes?**
L1575: The ability of etrasimod to inhibit or induce the activity of metabolic enzymes was investigated in vitro in HLM (inhibition), rUGTs (inhibition), and human hepatocytes (induction only).
L1576: ## CYP Enzyme/UGT Inhibition
L1577: - Etrasimod did not inhibit human CYPs or UGTs (see comments below for details).
- Two in vitro studies (Study MPR-11-004; Study XT165088) were conducted to evaluate the potential of etrasimod to inhibit CYP1A2, CYP2B6, CYP2C8, CYP2C9, CYP2C19, CYP2D6, or CYP3A4/5. Activity was assessed at concentrations of etrasimod up to 50 \muM using selective probe substrates. There was little or no direct or time dependent inhibition by etrasimod (10 \muM) with any of the CYP enzymes evaluated, except for CYP2C8. Etrasimod reversibly inhibited CYP2C8 with an IC_{50} of 4.4 \muM (amodiaquine as the in vitro substrate). However,
L1578: ## Velsipity (etrasimod)
L1579: the calculated AUCR (ratio of area under the concentration-time curve) was &lt; 1.25 (using repaglinide as the sensitive clinical CYP2C8 substrate).
L1580: - Etrasimod also reversibly inhibited CYP2C8 with an IC_{50} value of 34.7 \mu M in the study with paclitaxel as a probe substrate (Study MPR-11-004). However, the calculated AUCR value of 1.14 for CYP2C8 inhibition was less than the threshold value of 1.25 that would necessitate conduct of a clinical DDI study. Thus, etrasimod is not likely to be a clinically relevant CYP2C8 inhibitor.
- In Study XT165088, etrasimod did not inhibit UGT1A3, UGT1A4, UGT1A9, UGT2B7, or UGT2B17. However, etrasimod directly inhibited UGT1A1 and UGT1A6-mediated activities up to 28% and 48%, respectively, at 10 \mu M which greatly exceeds the typical observed mean steady-state C_{max} for etrasimod (2 mg once daily) of 0.247 \mu M [113 ng/mL]. Hence, etrasimod is unlikely to potentially produce clinically meaningful inhibition of these UGTs. No clinical study has been conducted to assess the impact of etrasimod on UGTs.
L1581: ## CYP Enzyme Induction
L1582: - Etrasimod is not an inducer of human CYP1A2, CYP2B6, and CYP3A4 based on the fold-change method as mRNA evaluations (see comments below for details).
- In vitro study using human hepatocytes (No. XT163079) showed that etrasimod (up to 10 \mu M) caused no significant induction of CYP1A2 mRNA levels; however, in one of three preparations up to 3.73-fold and 8.94-fold increase was observed for CYP2B6 and CYP3A4 mRNA, respectively, but the increases were less than 11% and 10% as effective as the positive controls (phenobarbital for CYP2B6 and rifampin for CYP3A4).
- In addition, the Applicant also assessed the potential for induction by estimating drug concentration in the liver by using two methods per the Guidance:Method 1: I_h = f_{u,p} \times [C_{max} + (F_a \times F_g \times k_a \times Dose)/Q_h/R_b], where I_h is the hepatic drug concentration, f_{u,p} is the unbound fraction in plasma, C_{max} is the steady state total maximal concentration in plasma, F_a is the fraction absorbed, F_g is the intestinal availability, k_a is the absorption rate constant, Q_h is the hepatic blood flow rate, and R_b is the blood-to-plasma concentration ratio
- Method 2: I_{max,u} \times 30, where I_{max,u} is the maximal unbound plasma concentration of the interacting drug at steady state
- Based on the two methods above, following etrasimod 2 mg once daily, steady-state etrasimod liver concentration was estimated to be 6.8 nM (I_h) or 156 nM (I_{max,u} \times 30) while in vitro variable induction potential was shown at 1 \mu M in human hepatocytes: mRNA increase of 3.44-fold in one donor's samples while &lt; 2-fold in samples from two other donors.
- Taken together, the potential for etrasimod to induce CYP enzymes at clinically relevant concentrations is likely low.
L1583: ## **Is the Drug a Substrate and/or an Inhibitor of Transport Processes?**
L1584: - Study XT168106 was designed to evaluate etrasimod (as etrasimod L-arginine) as an inhibitor and a substrate of human transporters (i.e., P-gp, BCRP, BSEP, OATP1B1, OATP1B3, OAT1, OAT3, OCT1, OCT2, MATE1, and MATE2-K).
- Etrasimod (up to 10 \muM) inhibited less than 50% of activity towards BSEP, OATP1B3, OAT1, OAT3, OCT1, OCT2, MATE1, and MATE2-K but inhibited P-gp, BCRP, and OATP1B1 with IC_{50} values of ~100, 35.7, and 10 \muM, respectively (concentrations of 45,750, 16,333, and 4575 ng/mL respectively). The results are summarized in Table 82 and Table 83.
- The Applicant also conducted a definitive bidirectional Caco-2 study to confirm the membrane passive permeability of etrasimod and potential as a substrate for P-gp and BCRP (APD334.DMPK.007). The efflux ratio for etrasimod (0.5 and 5 \muM) was \le 2, indicating it is not a substrate for either P-gp or BCRP.
- Therefore, etrasimod is not a substrate or an inhibitor of human ABC transporters (P-gp, BCRP, and BSEP), SLC transporters (OATP1B1, OATP1B3, OAT1/3, OCT1, OCT2, and MATE1/2-K). Overall, etrasimod is not expected to have transporter mediated clinical DDIs.

L1593: ## Metabolite Profiling in Mass Balance Study
L1594: Following single-dose oral administration of [^{14}C]etrasimod in humans (human mass balance study, No. APD334-107), etrasimod was primarily eliminated through metabolism; unchanged etrasimod was not detected in urine. Metabolism of etrasimod occurred via oxidation, dehydrogenation, sulfation, and glucuronidation with 11 minor circulating metabolites identified in plasma. Oxidative metabolites, M3 and M6, were the 2 most abundant minor circulating metabolites, with each accounting for &lt; 10% of total radioactivity. M2 was not detected in human plasma. Etrasimod was the predominant and only major circulating compound detected in plasma, representing 49% of plasma total radioactivity.
L1595: ## **16.3.4. Pharmacogenomics Analysis**
L1596: No pharmacogenomics analysis was conducted. In vitro effect of recombinant CYP2C9 was studied with CYP2C9*1 enzyme variant.
L1597: ## **16.3.5. Individual Study Review**
L1598: ## **Hepatic Impairment Study APD334-108**
L1599: **Title:** An Open-label, Single-dose, Parallel-group Study to Assess the Pharmacokinetics of Etrasimod in Subjects with Mild, Moderate, and Severe Hepatic Impairment Compared to Healthy Matched-control Subjects
L1600: Clinical Site: American Research Corporation at the Texas Liver Institute, San Antonio, Texas
L1601: Study Date: 23 January 2019 to 29 August 2019
L1602: ## Study Design
L1603: This was an open-label, single-dose, parallel-group study to evaluate the PK as well as the safety and tolerability of etrasimod 2 mg administration in males and females with mild, moderate, or severe hepatic impairment (Child-Pugh A/B/C) as compared to matched-control males and females with normal hepatic function. Subjects were enrolled within the following groups based on their Child-Pugh score:
L1604: - Group 1 – Normal hepatic function
- Group 2 – Mild hepatic impairment (Child-Pugh Class A)
- Group 3 – Moderate hepatic impairment (Child-Pugh Class B)
- Group 4 – Severe hepatic impairment (Child-Pugh Class C)
L1605: Subjects received a single oral dose of 2 mg etrasimod following an overnight fast.
L1606: ## Pharmacokinetic Results
