L1657: ## Renal Impairment Study APD334-112
L1658: **Title:** A Phase 1, Open-Label, Single-Dose Study to Investigate the Effects of Renal Impairment on the Pharmacokinetics of Etrasimod
L1659: Clinical Site: Alliance for Multispecialty Research, Knoxville, TN; Orlando Clinical Research Center, Orlando, FL
L1660: Study Date: 22 December 2020 to 26 August 2021
L1661: ## Study Design
L1662: This was an open-label, single-dose study to evaluate the PK, safety, and tolerability of etrasimod in subjects with renal impairment.
L1663: This study was first conducted using a reduced design. Subjects with severe renal impairment and/or end-stage renal disease (ESRD) (estimated glomerular filtration rate [eGFR] \le 29 mL/min; either requiring hemodialysis or not; defined as Group C) and matched subjects with normal renal function were initially enrolled. The eGFR was determined by the Modification of Diet in Renal Disease equation. For subjects on dialysis, etrasimod dosing on Day 1 occurred immediately after the approximately 4-hour dialysis procedure completed.
L1664: Only if warranted, the study would proceed to the full design (additional evaluation of subjects with mild and moderate renal impairment, and their matched healthy controls) as described in Table 92 below.
L1665: **Table 92. Renal Impairment Study APD334-112 Full Design**
L1666: Reduced design:
Group C	Severe impairment and/or ESRD (eGFR < 29; either requiring hemodialysis or not)
Matched healthy controls	eGFR \ge 90; demographically matched by age (\pm 10 years), body mass index (BMI; \pm 20\%), sex, and smoking status to an enrolled renally impaired subject(s)
Full design if warranted:
Group A	Mild impairment (eGFR \ge 60 to \le 89)
Group B	Moderate impairment (eGFR \ge 30 to \le 59)
L1667: Of note, the results of the initial investigation did not warrant further investigation, so Group A (mild renal impairment), Group B (moderate renal impairment) and their matched subjects with normal renal function were not studied in this study.

L1680: ## Mass Balance Study APD334-107
L1681: **Title:** A Phase 1, Open-label Study to Evaluate the Absorption, Metabolism, and Excretion of [14C]Etrasimod (APD334) Following a Single Oral Dose in Healthy Male Subjects
L1682: Clinical Site: Covance Clinical Research Unit, Madison, Wisconsin
L1683: Study Date: 20 July 2018 to 12 September 2018
L1684: ## Study Design
L1685: This was an open-label, non-randomized, single-dose study of radiolabeled etrasimod in eight healthy male subjects. Subjects received a single oral dose of 2 mg [14C] etrasimod containing approximately 100 µCi radioactivity following an overnight fast.
L1686: Blood, urine, and fecal PK samples were collected for determination of total radioactivity concentrations (whole blood, plasma, urine, and feces), for determination of etrasimod concentrations (plasma only), and for metabolite profiling and characterization (plasma, urine, and feces). Beginning on Day 8, subjects were to be discharged if 1 of the following discharge criteria for total radioactivity was met:
L1687: - ≥ 90% of the administered radioactive dose was recovered in the excreta, or
- ≤ 1% of the radioactive dose was recovered in urine and feces for 3 consecutive 24-hour collection intervals where samples were provided.
L1688: If neither of these discharge criteria were met by the morning of Day 8, subjects were to remain in the CRU and blood, urine, and feces collections continued at 24-hour intervals until 1 discharge criterion was met or until Day 15.

L1704: ## Metabolite Profiling and Identification Results
L1705: Following oral administration of [^{14}C]etrasimod to healthy male subjects, samples of plasma, urine, and feces were analyzed and a total of 11, 21, and 9 metabolites were observed in plasma, urine, and feces, respectively; of these observed metabolites, 14 were identified.
L1706: An overall metabolic pathway for etrasimod in humans is presented in Section 6.2.1 above.
L1707: Metabolite profiling was assessed for the 0-to-312-hour cross-subject plasma pool, which represented approximately 92% of the mean AUC_{0-inf} for total radioactivity (Table 97). Etrasimod was identified as the only major radioactive component (49.28%) in the cross-subject extracted pooled plasma. The 2 most abundant metabolites were AR503641 (M3) and AR504344 (M6), with each representing &lt; 10% of total radioactivity, as shown in Table 97.

L1711: ## 16.3.6. Bioanalytical Method Report
L1712: The Applicant developed six LC-MS/MS (Table 98) and adequately validated. The validation reports were submitted for all methods. The method CA22262-01 was mainly used for etrasimod in most clinical trials and method CA27234-01 was used for metabolites M3 and M6. Five of the methods were developed and validated by [REDACTED](b) (4). One method was transferred, modified, and validated by [REDACTED](b) (4). A method for unbound etrasimod was developed and validated by [REDACTED](b) (4) and used for samples from hepatic and renal impairment studies. The Applicant cross-validated between the methods used by [REDACTED](b) (4). Etrasimod concentrations in human urine samples were measured by a LC-MS/MS method. A summary of the bioanalytical methods by study are shown in Table 98.

L1717: Bioanalytical methods validation performance of CA22262-01, CA27234-01, and CA25946-01 are briefly summarized in Table 99, Table 100, and Table 101. The cross-validation was summarized in Table 102.
L1718: **Table 99. Summary of Bioanalytical Method Using UPLC-MS for Etrasimod in Plasma (CA22262-01)**
L1719: Method description	The concentration of etrasimod (APD334) in human plasma (K_2EDTA) was determined using UPLC with mass spectrometric detection. In this method, an aliquot of human plasma (K_2EDTA) containing the etrasimod and IS was extracted using a liquid-liquid extraction procedure. The samples were analyzed by an UPLC equipped with an AB SCIEX API 4000TM triple quadrupole mass spectrometer using an APCI source. Positive ions were monitored in the MRM mode. Quantitation was determined using weighted linear regression analysis (1/\text{concentration}^2) of peak area ratios of the analyte and internal standard.
Materials used for standard calibration curve and concentration	APD334 L-arginine, 5015-10-02, 21 December 2017; off-white powder
Validated assay range	0.250 to 100 ng/mL
Materials used for quality control (QCs) and concentration	APD334 L-arginine, 5015-10-02, 21 December 2017; off-white powder
Minimum required dilutions (MRDs)	NA
Source and lot of reagents	All reagents used are listed in method validation report.
d5-APD334, S1048741, 24 February 2019
Regression model and weighting	Weighted linear regression analysis (1/\text{concentration}^2) of peak area ratios of the analyte and IS
Validation parameters	Method validation summary	Source location
Standard calibration curve performance during accuracy and precision runs	Number of standard calibrators	9	CA22262-01 Amendment 4 to Validation Final Report Table 5

L1725: Table 100. Summary of Bioanalytical Method for Metabolites M3 and M6 (CA27234-01)
L1726: Method description	The concentrations of etrasimod metabolites M3 (AR503641) and M6 (AR504344) in human plasma (K2EDTA) was determined using UPLC with mass spectrometric detection. In this method, an aliquot of human plasma (K2EDTA) containing the analytes and IS was extracted using a protein precipitation procedure. The samples were analyzed by UPLC equipped with an AB SCIEX Triple QuadTM 6500 triple quadrupole mass spectrometer using an ESI source. Positive ions were monitored in the MRM mode. Quantitation was determined using weighted linear regression analysis (1/concentration2) of peak area ratios of the analyte and internal standard.

L1745: ## 16.4. Additional Efficacy Information
L1746: ## 16.4.1. Multiple Testing Procedure
L1747: A prespecified gatekeeping multiple testing approach was implemented to control the overall Type I error rate at 2-sided alpha of 0.05 for all co-primary and multiplicity-controlled secondary endpoints for APD334-301 and APD334-302, as shown in Figure 29 and Figure 30, respectively. First, the whole alpha was spent on testing family 1 (F1) consisting of the co-primary endpoints. If both null hypotheses in F1 were rejected, the testing with the whole alpha proceeded to F2. Truncated Hochberg procedure with a truncation fraction 0.8 was implemented for testing hypotheses in F2. Since all null hypotheses in F2 were rejected, the whole alpha was spent on testing F3 based on a traditional Hochberg procedure. The Hochberg method may not control type I error when endpoints within a family are negatively correlated; however, given the efficacy results for these studies, the review team is not concerned with potential type I error inflation in this instance.
L1748: Figure 29. Gatekeeping Approach to Control the Type I Error Rate for APD334-301
L1749: F1, family 1; F2, family 2, F3, family 3
L1750: Source: Statistical Analysis Plan (Version 1.0) for APD334-301, Figure 2 (page 26)
L1751: Figure 30. Gatekeeping Approach to Control the Type I Error Rate for APD334-302
L1752: F1, family 1; F2, family 2, F3, family 3
L1753: Source: Statistical Analysis Plan (Version 1.0) for APD334-302, Figure 2 (page 22)

L1846: ## Executive Summary (Clinical Relevance)
L1847: As part of the NDA package, the Applicant submitted three in vitro pharmacology studies and a pre- and postnatal development (PPND) study in rats with etrasimod, as well as genetic toxicology studies with two process-related impurities. These studies have not previously been reviewed under any IND for etrasimod and are reviewed here.
L1848: Etrasimod is a sphingosine-1-phosphate receptor subtype 1,4, and 5 (S1P_{1,4,5}) agonist. Etrasimod inhibited generation of cAMP, rapidly induced S1P_1 internalization, and induced \beta-arrestin recruitment to hS1P_1 and hS1P_5 with similar potency and efficacy to other S1P receptor modulators (i.e., fingolimod, ozanimod, and siponimod). However, etrasimod was generally a less potent agonist of GTP\gammaS binding to S1P receptors, including S1P_1, compared to other S1P receptor modulators. Etrasimod also induced a smaller increase in the I_{KACH} channel current in vitro using isolated human atrial cardiomyocytes compared to ozanimod or fingolimod under the same assay conditions.
L1849: In the PPND study, the NOAEL for maternal toxicity was considered the low dose of 0.4 mg/kg/day (approximately 2 times exposure at the recommended clinical dose of 2 mg, based on AUC) due to observations of mortality during delivery, increased post-implantation loss, increased number of females with stillborn pups, increased number of stillborn pups per litter, decreased viability index, and decreased lactation index at higher doses. A developmental NOAEL could not be established based on the findings of increased perinatal mortalities (\ge 2 mg/kg/day), adverse clinical signs and decreased body weight/body weight gain in offspring during the preweaning period (all doses), as well as a reduced number of implantations, increased rate of pre-implantation loss, and corresponding decrease in the number of live embryos per litter observed (4 mg/kg/day); these effects occurred at exposures less than that expected at the clinical dose.

L1853: ## Pharmacology/ Toxicology Summary
L1854: ## Pharmacology
L1855: ## Key Findings:
L1856: Etrasimod is sphingosine-1-phosphate receptor subtype 1,4, and 5 (S1P_{1,4,5}) agonist. Etrasimod concentration-dependently inhibited generation of cAMP in vitro with similar efficacy and potency compared to the endogenous ligand, S1P, as well as other S1P receptor modulators (i.e., fingolimod phosphate, ozanimod, siponimod). Similar to other S1P receptor modulators but in contrast to S1P, etrasimod rapidly and concentration-dependently induced S1P_1 internalization in S1P_1-overexpressing CHO cells, with the maximum effect occurring by 2 hours and persisting through at least 6 hours post-treatment.
L1857: Etrasimod potently induced GTP\gammaS binding to human S1P_1 (hS1P_1) and hS1P_5. Etrasimod was also a partial agonist for GTP\gammaS binding at hS1P_3 but not hS1P_2 or hS1P_4. Etrasimod was generally a less potent agonist of GTP\gammaS binding to S1P receptors, including S1P_1, compared to other S1P receptor modulators.
L1858: Etrasimod potently induced \beta-arrestin recruitment to hS1P1 and hS1P5. Etrasimod was a partial agonist at hS1P4 but did not induce \beta-arrestin recruitment to hS1P2 or hS1P3. Compared to ozanimod and siponimod, etrasimod had similar efficacy and potency for \beta-arrestin recruitment to hS1P1. Etrasimod also had similar efficacy but lower potency at this receptor compared to fingolimod phosphate.
L1859: While etrasimod concentration-dependently induced increases in the IKACH channel current, the maximum increase of 39% at 1000 nM (4 times higher than the mean steady state Cmax in healthy volunteers following 21 days of dosing with etrasimod at 2 mg) was less than that of ozanimod or fingolimod under the same assay conditions.
L1860: ## Genotoxicity
L1861: ## *Key Findings:*
L1862: Bacterial reverse mutation assays were conducted with process-related impurities [REDACTED] (b) (4) was considered positive for mutagenicity, while [REDACTED] (b) (4) was considered negative for mutagenicity under the tested conditions.
L1863: [REDACTED] (b) (4) is a starting material used in the preparation of an [REDACTED] (b) (4) manufacturing product. As [REDACTED] (b) (4) is considered a potentially genotoxic impurity, it is controlled in the [REDACTED] (b) (4) manufacturing product at below [REDACTED] (b) (4) \mug/day ([REDACTED] (b) (4) ppm for the clinical dose of etrasimod of 2 mg/day, given a purge factor of at least [REDACTED] (b) (4)), in accordance with ICH M7 option 2.
L1864: ## Reproductive Toxicology
L1865: ## *Key Findings:*
L1866: Pregnant Sprague Dawley rats were administered vehicle or etrasimod at 0.4, 2, or 4 mg/kg/day from GD 6 through LD 20. There were no adverse effects on maternal clinical signs, or body weights at any dose of etrasimod, although decreased feed consumption was observed in all treated groups during gestation.
L1867: One female at 4 mg/kg/day was prematurely terminated on GD 22 due to severe adverse clinical signs during delivery. A significant increase in post-implantation loss was observed at 4 mg/kg/day and significantly increased gestation length was observed at 2 and 4 mg/kg/day compared to controls. A significant increase in the number of females with stillborn pups at 2 and 4 mg/kg/day and increased number of stillborn pups per litter at 2 mg/kg/day compared to controls were also observed. The viability index was decreased at 2 and 4 mg/kg/day while the lactation index was decreased at 4 mg/kg/day compared to controls.
L1868: Etrasimod-related clinical signs in F1 offspring included cold to touch, suspected dehydration (based on skin turgor), discolored skin, skin pallor, swelling, and thin appearance at 4 mg/kg/day and no milk band in the stomach at 2 and 4 mg/kg/day. Decreased maternal feed consumption during lactation corresponded to decreased pup body weights and body weight gain during the preweaning period at 2 and 4 mg/kg/day. No etrasimod-related macroscopic findings were observed in F1 offspring at the
L1869: preweaning terminal necropsy; however, drug-related findings in prematurely terminated or stillborn pups included full body edema at 4 mg/kg/day and no milk in the stomach at 2 and 4 mg/kg/day. No etrasimod-related findings, including behavioral assessments and sexual maturation, were observed in pups during the postweaning period, and the previously observed differences in body weights normalized during this time in all treated groups compared to controls.
L1870: There were no etrasimod-related effects on pre-coital intervals, mating index, or fertility index in the F1 generation. A reduced number of implantations and increased rate of pre-implantation loss were observed at 4 mg/kg/day compared to controls and correlated with a decreased number of live embryos per litter at 4 mg/kg/day compared to controls.
L1871: The NOAEL for maternal toxicity was considered the low dose of 0.4 mg/kg/day (approximately 2 times exposure at the recommended clinical dose of 2 mg, based on AUC), due to observations of mortality during delivery, increased post-implantation loss, increased number of females with stillborn pups, increased number of stillborn pups per litter, decreased viability index, and decreased lactation index at higher doses. Based on increased perinatal mortalities at \ge 2 mg/kg/day, adverse clinical signs and decreased body weight/body weight gain in offspring prior to weaning at all doses, as well as the reduced number of implantations, increased rate of pre-implantation loss, and corresponding decrease in the number of live embryos per litter in F1 animals observed at 4 mg/kg/day during the postweaning period, a developmental NOAEL could not be determined.
L1872: ## 1 Background
L1873: ## 1.1 Regulatory History
L1874: The Applicant, Pfizer, Inc. (Arena Pharmaceuticals was acquired by Pfizer Inc. in 2022), submitted a Type B pre-IND meeting request on January 29, 2015 (IND 125154, SDN 2). No nonclinical questions were included in this meeting; however, preliminary comments from the Agency included recommendations to conduct a study in an animal model of inflammatory bowel disease (IBD) to show the potential effectiveness of etrasimod in the intended study population and to conduct a fertility and early embryonic development (FEED) study prior to the initiation of the proposed phase 2 clinical trial.1 The Applicant addressed these comments in a subsequent submission (SDN 6; May 4, 2015). The Applicant stated that a study in an animal model of IBD would be conducted concurrently with clinical development. Additionally, the Applicant referenced previously submitted 3-month toxicology studies in rats and dogs (b) (4) and indicated that these data would be supplemented with a study of sperm morphology and motility to adequately address all FEED study endpoints prior to the initiation of the phase 2 clinical study. The final report of a 28-day study of the effects of etrasimod on spermatogenesis in male rats was submitted (b) (4).
L1875: The Applicant submitted IND 125154 to the Division of Gastroenterology and Inborn Errors Products (DGIEP, now the Division of Gastroenterology [DG]) on May 4, 2015 (SDN 5) to develop etrasimod as a treatment for subjects with moderately to severely active ulcerative colitis. No nonclinical studies were submitted to IND 125154 (b) (4).
L1876: On June 4, 2015, no safety concerns were identified with the proposed phase 2 clinical protocol in subjects with ulcerative colitis under IND 125154 (Nonclinical Review; Dr. Sruthi King).
L1877: Most nonclinical studies submitted (b) (4) were reviewed by Dr. John Dougherty under IND 142182.3
L1878: A Type B, End of Phase 2 (EOP2) meeting was held between the Applicant and the Agency on October 30, 2018. During this meeting, the Agency noted that the completed, ongoing, and planned nonclinical studies appeared adequate to support the proposed phase 3 clinical; however, a pre- and postnatal development study in rats
L1879: 1 IND 125154: Type B PIND Meeting – Preliminary Comments. K. Richards (DGIEP); March 20, 2015.
IND 125154: Type B PIND Meeting – Final Meeting Minutes. K. Richards (DGIEP); April 2, 2015. (b) (4)
L1880: 3 IND 142182: 30-Day IND Full Review. J. Dougherty (DDDP); June 10, 2019.
L1881: should be conducted prior to the NDA submission. Final meeting minutes were issued following the meeting.4
L1882: NDA 216956 for etrasimod for the treatment of moderately to severely active ulcerative colitis was submitted to DG on October 14, 2022 (SDN 1). The purpose of the current review is to review studies submitted to the NDA that were not previously submitted to any IND for etrasimod.

L1899: ## 4 Pharmacology
L1900: ## 4.1 Primary Pharmacology
L1901: ## Activity of Etrasimod and Other S1P Modulators in S1P1 Cyclic AMP and Internalization Assays in Recombinant CHO Cells
L1902: APD334.TS.012
L1903: The ability of etrasimod and other sphingosine 1-phosphate (S1P) receptor modulators, fingolimod phosphate, ozanimod, and siponimod, to induce S1P1 receptor internalization and inhibit cyclic adenosine monophosphate (cAMP) generation was evaluated using two Chinese hamster ovary (CHO) cell lines engineered to express human S1P1 (one high-expressing line, CHO-21BWc1, and one moderate-expressing line, CHO-21BWc2) and primary human umbilical vein endothelial cells (HUVEC), which endogenously express S1P1.
L1904: Etrasimod concentration-dependently inhibited cAMP generation in both CHO-21BWc1 and CHO-21BWc2 cells, with IC50 values of 453.9 and 464.5 pM, respectively, and 103% efficacy relative to that of the endogenous ligand S1P in both cell lines. These findings were similar to those observed with fingolimod phosphate (IC50 = 7.2 and 7.9 pM, 101% and 103% efficacy, respectively), ozanimod (IC50 = 29.5 and 38.9 pM, 104% and 100% efficacy), siponimod (IC50 = 28.2 and 27.5 pM, 101% and 103% efficacy), and S1P itself (IC50 = 28.2 and 27.5 pM).

L1913: In HUVEC, etrasimod concentration-dependently inhibited cAMP generation with an IC50 of 30.5 nM and an efficacy of 101% relative to S1P. These findings were similar to those observed with fingolimod phosphate (IC50 = 1.6 nM, 94% efficacy), ozanimod (IC50 = 2.0 nM, 102% efficacy), siponimod (IC50 = 1.6 nM, 101% efficacy), and S1P itself (IC50 = 19.1 nM).

L1918: Following incubation of hS1P1-expressing CHO cells with 100 nM of the various S1P receptor modulators, all compounds induced rapid internalization in both CHO-21BWc1 and CHO-21BWc2 cells that reached a maximum level within 2 hours and persisted through the end of the assay (6 hours). In contrast, S1P induced minimal internalization, and surface expression of S1P1 returned to baseline levels within approximately 3 hours.
L1919: **Figure 1. Time Course of S1P1 Receptor Internalization in Recombinant CHO Cells**
L1920: APD334: etrasimod; FTY720p: fingolimod phosphate.
L1921: *Figure adapted from study report (Study APD334-TS-012).*
L1922: As S1P at 100 nM induced minimal and more transient internalization in the previous assay, internalization of S1P1 with S1P receptor modulators at 10x the concentration (1 µM) was quantified in the recombinant CHO cell lines with an incubation time of 2 hours. Fingolimod phosphate was used as the control to determine the relative efficacy of the other compounds.
L1923: Etrasimod concentration-dependently induced S1P1 internalization in both CHO-21BWc1 and CHO-21BWc2 cells, with IC50 values of 8.9 and 8.3 nM, respectively. The efficacy of etrasimod for internalization relative to fingolimod phosphate was 90% and 99% in the two cell lines, respectively. These findings were similar to those observed with ozanimod (IC50 = 4.2 and 3.5 nM, 103% and 111% efficacy, respectively), siponimod (IC50 = 4.2 and 1.8 nM, 107% and 103% efficacy) and fingolimod phosphate (IC50 = 3.7 and 1.4 nM). Under the tested conditions, S1P dose-dependently induced internalization with IC50 values of 5211.9 and 608.1 nM and efficacy of 103% and 94%, respectively, relative to fingolimod phosphate.

L1934: ## Pharmacological Profiling of Etrasimod, Its M3 and M6 Metabolites, and Other S1P Receptor Modulators in \beta-Arrestin and GTP\gammaS Assays for S1P1-5 ReceptorsAPD334.TS.020
L1935: The ability of etrasimod, its M3 and M6 metabolites, and other S1P receptor modulators ozanimod, ozanimod ketone metabolite, fingolimod phosphate, and siponimod, to induce the binding of guanosine 5'-O-(\gamma-thio)-triphosphate (GTP\gammaS) and recruit \beta-arrestin to human S1P1-5 were evaluated using in vitro assays. GTP\gammaS binding was evaluated in S1P receptor-expressing CHO-K1 cells using radiolabeled GTP. \beta-arrestin recruitment was evaluated using the relevant DiscoverX assay (a modified chemiluminescent assay using HEK cells). Efficacy values for experiments with hS1P1,2,3,5 were normalized to the endogenous ligand, S1P, while experiments with hS1P4 were normalized to amiselimod phosphate.
L1936: ## GTP\gammaS binding
L1937: Etrasimod potently induced GTP\gammaS binding to hS1P1 and hS1P5 (EC50s = 57 nM and 63.5 nM, respectively, and efficacy 99% and 72% of S1P, respectively). Etrasimod was also a partial agonist at hS1P3 (EC50 = 6050 nM, efficacy 42%); however, the clinical relevance of this effect is expected to be limited, as the EC50 of 6050 nM is approximately 25 times higher than the mean steady state Cmax in healthy volunteers following 21 days of dosing with etrasimod at 2 mg (113 ng/mL or 247 nM; Study APD334-002). Etrasimod did not induce GTP\gammaS binding to hS1P2 or hS1P4.
L1938: Etrasimod metabolite M3 had similar efficacy with approximately 10-fold lower potency for GTP\gammaS binding at hS1P1 and hS1P5 as well as similar efficacy and potency at hS1P3
L1939: compared to the parent drug. Metabolite M6 had similar efficacy with approximately 3-fold lower potency at hS1P1 as well as similar efficacy and potency at hS1P3 and hS1P5 compared to the parent drug.
L1940: At hS1P1, etrasimod had similar efficacy but 15- to 48-fold lower potency compared to ozanimod, fingolimod phosphate, and siponimod. At hS1P5, etrasimod had similar efficacy but 577-fold lower potency compared to fingolimod phosphate but lower efficacy and 52- to 1270-fold lower potency compared to ozanimod and siponimod. Overall, etrasimod is a less potent agonist of GTPyS binding to S1P receptors, including S1P1, compared to other S1P receptor modulators. This finding may have clinical relevance, as GTPyS binding to S1P1 has been proposed to contribute to the transient heart rate reduction observed after the first dose of other S1P receptor modulators.5,6,7

L1964: ## \beta-arrestin recruitment
L1965: Etrasimod potently induced \beta-arrestin recruitment to hS1P_1 and hS1P_5 (EC_{50s} = 44 nM and 63.4 nM, respectively, and efficacy 120% and 86% of S1P, respectively). Etrasimod was a partial agonist of \beta-arrestin recruitment at hS1P_4 (EC_{50} = 141 nM, efficacy 54% of amiselimod phosphate); the physiological or clinical relevance of this finding is unknown. Etrasimod did not induce \beta-arrestin recruitment to hS1P_2 or hS1P_3.
L1966: Etrasimod metabolites M3 and M6 had similar efficacy at hS1P1 and hS1P5 but approximately 3-fold (M6) or 10-fold (M3) lower potency compared to the parent drug. M6 was also a partial agonist at hS1P4, with similar efficacy and approximately 10-fold lower potency compared to the parent drug.
L1967: Compared to ozanimod and siponimod, etrasimod had similar efficacy and potency (EC50) in the \beta-arrestin recruitment assay at hS1P1. Etrasimod also had similar efficacy but approximately 4-fold lower potency at this receptor compared to fingolimod phosphate. Notably, the various S1P receptor modulators differed in efficacy and potency at hS1P4 and hS1P5 (see tables below). Etrasimod consistently recruited \beta-arrestin more potently in this assay compared to other S1P receptor modulators, though with a lower efficacy.

L1980: ## 4.2 Secondary Pharmacology
L1981: ## Analysis of Effects of AR401959, Fingolimod and Ozanimod on GIRK (IKACh currents) in voltage clamp studies in human atrial myocytes
L1982: ## APD334.TS.011
L1983: The ability of etrasimod (named AR401959 in this report), ozanimod, and fingolimod to affect IKACH channel current was evaluated using patch clamping of isolated human atrial cardiomyocytes (n = 6 donors). Compounds were tested over the range of 0.1-1000 nM. Changes in the IKACH channel current were normalized the increased observed with 10 \muM carbachol.
L1984: Overall, etrasimod increased IKACH channel current in human atrial cardiomyocytes less than ozanimod and fingolimod. Etrasimod concentration-dependently induced increases in the IKACH channel current, with a maximum increase of 39% at 1000 nM, (4 times higher than the mean steady state Cmax in healthy volunteers following 21 days of dosing with etrasimod at 2 mg: 113 ng/mL or 247 nM; Study APD334-002). Ozanimod and fingolimod also concentration-dependently induced increases in the IKACH channel current, with maximum increases of 70.4% and 53.3%, respectively, at 1000 nM.
L1985: **Figure 2. Effects of S1P Receptor Modulators on I_{KACH} Channel Current in Human Atrial Cardiomyocytes**
L1986: AR401959: etrasimod.
L1987: Figure copied directly from study report (Study APD334-TS-011).

L1997: ## Results
L1998: Positive mutagenic responses (maximum increases (b) (4)-fold control) were observed with (b) (4) in strains TA98 and WP2 *uvrA* in the presence and absence of S9 activation and with TA1537 in the presence of S9 activation.
L1999: Equivocal responses (maximum increases (b) (4)-fold control, respectively) were observed with TA100 in the presence of S9 activation and TA1537 in the absence of S9 activation. Although the increases in the number of revertant colonies for these conditions were outside of the respective historical control limits, they did not completely meet all protocol acceptance criteria for evaluation as positive (i.e., not concentration-dependent and/or not meeting the prespecified threshold).
L2000: Based on these findings, (b) (4) was considered positive for mutagenicity.

L2017: ## 9 Reproductive and Developmental Toxicology
L2018: ## 9.3 Prenatal and Postnatal Development (PPND)*
L2019: ## 9.3.1 A Dose Range-Finding Prenatal/Postnatal Development Study of APD334 Administered by Oral Gavage to Sprague Dawley Rats
L2020: Study no.: APD334.TOX.012
Study report location: EDR 4 2 3 5 4
Conducting laboratory and location: (b) (4)
L2021: GLP compliance: No
Drug, lot #, and % purity: Etrasimod (APD334)
Lot # 18-534-003
Purity 99.83%
Enhanced PPND Study: N
L2022: ## Methods
L2023: Doses: 0.05, 0.2, 1, 2 mg/kg/day
Frequency of dosing: Once daily
Number/Sex/Group: 8/group (females only)
Dose volume: 5 mL/kg
Formulation/Vehicle: L-arginine monohydrochloride at 0.189 mg/mL in 0.5% (v/v) Tween 20, 10 mM Tris, pH 8.5-9.0
Route of administration: ORAL GAVAGE
Species: Rat
Strain: Sprague Dawley
Comment on Study Design and Conduct: Pregnant females were dosed from gestation day (GD) 6 through lactation day (LD) 20. Offspring were not directly dosed.
Dosing Solution Analysis: Acceptable (\pm (b) (4)% of nominal)
L2024: ## Summary of Findings
L2025: In this preliminary dose range-finding study, pregnant Sprague Dawley rats were administered vehicle or etrasimod (named APD334 in this report) at 0.05, 0.2, 1, or 2 mg/kg/day from gestation day (GD) 6 through lactation day (LD) 20. There were no maternal deaths, clinical signs of toxicity, or drug-related effects on body weight/body weight gain, feed consumption, or macroscopic examinations during gestation or lactation. No effects of etrasimod administration were observed on parturition. An increased number of stillborn pups and decreased viability index were observed at \ge 1 mg/kg/day compared to controls. Lower pup body weights were observed at \ge 0.2 mg/kg/day compared to controls on PNDs 20 and 21, but these effects were not dose-dependent. Based on these findings, a high dose of at least 2 mg/kg/day was considered appropriate for use in the pivotal PPND study.

L2037: ## **Summary of Findings**
L2038: Pregnant Sprague Dawley rats were administered vehicle or etrasimod (named APD334 in this report) at 0.4, 2, or 4 mg/kg/day from GD 6 through LD 20. There were no adverse effects on maternal clinical signs, or body weights at any dose of etrasimod, although decreased feed consumption was observed in all treated groups during gestation.
L2039: The number of pregnant females was similar in all groups. One female at 4 mg/kg/day was prematurely terminated on GD 22 due to severe, adverse clinical signs during delivery. A significant increase in post-implantation loss was observed at 4 mg/kg/day. There was also a significant increase in the number of females with stillborn pups at 2 and 4 mg/kg/day and increased number of stillborn pups per litter at 2 mg/kg/day compared to controls. The viability index was decreased at 2 and 4 mg/kg/day while the lactation index was decreased at 4 mg/kg/day compared to controls.
L2040: An increased number of F1 offspring were found dead or found missing and presumed to be cannibalized, predominantly between PND 0-4, in the 2 and 4 mg/kg/day groups compared to controls. Etrasimod-related clinical signs in F1 offspring from birth through PND 4 included cold to touch, suspected dehydration (based on skin turgor), discolored skin, skin pallor, and swelling at 4 mg/kg/day and no milk band in the stomach at 2 and 4 mg/kg/day. After litters were culled on PND 4, clinical signs related to etrasimod administration at 4 mg/kg/day included suspected dehydration (based on skin turgor) and thin appearance. Decreased maternal feed consumption during lactation corresponded to decreased pup body weights and body weight gain during the preweaning period at 2 and 4 mg/kg/day. Pup body weights at the end of the preweaning period (PND 21) were 10%, 12%, and 20% lower than controls at 0.4, 2, and 4 mg/kg/day, respectively, while total body weight gains were 11%, 14%, and 23% lower than controls, respectively. No etrasimod-related macroscopic findings were
L2041: observed in F1 offspring at the preweaning terminal necropsy; however, drug-related findings in prematurely terminated or stillborn pups included full body edema at 4 mg/kg/day and no milk in the stomach at 2 and 4 mg/kg/day. No etrasimod-related findings were observed in the F1 generation during the postweaning period, including behavioral assessments and sexual maturation, and the previously observed differences in body weights normalized during this time in all treated groups compared to controls.
L2042: There were no etrasimod-related effects on pre-coital intervals, mating index, or fertility index in the F1 generation. A reduced number of implantations and increased rate of pre-implantation loss were observed at 4 mg/kg/day compared to controls. These findings correlated with a decrease in the number of live embryos per litter at 4 mg/kg/day compared to controls.
L2043: Based on the premature termination of one female during delivery (4 mg/kg/day), increased post-implantation loss (4 mg/kg/day), increased number of females with stillborn pups (2 and 4 mg/kg/day), increased number of stillborn pups per litter (2 mg/kg/day), decreased viability index (2 and 4 mg/kg/day), and decreased lactation index (4 mg/kg/day), the NOAEL for maternal toxicity was considered the low dose of 0.4 mg/kg/day (approximately 2 times exposure at the recommended clinical dose of 2 mg, based on AUC). Based on the increased perinatal mortalities at \ge 2 mg/kg/day, adverse clinical signs, and findings of decreased body weight/body weight gain at all doses during the preweaning period as well as a reduced number of implantations, increased rate of pre-implantation loss, and corresponding decrease in the number of live embryos per litter observed at 4 mg/kg/day, a developmental NOAEL could not be determined.
L2044: ## Observations and Results
L2045: ## F0 Dams
L2046: ## Mortality
L2047: One control female and three females at 4 mg/kg/day were prematurely terminated during the study.
L2048: The control female and two females at 4 mg/kg/day were prematurely terminated on lactation day (LD) 1 due to a lack of surviving pups. No clinical signs of toxicity or adverse changes in body weight or feed consumption were observed in these animals prior to termination. Upon necropsy, one of the 4 mg/kg/day females was observed to have pup tissue in the stomach.
L2049: A third female at 4 mg/kg/day was prematurely terminated on GD 22 due to severe, adverse clinical signs during delivery. No clinical signs of toxicity were observed during the gestation period. However, this animal lost 25 g (7% of body weight) from GD 18-22. There were no macroscopic abnormalities observed during necropsy. This rat was pregnant and had a litter consisting of 14 dead fetuses.
L2050: All other F0 animals survived to scheduled necropsy.
L2051: ## **Clinical Signs**
L2052: No clinical signs related to etrasimod administration were observed.
L2053: ## **Body Weight**
L2054: Although isolated differences in body weight and/or body weight gain were observed between treated and untreated groups (or in individual animals), no clear effects of etrasimod on these parameters were observed during gestation or lactation.
L2055: ## **Feed Consumption**
L2056: Decreased feed consumption was observed in all treated groups during gestation and lactation.
L2057: During gestation, significantly decreased feed consumption exceeding 10% below controls was observed at 2 and 4 mg/kg/day from GD 6-9. Significantly decreased feed consumption (less than 10% different from controls) was also observed at 4 mg/kg/day from GD 12-15. These differences resulted in an overall reduction in feed consumption over the gestation period (GD 6-18) of -6.6% at 2 mg/kg/day and -8.5% at 4 mg/kg/day compared to controls. Decreased feed consumption did not correlate with decreased body weight or decreased body weight gain during any interval in any group.
L2058: During the lactation period, significantly decreased feed consumption was consistently observed at 4 mg/kg/day, such that overall feed consumption during this period was 27% lower than the control group. Feed consumption was also decreased by approximately 10% at 2 mg/kg/day during the periods of PND 1-4 and PND 17-21 compared to controls. Decreased feed consumption did not correlate with decreased body weight or decreased body weight gain during any interval of the lactation period in any treated group.

L2064: ## Maternal Performance
L2065: The number of pregnant females was similar in all groups (22, 21, 22, and 22 in the control, low, mid, and high dose groups, respectively). One female at 4 mg/kg/day was prematurely terminated on GD 22 due to severe, adverse clinical signs during delivery.
L2066: A significant increase in post-implantation loss was observed at 4 mg/kg/day compared to controls (26.15% versus 12.29%).
L2067: Although gestation length significantly increased at 2 and 4 mg/kg/day compared to controls (21.9 and 21.8 days, respectively, versus 21.4 days), these values were all within the historical control range (mean: 21.9 days, range: 21.3-22.7 days). There was also a significant increase in the number of females at 2 and 4 mg/kg/day with stillborn pups (seven and four, respectively, compared to zero in the control group). This corresponded with a significant increase in the number of stillborn pups per litter at 2 mg/kg/day (3.11) but not at 4 mg/kg/day (2.24) compared to controls (0); the number of stillborn pups at both 2 and 4 mg/kg/day exceeded the historical control range (0.4-1.7). The viability index (percentage of liveborn pups that survived to PND 4) was decreased at 2 mg/kg/day (87.36%) and significantly decreased at 4 mg/kg/day (42.84%) compared to controls (92.45%). Lactation index (percentage of liveborn pups that survived to PND 21) was decreased at 4 mg/kg/day compared to controls (88.95% versus 99.40%).
L2068: Although not significant, the percentage of live male pups was lower at 4 mg/kg/day compared to controls (44.71% versus 51.30%).
L2069: No effects of etrasimod were observed on maternal-litter interactions (e.g., pup grooming, nesting, nursing).
L2070: No etrasimod-related macroscopic abnormalities were observed in F0 females following necropsy.

L2075: ## **Toxicokinetics**
L2076: Following the first dose of etrasimod on GD 6, plasma concentrations for all post-dose samples (i.e., 1, 4, and 24 hours post-dose) were below the lower limit of quantification (&lt; 5.00 ng/mL) for all control animals as well as for one animal at 0.4 mg/kg/day and one animal at 4 mg/kg/day. All other treated animals had measurable concentrations of drug. There were no deviations or documented events noted in the dosing records; thus, no explanation was provided for the observed lack of drug exposure in these animals. Toxicokinetic analyses were reported both with and without the apparently anomalous data from these animals, but only the analysis excluding the anomalous values is presented below. No unexplained abnormalities were observed in the toxicokinetic data collected on GD 17 or PND 20.
L2077: Following a single dose (GD 6), C_{max} and AUC of etrasimod increased approximately proportionally with increasing dose. T_{max} of etrasimod occurred between 1-4 hours and tended to occur later with increasing dose.
L2078: On GD 17, C_{max} and AUC of etrasimod increased approximately proportionally with increasing dose. T_{max} of etrasimod occurred at 4 hours for all dose levels. No significant accumulation of etrasimod was observed between GD 6 and 20.
L2079: On PND 20, C_{max} and AUC of etrasimod increased approximately proportionally with increasing dose. T_{max} of etrasimod occurred between 1-2 hours with no clear effect of dose. A slight loss of exposure to etrasimod was observed at 0.4 and 2 mg/kg/day (PND 20 AUC values 64% and 69% those observed on GD 6, respectively) but not at 4 mg/kg/day.

L2084: ## F1 Generation
L2085: ## Preweaning
L2086: An increased number of mortalities was observed in pups from the 2 and 4 mg/kg/day groups compared to controls, either found dead or missing/cannibalized. Specifically, 12 and 27 pups were found dead at 2 and 4 mg/kg/day, respectively, compared to 3 pups in the control group (5.3% and 15% of liveborn pups, respectively, versus 1.4% of liveborn control pups). Most pups were found dead between PND 0-4. Additionally, 18 and 60 pups were found missing and presumed cannibalized at 2 and 4 mg/kg/day, respectively, compared to 6 pups in the control group (7.9% and 33% of liveborn pups, respectively, versus 2.8% of liveborn control pups). Most presumptive cannibalizations occurred between PND 0-4. The mortality rates at 2 and 4 mg/kg/day greatly exceed the historical control rates (maximum 1.3% of liveborn pups through PND 21 in studies with culling).

L2112: ## Postweaning
L2113: No etrasimod-related mortality or clinical signs of toxicity were observed in F1 offspring during the postweaning period.
L2114: In males, though the observed decreased body weight during the preweaning period resolved during the postweaning period in all treated groups, reduced body weight gain (&gt; 10% difference) was observed at 4 mg/kg/day compared to controls between PND 22-57; these findings correlated with reduced feed consumption during this period (-10% to -15% compared to controls). However, body weight gains were similar after this point. Final body weights were not significantly different between treated and control animals (-0.3%, -4.6%, and -7.6% in the low, mid, and high dose groups, respectively, compared to controls).
L2115: **Figure 3. Body Weights of F1 Offspring Postweaning – Males**
L2116: Figure copied directly from study report (APD334-TOX-011).
L2117: In females, though the observed decreased body weight during the preweaning period resolved during the postweaning period in all treated groups, reduced body weight gain (&gt; 10\% difference) was observed at 4 mg/kg/day compared to controls between PND 22-29 with no corresponding difference in feed consumption. However, body weight gains were similar after this point. Final body weights were not significantly different between treated and control animals (-3.5\%, -2.8\%, and -3.6\% in the low, mid, and high dose groups, respectively, compared to controls).
L2118: **Figure 4. Body Weights of F1 Offspring Postweaning – Females**

L2120: No effects of etrasimod were observed on vaginal patency in females. In male offspring at 2 and 4 mg/kg/day, preputial separation occurred significantly later than controls (46.0 and 49.2 days, respectively, compared to 44.4 days), and the number of days at 4 mg/kg/day exceeded the historical control range of 40.6-46.7 days. These findings were considered a consequence of the reduced pup body weight in these groups and not a direct effect of etrasimod administration.

L2125: ## Reproductive Performance
L2126: There were no etrasimod-related effects on pre-coital intervals, mating index, or fertility index. Pregnancy was confirmed in 16, 17, 19, and 14 F1 females in the control, 0.4, 2, and 4 mg/kg/day groups, respectively. All values for mating, fertility, and pregnancy index were within the historical controls range.
L2127: In pregnant F1 females, no differences in body weight, body weight gain, or feed consumption were observed over the period GD 0-13.
L2128: The number of implantations was highly variable in all groups, including controls, and ranged from 3-21 per animal. The mean number of implantations was reduced at 4 mg/kg/day compared to controls (11.1 versus 14.8). Pre-implantation loss was also increased at 4 mg/kg/day compared to controls (23.76% versus 3.56%). The number of implantations and pre-implantation loss at 4 mg/kg/day were outside the respective historical control ranges. The decrease in implantations correlated with a decrease in the number of live embryos per litter at 4 mg/kg/day compared to controls (10.5 versus 14.3). No effects of etrasimod were observed at 0.4 or 2 mg/kg/day.

L2133: ## Toxicokinetics
L2134: A full toxicokinetic analysis was not performed in F1 offspring.
L2135: On PND 4, the plasma concentration of etrasimod increased approximately proportionally with dose in male offspring at all doses and in females at 0.4 and 4 mg/kg/day but not 2 mg/kg/day. The pup to maternal concentration ratio in male pups decreased with increasing dose, while no clear effect of dose was observed on the pup to maternal concentration ratio in female pups. Plasma concentrations were consistently higher in dams compared to pups.

L2157: ## Executive Summary (Clinical Relevance)
L2158: To support clinical development of etrasimod in subjects with ulcerative colitis, the Applicant submitted a nonclinical study with etrasimod using the CD4+CD45RBhigh T cell transfer mouse model of ulcerative colitis. In this model, etrasimod ameliorated the effects of colitis induction including body weight loss, increased colon weight to length ratio, increased mucosal thickness, increased histopathology score, increased expression of immune cell markers, and increased expression of pro-inflammatory cytokines. The observed effects of etrasimod in this model were not dose-dependent, but generally occurred at \ge 3 mg/kg/day (approximately 2.4-fold the clinical dose of 2 mg/day, based on HED).

L2164: ## *Key Findings:*
L2165: The pharmacodynamic effects of etrasimod were investigated in the CD4+CD45RBhigh T cell transfer mouse model of ulcerative colitis. Etrasimod ameliorated the effects of colitis induction including body weight loss, increased colon weight to length ratio, increased mucosal thickness, increased histopathology score, increased expression of immune cell markers, and increased expression of pro-inflammatory cytokines. The observed effects of etrasimod in this model were not dose-dependent, but generally occurred at \ge 3 mg/kg/day.
L2166: ## 1 Background
L2167: ## 1.1 Regulatory History
L2168: The Applicant, Pfizer, Inc. (Arena Pharmaceuticals was acquired by Pfizer Inc. in 2022), submitted a Type B pre-IND meeting request on January 29, 2015 (IND 125154, SDN 2). No nonclinical questions were included in this meeting; however, preliminary comments from the Agency included recommendations to conduct a study in an animal model of inflammatory bowel disease (IBD) to show the potential effectiveness of etrasimod in the intended study population and to conduct a fertility and early embryonic development (FEED) study prior to the initiation of the proposed phase 2 clinical trial.1 The Applicant addressed these comments in a subsequent submission (SDN 6; May 4, 2015). The Applicant stated that a study in an animal model of IBD would be conducted concurrently with clinical development. Additionally, the Applicant referenced previously submitted 3-month toxicology studies in rats and dogs (b) (4) and indicated that these data would be supplemented with a study of sperm morphology and motility to adequately address all FEED study endpoints prior to the initiation of the phase 2 clinical study. The final report of a 28-day study of the effects of etrasimod on spermatogenesis in male rats was submitted (b) (4)
L2169: The Applicant submitted IND 125154 to the Division of Gastroenterology and Inborn Errors Products (DGIEP, now the Division of Gastroenterology [DG]) on May 4, 2015 (SDN 5) to develop etrasimod as a treatment for subjects with moderately to severely active ulcerative colitis. No nonclinical studies were submitted to IND 125154. (b) (4)
L2170: On June 4, 2015, no safety concerns were identified with the proposed phase 2 clinical protocol in subjects with ulcerative colitis under IND 125154 (Nonclinical Review; Dr. Sruthi King).
L2171: Most nonclinical studies submitted (b) (4) were reviewed by Dr. John Dougherty under IND 142182.3
L2172: A Type B, End of Phase 2 (EOP2) meeting was held between the Applicant and the Agency on October 30, 2018. During this meeting, the Agency noted that the completed, ongoing, and planned nonclinical studies appeared adequate to support the proposed phase 3 clinical; however, a pre- and postnatal development study in rats should be conducted prior to the NDA submission. Final meeting minutes were issued following the meeting.4
L2173: 1 IND 125154: Type B PIND Meeting – Preliminary Comments. K. Richards (DGIEP); March 20, 2015.
IND 125154: Type B PIND Meeting – Final Meeting Minutes. K. Richards (DGIEP); April 2, 2015. (b) (4)
L2174: 3 IND 142182: 30-Day IND Full Review. J. Dougherty (DDDP); June 10, 2019.
L2175: 4 IND 125154: End of Phase 2 Final Meeting Minutes. K. Richards (DGIEP); October 30, 2018.
L2176: NDA 216956 for etrasimod for the treatment of moderately to severely active ulcerative colitis was submitted to DG on October 14, 2022 (SDN 1). The purpose of the current review is to review a nonclinical pharmacology study submitted to IND for etrasimod that is relevant to the NDA but was not previously reviewed.

L2185: ## 4 Pharmacology
L2186: ## 4.1 Primary Pharmacology
L2187: ## Effect of Treatment with 1 &amp; 3 mg/kg APD334 on Experimental Colitis in Mice
## APD334.TS.001
L2188: The pharmacodynamic effects of etrasimod (named APD334 in this report) were investigated in a mouse model of ulcerative colitis. In this model, CD4+CD45RBhigh T cells are isolated from wild type mice and transferred to SCID mice ("naïve") wherein they induce colon inflammation and pathology reminiscent of human ulcerative colitis (e.g., loose, mucinous stool, body weight loss). These mice were administered etrasimod at 1, 3, or 10 mg/kg/day from the day of T cell transfer (day 1) through day 32.
L2189: While healthy mice (labeled "unsorted/water" in the figures below) gained weight during the course of the study, vehicle-treated colitis mice ("water") lost body weight during the course of the study, particularly after day 20. Etrasimod significantly inhibited colitis-associated body weight loss at 3 and 10 mg/kg/day compared to vehicle, though the effects were independent of dose. Fingolimod (named FTY720 in this report) also significantly inhibited colitis-associated body weight compared to vehicle.
L2190: **Figure 1. Effects of Etrasimod on Body Weight Change in Colitis Mice**
L2191: * p &lt; 0.05, *** p &lt; 0.001 compared to vehicle.
L2192: APD334: etrasimod; FTY720: fingolimod.
L2193: Figure copied directly from study report (Study APD334-TS-001).
L2194: The induction of colitis resulted in increased colon weight to length ratio compared to healthy animals ("naïve" or "unsorted/H2O"). Etrasimod significantly reduced the colitis-associated increase in colon weight to length ratio at all tested doses compared to vehicle ("sorted/water"). Fingolimod also significantly reduced the colitis-associated increase in colon weight to length ratio compared to vehicle.
L2195: **Figure 2. Effects of Etrasimod on Colon Weight to Length Ratio in Colitis Mice**
L2196: * p &lt; 0.05, ** p &lt; 0.01, *** p &lt; 0.001, **** p &lt; 0.0001 compared to vehicle.
L2197: APD334: etrasimod; FTY720: fingolimod.
L2198: *Figure copied directly from study report (Study APD334-TS-001).*
L2199: The induction of colitis also resulted in increased mucosal thickness compared to healthy animals. Etrasimod significantly reduced the colitis-associated increase in mucosal thickness at 3 and 10 mg/kg/day compared to vehicle. Fingolimod also significantly reduced the colitis-associated increase in mucosal thickness compared to vehicle.
L2200: **Figure 3. Effects of Etrasimod on Mucosal Thickness in Colitis Mice**
L2201: * p &lt; 0.05, *** p &lt; 0.001, **** p &lt; 0.0001 compared to vehicle.
L2202: APD334: etrasimod; FTY720: fingolimod
L2203: Figure copied directly from study report (Study APD334-TS-001).
L2204: Histopathology was performed on the distal colon. The tissue was scored based on the following criteria:
L2205: - Crypt score based on abnormal crypt architecture including distortion, branching, atrophy and crypt loss (range 0-3)
- goblet cell loss (range 0-2)
- mucosal erosion and ulceration (range 0-1)
- immune cell infiltration (range 0-2)
L2206: Total histopathological score was calculated by combining the scores for each of the four parameters for a maximum score of 8. However, the Applicant did not clearly report which criteria contributed to the observed drug-related effects on this parameter.
L2207: The histopathology score was significantly increased in colitis animals compared to healthy controls. Etrasimod treatment significantly reduced the histopathology score at 3 mg/kg/day only compared to vehicle control. Fingolimod also significantly reduced the histopathology score compared to vehicle control
L2208: **Figure 4. Effects of Etrasimod on Distal Colon Histopathology Score in Colitis Mice**
L2209: ** p &lt; 0.01, **** p &lt; 0.0001 compared to vehicle.
L2210: APD334: etrasimod; FTY720: fingolimod
L2211: Figure copied directly from study report (Study APD334-TS-001).
L2212: Compared to healthy mice, colitis mice demonstrated increased mRNA expression of immune cell markers and cytokines in colonic tissue. Specifically, colitis mice had higher levels of CD4 (indicative of T cells, natural killer cells, and/or dendritic cells), CD3\gamma (T cells), and CD11b (monocytes, natural killer cells, and/or dendritic cells), while levels of Ly6G (neutrophils and/or granulocytes) were unchanged. Etrasimod treatment resulted in decreased levels of CD4 (\ge 3 mg/kg/day), CD3\gamma (\ge 3 mg/kg/day), and CD11b (all doses) compared to vehicle controls, while no differences were observed on Ly6G.
L2213: **Figure 5. Effects of Etrasimod on Inflammatory Cell Recruitment as Determined by mRNA Expression in Distal Colon Tissue of Colitis Mice**
L2214: * p &lt; 0.05, ** p &lt; 0.01, *** p &lt; 0.001, **** p &lt; 0.0001 compared to vehicle.
APD334: etrasimod; FTY720: fingolimod.
L2215: Figure copied directly from study report (Study APD334-TS-001).
L2216: Similarly, mRNA expression of T cell and/or monocyte-derived pro-inflammatory cytokines, including tumor necrosis factor alpha (TNF-\alpha), interleukin (IL)-1\beta, IL-6, IL-17A, and interferon gamma (IFN-\gamma), was higher in colitis mice compared to healthy animals ("naïve" or "unsorted/H2O"), while no differences were observed in the expression of IL-10 or IL-13 compared to vehicle control mice ("unsorted/H2O"). Etrasimod treatment significantly decreased mRNA expression of TNF-\alpha (all doses), IL-1\beta (all doses), IL-6 (3 mg/kg/day only), IL-17A (all doses), and IFN-\gamma (1 mg/kg/day only) compared to vehicle controls.
L2217: Figure 6. Effects of Etrasimod on mRNA Expression of Pro-Inflammatory Cytokines in Distal Colon Tissue of Colitis Mice

APD334: etrasimod; FTY720: fingolimod.

L2234: ## Executive Summary (Clinical Relevance)
L2235: Etrasimod was positive for carcinogenicity in mice but negative for carcinogenicity in rats.
L2236: In a two-year carcinogenicity study in mice, a statistically significant positive dose-response in the incidences of hemangiosarcoma malignant tumor (multicentric neoplasm) and combined hemangioma/hemangiosarcoma were observed at 6 and 20 mg/kg/day males and females compared to controls. These doses correspond to 46- and 134-fold, respectively, the MRHD of 2 mg/day etrasimod (based on AUC comparison).
L2237: In a two-year carcinogenicity study in rats, no carcinogenic findings were observed in males or females administered up to 20 mg/kg/day for 86 to 89 weeks, after which mortalities precluded continued conduct of the study. This dose corresponds to 88- and 198-fold the MRHD of 2 mg/day etrasimod in males and females, respectively (based on AUC comparison).
L2238: The Executive Carcinogenicity Assessment Committee concurred that the two-year mouse carcinogenicity study was adequately conducted, and there was a statistically significant, drug-related increase in the incidences of hemangiosarcoma malignant tumor (multicentric neoplasm) and combined hemangioma/hemangiosarcoma at the mid and high doses (6 and 20 mg/kg/day) in male and female mice. The Committee also concurred that the two-year rat carcinogenicity study was adequately conducted, and there were no drug-related neoplasms in either males or females.1
L2239: 1 NDA 216956: ECAC Meeting Minutes. R. Aziz (OND IO); April 4, 2023.

L2246: Etrasimod was not mutagenic or clastogenic in a standard battery of genotoxicity studies.2
L2247: (b) (4) a starting material and potential drug substance impurity, was negative for genotoxicity in a bacterial reverse mutation assay and is therefore being controlled as a non-mutagenic impurity in accordance with ICH Q3A.
L2248: (b) (4) a raw/starting material and potential process impurity, was positive for the ability to induce reverse mutations at selected loci of selected strains of *S. typhimurium* and at the tryptophan locus of *E. coli* WP2uvrA in the presence and absence of an exogenous metabolic activation system. This substance is therefore considered a class

L2252: ## Carcinogenicity
L2253: ## *Key Findings:*
L2254: In a 2-year carcinogenicity study in mice, male and female animals were orally administered etrasimod at 2, 6, or 20 mg/kg/day. Etrasimod dose-dependently and significantly increased the mortality rate in high dose males and females compared to vehicle controls. Drug-related non-neoplastic lesions were observed in the lungs (minimal to marked alveolar histiocytosis, minimal to moderate alveolar eosinophilic accumulation, minimal to mild multifocal fibrosis, minimal to mild osseous metaplasia, and minimal fibrin deposition) in all treated groups and in the thymus (minimally to moderately increased lymphocytes in the medulla) in the mid and high dose groups. Additionally, angiomatous hyperplasia was observed in various tissues (bone marrow, epididymides, heart, liver, subcutis, femorotibial joint) in high dose males and in the uterus of mid and high dose females with increased incidence compared to control animals. Etrasimod-related neoplastic lesions included a dose-dependent increase in the incidence of hemangiosarcomas (multicentric neoplasm) and combined hemangiomas/hemangiosarcomas in both male and female mice compared to control animals.
L2255: In a 2-year carcinogenicity study in rats, male and female animals were orally administered etrasimod at 2, 6, or 20 mg/kg/day. Etrasimod did not significantly affect the mortality rate. Drug-related non-neoplastic lesions were observed in the lymphoid tissues (increased medullary lymphocytes in the thymus; decreased marginal zone lymphocytes and increased incidence of extramedullary hematopoiesis in the spleen; sinus dilation, sinus histiocytosis and/or sinus erythrocytosis/erythrophagocytosis in the mandibular, mesenteric, iliac, renal and/or mediastinal lymph nodes) and brain (minimal to mild focal thalamic mineralization) of males and females at all doses as well as in the liver (basophilic, clear, and/or eosinophilic foci of cellular alteration and minimal to mild bile duct hyperplasia) of all treated females and males in the mid and high dose groups. No statistically significant increases in tumor incidences occurred in this study.

L2257: ## 1.1 Regulatory History
L2258: The original IND for etrasimod, IND [REDACTED] (b) (4) was submitted to the Division of Neurology Products (DNP) [REDACTED] (b) (4). The Applicant has subsequently submitted INDs 125154, 125509, [REDACTED] (b) (4), 142182, 146360, and 146975 [REDACTED] (b) (4) for etrasimod to various divisions. To support the development of etrasimod for use in subjects with ulcerative colitis (IND 125154), [REDACTED] (b) (4).
L2259: [REDACTED] (b) (4) II nonclinical studies (including carcinogenicity protocols and discussion of early termination criteria) were submitted [REDACTED] (b) (4).
L2260: Etrasimod has been evaluated in 2-year oral carcinogenicity studies in rat and mouse. Special protocol assessment (SPA) requests were submitted [REDACTED] (b) (4).
L2261: [REDACTED] (b) (4) The SPAs were reviewed by the Executive CAC (ECAC), and their agreement and recommendations were communicated to the Applicant [REDACTED] (b) (4).
L2262: ## Rat Study:
L2263: - On August 7, 2020 (SDN 44), the Applicant requested advice on their proposed modification plans for the 2-year carcinogenicity study in rats, based on decreased survival at Week 78. The Division of Pharmacology/Toxicology for Neurology (DPT-N) advised that no actions be taken at that time but recommended standard stopping and termination criteria in the event that survival decreased further.5
- On September 17, 2020, the Applicant notified DPT-N by email that survival in the female water control group had declined to 25, and the Applicant planned to terminate all females when survival in this group reached 20; the Division agreed with this plan.6 On October 21, 2020 (SDN 47), the Applicant notified the Division that survival in the female water control group had reached 20 at Week 90, and all surviving females were terminated at that time.
- In October 2020, the Applicant notified DPT-N by email that survival in the male high dose group had declined to 20. The Applicant stopped dosing in this group and planned to terminate all surviving high dose males when survival in this group reached 15 prior to Week 100 or to terminate all males in the study should survival in the high dose male group reach 15 at Week 100 or later; the Division agreed with this plan.7 On October 27, 2020, the Applicant notified the Division [REDACTED] (b) (4).
L2264: that survival in the control males had reached 20 at Week 91, and all surviving males were terminated at that time (SDN 48).
L2265: ## **Mouse Study:**
L2266: - On October 2, 2020 (SDN 46), the Applicant requested advice from DPT-N on their proposed modification plans for the 2-year carcinogenicity study in mice, based on decreased survival at Week 79. ECAC recommended that no actions be taken at that time but provided recommended stopping and termination criteria should survival decrease further.8
- On December 30, 2020 (SDN 53), the Applicant notified DPT-N that survival in the high dose male group had reached 20 at Week 91, and dose administration was suspended at that time. This group reached 15 survivors at Week 99, triggering termination of surviving males. Survival in the mid dose male group reached 20 at Week 99, and dose administration was suspended at that time. However, mid dose males did not reach 15 survivors prior to scheduled necropsy at the end of the study period. Water control, vehicle control, and low-dose male groups were administered control or etrasimod daily, as appropriate, until the end of the scheduled dosing period (Week 104).
- On November 17, 2020 (SDN 51), the Applicant notified DPT-N that survival in the high dose female group had reached 20 at week 84, and dose administration was suspended at that time. On December 30, 2020 (SDN 53), the Applicant notified the Division that survival in the high dose female group had reached 15 at Week 91, triggering termination of surviving females in that group. Survival in mid and low dose females reached 20 at Weeks 92 and 98, respectively, resulting in suspension of dose administration. The mid dose female group reached 15 survivors at Week 96, triggering termination of surviving females in this group. Survival in the female water control group reached 20 at Week 100, triggering termination of all surviving females at this time.
L2267: On October 14, 2022 (SDN 1), the Applicant submitted NDA 216956 to the Division of Gastroenterology (DG) for etrasimod as a treatment of moderately to severely active ulcerative colitis. The NDA submission included the final study reports and corresponding SEND datasets for the 2-year carcinogenicity studies in mice and rats. The adequacy and findings of these studies are the subject of this review.

L2277: ## 8.1 APD334 L-Arginine: A 104-Week Oral Oncogenicity Study in Mice
L2278: Study no.:	APD334.TOX.003
Study report location:	EDR 4.2.3.4
Study initiation date:	March 5, 2019
Conducting laboratory and location:	(b) (4)
GLP compliance:	Yes
Drug, lot #, and % purity:	Etrasimod (APD334 L-arginine)
Lot # 5015-12-16
Purity 99.95%
Prior Exec CAC Dose Concurrence:	Y
Basis for Dose Selection:	The high dose of 20 mg/kg/day was based on findings of mortality at 200 mg/kg/day and adverse histopathology findings (lung and thyroid) at \ge 60 mg/kg/day in the 91-day preliminary study (Study 900-169). The mid and low doses were selected to provide an appropriate dose-response.
L2279: Reviewer Carcinogenicity Conclusion (negative/ positive): Positive
L2280: ECAC Carcinogenicity Conclusion (negative/ positive): Positive
L2281: ## Key Study Findings
L2282: - Etrasimod had a dose-dependent effect on the mortality rate with significantly increased mortalities in high dose males and females compared to vehicle controls. Mortality rates were 72%, 64%, 66%, 72%, and 77% for water control, vehicle control, low, mid, and high dose males, respectively. Mortality rates were 73%, 69%, 69%, 77%, and 77% in female mice, respectively.
- Etrasimod-related non-neoplastic lesions were observed in the lungs in all treated groups and in the thymus in the mid and high dose groups. Additionally, angiomatic hyperplasia was observed in various tissues (bone marrow, epididymides, heart, liver, subcutis, femorotibial joint) in high dose males and in the uterus of mid and high dose females with increased incidence compared to control animals.
- Etrasimod-related neoplastic lesions included a dose-dependent increase in the incidence of combined hemangioma and hemangiosarcomas (multicentric neoplasm) in both male and female mice compared to control animals.
L2283: ## **Tumor Findings:**
L2284: Independent analyses of the tumor findings were conducted by FDA statistical reviewer, Feng Zhou, M.S.9,10 Statistically significant positive dose-responses in the incidences of hemangiosarcoma malignant tumor (multicentric neoplasm) and combined hemangioma/hemangiosarcoma were observed in both male and female mice (P&lt;0.005) when compared with the vehicle control group. The incidences of hemangiosarcoma malignant tumor and combined hemangioma/hemangiosarcoma in the mid and high dose groups were statistically significantly higher compared to the vehicle control group (P&lt;0.01). Tumor incidences were similar between the vehicle control and water control groups.
L2285: ## **Methods**
L2286: Doses:	2, 6, 20 mg/kg/day
Frequency of dosing:	Once daily
Number/Sex/Group:	Control groups: 75/sex/group
Treated groups: 65/sex/group
TK control groups: 6/sex/group
TK treated groups: 27/sex/group (including 3 potential replacement animals/group)
Dose volume:	Initially, the low, mid, and high dose groups were dosed at 1, 3, and 10 mL/kg, respectively. Beginning on day 92, all groups were administered 10 mL/kg.
Formulation/Vehicle:	L-arginine monohydrochloride 0.92 mg/mL in deionized water
Route of administration:	ORAL GAVAGE
Species:	MOUSE
Strain:	CD1(ICR)
Age:	10 weeks
Comment on Study Design and Conduct:	The carcinogenicity study was adequately conducted.

- Low dose females: Week 98
- Mid dose males: Week 99
L2291: All surviving animals in the group were terminated due to survival in the group reaching 15:
L2292: - High dose females: Week 91
- Mid dose females: Week 96
- High dose males: Week 99
L2293: All surviving females from the water control, vehicle control, and low dose groups were terminated at week 100 due to survival in the water control female group reaching 20.
L2294: Dosing Solution Analysis: During weeks 1-3 of the study, the 2 mg/mL formulation concentrations were 80.5%, 73.5%, and 85% of nominal, respectively. Following optimization of the formulation preparation procedures and batch sizes, all further concentrations (week 4 onward) were within the acceptable range (\pm (b)/(4) % of nominal). As all treated groups were equally affected and the deviations were not large, these findings do not appear to have affected the validity of the study.

L2302: ## Observations and Results
L2303: ## Mortality
L2304: Animals were observed at least twice daily for morbidity and mortality. Increased early terminations related to hemangiomas/hemangiosarcomas were reported at \ge 6 mg/kg/day and were attributed to etrasimod administration. Other causes of morbidity/moribundity were observed with similar incidence in all groups, including controls, and therefore were considered unrelated to etrasimod administration.
L2305: Due to mortality, dosing was suspended and/or groups were terminated prior to completion of the planned 104-week study. The timeline of dosing cessation and/or termination for this study is provided in Table 3.

L2314: Independent analyses of survival were conducted by FDA statistical reviewer, Feng Zhou, M.S.9,10 The survival distributions of mice in all treatment groups were estimated using the Kaplan-Meier product limit method. For control, low, medium, and high dose groups, the dose-response relationship was tested using the likelihood ratio test and the homogeneity of survival distributions was tested using the log-rank test.
L2315: The statistical reviewer's analysis showed the numbers (percent) of deaths that occurred prior to scheduled termination were 54 (72%), 48 (64%), 43 (66%), 47 (72%), or 50 (77%) in male mice and 55 (73%), 52 (69%), 45 (69%), 50 (77%), or 50 (77%) in female mice in the WC, VC, LD, MD, and HD groups, respectively. Survival analyses showed a statistically significant dose-response relationship in mortality in both males and females when compared with the VC group (P_s &lt; 0.05). Mortality rates in HD groups were statistically significantly higher than VC group for both male and female mice (P_s &lt; 0.05). Death rates for the WC group were similar as that of the VC group for both male and female mice.

L2326: ## Clinical Signs
L2327: Detailed clinical examinations were conducted weekly throughout the study. No etrasimod-related clinical signs were observed, including no trends in the incidence or frequency of palpable masses/nodules.
L2328: ## Body Weights
L2329: Decreased absolute body weight was observed in all treated groups compared to the WC throughout the study, though differences rarely exceeded 10%. Decreased body weight was also observed in all treated groups compared to the VC throughout the study, with differences exceeding 10% on some occasions from Week 76 onward. Similarly, dose-independent decreased body weight gain was observed in all treated males compared to the WC (-10% to -25%) and VC groups (-16% to -30%) and in LD an HD females compared to the WC (-31% and -28%, respectively) and VC groups (-30% and -27%).
L2330: Figure 3. Body Weights in Male Mice Administered Etrasimod
L2331: Figure copied directly from study report (Study APD334-TOX-003).
L2332: Figure 4. Body Weights in Female Mice Administered Etrasimod
L2333: Figure copied directly from study report (Study APD334-TOX-003).
L2334: ## Feed Consumption
L2335: Decreased feed consumption was observed in all treated groups compared to WC and VC groups at various points during the study with no dose-dependence. Decreased feed consumption generally correlated with decreased body weights in treated compared to control animals.
L2336: ## Gross Pathology
L2337: Etrasimod-related macroscopic findings were observed in the liver and subcutis of a few HD males. In most cases, cysts in the liver and edema in the subcutis of these animals correlated with histopathological findings of hemangiosarcoma.
L2338: No treatment-related macroscopic findings were reported in LD or MD males or in treated females from any group.

L2343: ## Histopathology
L2344: Peer Review Conducted: Yes (Theodore Slone, Jr., DVM, DACVP)
L2345: Historical Control Provided for Tumor Incidence: No
L2346: ## Neoplastic
L2347: Neoplastic findings attributed by the Applicant to treatment with etrasimod were limited to an increased incidence of hemangiosarcomas and hemangiomas in males and females in the MD and HD groups. Commonly affected tissues included the liver, subcutis, heart, bone marrow, and spleen. A few hemangiosarcomas/hemangiomas were also observed in the uterus, intestine, skeletal muscle, lung, thymus, adrenal glands, kidneys, eyes, mesenteric lymph node, mesentery/peritoneum, spinal cord, and epididymides. These neoplasms were observed earlier in the study in the MD and HD groups compared to control animals. Additionally, the number of animals with two or more tissues with hemangiosarcomas/hemangiomas was higher in MD and HD males as well as HD females compared to controls.
L2348: Independent analyses of survival were conducted by FDA statistical reviewer, Feng Zhou, M.S., using the SAS data sets received on October 14, 2022, via NDA 216956/0001.9,10 The tumor data were analyzed for dose-response relationships and
L2349: pairwise comparisons of the WC and VC groups separately with each of the treated groups using the Poly-k method (see Statistical Review for further details).
L2350: Statistically significant positive dose-responses in the incidences of hemangiosarcoma malignant tumor (multicentric neoplasm) and combined hemangioma/hemangiosarcoma were observed in both male and female mice (P&lt;0.005) when compared with the VC group. The incidences of hemangiosarcoma malignant tumor and combined hemangioma/hemangiosarcoma in the MD and HD groups were statistically significantly higher compared to the VC group (P&lt;0.01). Tumor incidences were similar between the VC and WC groups.

L2354: ## Non Neoplastic
L2355: Non-neoplastic findings related to etrasimod (based on increased incidence and/or severity compared to controls) included angiomatous hyperplasia in various tissues as well as findings in the lungs and thymus.
L2356: Angiomatous hyperplasia, consisting of focal to multifocal localized areas of increased capillaries and small vessels lined by flattened endothelium with no distortion of the adjacent normal tissue, was observed in various tissues (bone marrow, epididymides, heart, liver, subcutis, femorotibial joint) in HD males with increased overall incidence compared to control animals. Angiomatous hyperplasia was observed in various tissues in females (control or treated) as well, but only considered drug-related in the uterus, (
L2357: thick-walled blood vessel within the myometrium that was more prominent near the broad ligament); this finding was observed with increased incidence and severity in MD and HD females compared to controls.
L2358: Etrasimod-related changes in the lungs, including minimal to marked alveolar histiocytosis, minimal to moderate alveolar eosinophilic accumulation, minimal to mild multifocal fibrosis, minimal to mild osseous metaplasia, and minimal fibrin deposition, were observed in all treated groups compared to controls; two or more of these findings were often observed in affected animals.
L2359: Finally, minimally to moderately increased lymphocytes were observed in the medulla of the thymus with increased incidence and/or severity in MD and HD males and females compared to controls.

L2364: ## Toxicokinetics
L2365: Etrasimod was not detected in any control group samples (limit of quantitation = 500 ng/mL).
L2366: Etrasimod was quantifiable in plasma samples from all treated animals through 24 hours post-dose on day 364 of dosing. C_{max} and AUC for etrasimod increased approximately proportionally with increasing dose over the tested range. T_{max} occurred between 2-12 hours with no clear effect of dose. No sex differences in toxicokinetic parameters were observed.
