L2370: ## **8.2 APD334 L-Arginine: A 104-Week Oral Oncogenicity Study in Rats**
L2371: Study no.:	APD334.TOX.002
Study report location:	EDR 4.2.3.4
Study initiation date:	January 7, 2019
Conducting laboratory and location:	(b) (4)
GLP compliance:	Yes
Drug, lot #, and % purity:	APD334 L-arginine
Lot # 5015-12-16
Purity 99.95%
Prior Exec CAC Dose Concurrence:	Y
Basis for Dose Selection:	Based on the findings in a previous 6-month study in rats (Study TX14009), the high dose of 20 mg/kg/day was expected to exceed 25 times the clinical exposure (AUC) for both etrasimod and the metabolite M3. The low and mid doses were selected to allow for adequate AUC spacing between doses.
L2372: Reviewer Carcinogenicity Conclusion (negative/ positive): Negative
ECAC Carcinogenicity Conclusion (negative/ positive): Negative
L2373: ## **Key Study Findings**
L2374: - Etrasimod did not have a dose-dependent effect on the mortality rate or significantly increased mortalities in treated animals compared to vehicle controls. The statistical reviewer's analysis showed the numbers (percent) of deaths that occurred prior to scheduled termination of the group were 73%, 67%, 57%, 62%, and 72% in male rats and 73%, 65%, 55%, 58%, and 58% in female mice in the water control, vehicle control, low dose, mid dose, and high dose groups, respectively.
- Etrasimod-related non-neoplastic lesions were observed in the lymphoid tissues and brain of males and females at all tested doses as well as in the liver of all treated females and males in the MD and HD groups.
- No statistically significant increases in tumor incidences occurred in this study.
L2375: ## **Tumor Findings:**
L2376: Independent analyses of the tumor findings were conducted by FDA statistical reviewer, Feng Zhou, M.S.9,10 The tumor analysis showed no statistically significant positive dose-response relationship between the treated groups and vehicle control group for tumors of any type.
L2377: ## **Methods**
L2378: Doses:	0 (vehicle), 0 (water), 2, 6, and 20 mg/kg/day
Frequency of dosing:	Once daily
Number/Sex/Group:	Main study: controls = 75/sex/group, treated = 65/sex/group
TK: controls = 5/sex/group, treated = 9/sex/group
Dose volume:	10 mL/kg (with deviations)
Formulation/Vehicle:	L-arginine monohydrochloride 0.92 mg/mL in deionized water
Route of administration:	ORAL GAVAGE
Species:	RAT
Strain:	SPRAGUE-DAWLEY
Age:	8 weeks
Comment on Study Design and Conduct:	Although metabolites were not assessed in this study, other PK studies (i.e., Study APD334-DMPK-002) have demonstrated that rats produce the major human metabolites, M3 and M6, at levels that exceed those in humans when tested at the dose levels used in this study. Overall, the carcinogenic potential of the major human metabolites was adequately assessed, and the carcinogenicity study was adequately conducted.
Dosing Comments (Dose Adjustments or Early Termination):	Week 86: Survival in the male HD group (20 mg/kg/day) reached 20, and dose administration was suspended.
Week 89: All surviving females were terminated due to survival in the water control group reaching 20.
Week 90: All surviving males were terminated due to survival in the water control group reaching 20.
Dosing Solution Analysis:	At Weeks 4 and 8, the observed concentrations for the 0.2 mg/mL formulation were 63% and 68.5% of nominal, respectively. At Week 8, the observed concentration for the 0.6 mg/mL formulation was 53.9% of nominal. The 2 mg/mL formulation was not affected. Following review of the preparation methodology, beginning on Day 54, all dose formulations were prepared at 2 mg/mL. All formulations following this time point were acceptable
L2379: (\pm (b)/(4)% of nominal). These deviations did not appear to have affected the study results.

L2393: ## Observations and Results
L2394: ## Mortality
L2395: Animals were observed at least twice daily for morbidity and mortality. No morbidity or mortality was attributed to administration of etrasimod (named APD334 in this report).
L2396: Survival in the male HD group (20 mg/kg/day) reached 20 at Week 86, and dose administration was suspended at that time. Additionally, survival in the male and female WC groups reached 20 at Week 90 and Week 89, respectively. As such, all surviving
L2397: males were necropsied during Weeks 90 and 91 and all surviving females were necropsied during Weeks 89 and 90.
L2398: Independent analyses of survival were conducted by FDA statistical reviewer, Feng Zhou, M.S.9,10 The survival distributions of rats in all treatment groups were estimated using the Kaplan-Meier product limit method. For control, LD, MD, and HD groups, the dose-response relationship was tested using the likelihood ratio test and the homogeneity of survival distributions was tested using the log-rank test.
L2399: The statistical reviewer's analysis showed the numbers (percent) of deaths that occurred prior to scheduled termination of the group were 73%, 67%, 57%, 62%, and 72% in male rats and 73%, 65%, 55%, 58%, and 58% in female mice in the WC, VC, LD, MD, and HD groups, respectively. No statistically significant dose-response relationship in mortality was observed in treated males and females compared to the VC group. Death rates for the WC group were similar to that of the VC group for both male and female mice.

L2410: ## Clinical Signs
L2411: Detailed clinical examinations were conducted weekly throughout the study. No etrasimod-related clinical signs were observed, including no trends in the incidence or frequency of palpable masses/nodules.
L2412: ## Body Weights
L2413: Decreased absolute body weight was observed in all treated groups compared to WC or VC throughout the study, though differences rarely exceeded 10%. Similarly, dose-independent decreased body weight gain was observed in LD and HD males compared to the WC and VC groups (-10% and -14%, respectively) and in all treated females compared to the WC (-16% to -18%) and VC groups (-14% to -16%).

L2418: ## **Feed Consumption**
L2419: Decreased feed consumption was observed in all treated groups compared to the WC and VC groups at various points during the study with no dose-dependence. Decreased feed consumption generally correlated with decreased body weights in treated compared to control animals.
L2420: ## **Gross Pathology**
L2421: No macroscopic findings related to etrasimod administration were reported.
L2422: ## **Histopathology**
L2423: Peer Review Conducted: Yes (Joelle Ibanes, DVM, DACVP)
L2424: Historical Control Provided for Tumor Incidence: No
L2425: ## **Neoplastic**
L2426: According to the Applicant, there were no neoplastic findings related to etrasimod administration. A statistically significant incidence of carcinoma of the Zymbal gland was reported in female mice treated with etrasimod compared to controls (p = 0.0244 by the Poly-3 trend test). However, the absolute incidences of this tumor were low in all groups (0/74, 1/74, 0/64, 0/65, and 2/63 in the WC, VC, LD, MD, and HD groups, respectively), no statistically significant differences were observed using other tests (Fisher exact test, Cochran-Armitage trend test, and Peto test), and a similar trend was not observed in male animals. The incidence in females at the high dose (2/63 or 3.2%) was slightly above the top of the historical control range for females (1.7%). Based on these factors, this finding was considered incidental and not related to treatment with etrasimod.
L2427: Independent analyses of survival were conducted by FDA statistical reviewer, Feng Zhou, M.S., using the SAS data sets received on October 14, 2022, via NDA 216956/0001.9,10 The tumor data were analyzed for dose-response relationships and pairwise comparisons of the WC and VC groups separately with each of the treated groups using the Poly-k method (see Statistical Review for further details).
L2428: Based on the criteria of adjustment for multiple testing, there were no statistically significant positive dose-response relationships between the treated groups and VC group for male and female rats. The tumor incidences were similar between the vehicle control group and water control group.
L2429: ## **Non Neoplastic**
L2430: Non-neoplastic findings related to etrasimod administration (based on increased incidence and/or severity compared to controls) were observed in the lymphoid system (lymph nodes, spleen, thymus) and brain of males and females at all tested doses as well as in the liver of all treated females and males in the MD and HD groups.
L2431: Etrasimod-related effects in the lymphoid organs of males and females at all doses included minimally to moderately increased lymphocytes in the thymus medulla, minimally to markedly decreased lymphocytes in the marginal zone of the spleen,
L2432: minimally to markedly increased extramedullary hematopoiesis in the spleen, as well as variably increased incidence and/or severity of sinus dilation, sinus histiocytosis, and/or sinus erythrocytosis/erythrophagocytosis in the iliac, mandibular, mediastinal, mesenteric, and/or renal lymph nodes. Findings in the thymus were generally not dose-dependent in incidence or severity, although increased severity (moderate) was observed in HD males. Decreased marginal zone lymphocytes and increased extramedullary hematopoiesis in the spleen were observed without dose-dependent incidence or severity. Lymph node findings also did not show clear or consistent dose-dependence.
L2433: An increased incidence of minimal to mild focal thalamic mineralization, frequently bilateral, was observed in the brain in all etrasimod-treated animals with no dose-dependence. The mineralization was characterized by focal accumulations of circular, densely basophilic concretions within the thalamus.
L2434: In the liver, increased incidence and/or severity of basophilic foci of cellular alteration were observed in females at all doses compared to controls. Increased incidence and/or severity of clear or eosinophilic foci of cellular alteration were observed in MD and HD males and females. Increased incidence of minimal or mild bile duct hyperplasia was observed in MD and HD males and females compared to controls. Findings in the liver were generally not dose-dependent in incidence or severity.

L2441: Table created by reviewer, based on Applicant's raw data (Study APD334-TOX-002).
L2442: ## Toxicokinetics
L2443: Etrasimod was not detected in any control group samples (limit of quantitation = 5.00 ng/mL).
L2444: Etrasimod was quantifiable in plasma samples from all treated animals through 24 hours post-dose on day 364 of dosing. C_{max} and AUC for etrasimod increased approximately proportionally with increasing dose over the tested range. T_{max} occurred between 2-12 hours with no clear effect of dose. AUC and C_{max} values for etrasimod were approximately 2-fold higher in females compared to males at all tested doses.

L2498: ## 1 Summary
L2499: This review evaluates statistically the data of the 2-year oral carcinogenicity study of Etrasimod (referred to as APD334 hereafter) in rats and the 2-year oral carcinogenicity study of APD334 in mice. The review analyzes the dose-response relationship of tumor incidences and mortality (including tumor-related mortality).
L2500: ## For the rat study:
L2501: The reviewer's survival data analysis results showed that the survival analyses didn't show any statistically significant dose response relationship in mortality in males and females when compared with vehicle control. The death rates for the water control group were similar with that of vehicle control group for both male and female rats.
L2502: The reviewer's tumor data analysis results showed that there were no statistically significant positive dose response relationships between the treated groups and vehicle control group for male and female rats. The tumor incidences were similar between the vehicle control group and water control group.
L2503: ## For the mouse study:
L2504: The reviewer's survival data analyses show a statistically significant dose response relationship in mortality in both males and females when compared with vehicle control group (P&lt;0.05). The mortality rates in high dose treated groups were statistically significantly higher than vehicle control group for both male and female mice (P&lt;0.05). The death rates for the water control group were similar as that of vehicle control group for both male and female mice.
L2505: The reviewer's tumor data analysis results showed that APD334 cause a statistically significant positive dose response in the combined incidence of hemangiosarcoma malignant tumor for all organs in whole body in male and female mice. For this tumor type, the pairwise comparisons between the vehicle control group and APD334 mid dose (6 mg/kg/day) group and APD334 high dose (20 mg/kg/day) group were statistically significant. See Table 1 below for tumor incidences and p-values of the tumor types mentioned above. The tumor incidences were similar between the vehicle control group and water control group.
L2506: APD334 also cause a statistically significant positive dose response in incidence of the combination of hemangiosarcoma malignant tumor with hemangioma, benign tumor for all organs in whole body in male and female mice. For this tumor type, the pairwise comparisons between the vehicle control group and APD334 mid dose (6 mg/kg/day) group and APD334 high dose (20 mg/kg/day) group were statistically significant. See Table 1 below for tumor incidences and p-values of the tumor types mentioned above. The tumor incidences were similar between the vehicle control group and water control group.

L2513: ## 2 Background
L2514: The sponsor, Arena Pharmaceuticals, Inc, is submitting, under section 505(b)(1) of the Federal Food, Drug, and Cosmetic Act and 21 CFR 314.50, a New Drug Application (NDA) for etrasimod for the treatment of moderately to severely active ulcerative colitis (UC)
L2515: The sponsor submitted two carcinogenicity study reports: Study apd334-tox-003: "A 104-Week Oral Oncogenicity Study in Mice" and Study apd334-tox-002: "A 104-Week Oral Oncogenicity Study in Rats". The study reports and the electronic SEND datasets were submitted on 10/14/2022 (via NDA 216956/S0001).
L2516: The phrase "dose response relationship" refers to the linear component of the effect of treatment, and not necessarily to a strictly increasing or decreasing mortality or tumor incidences rate as dose increases. Results of this review have been discussed with the nonclinical team.
L2517: ## 3 Rat Study- apd334-tox-002
L2518: **Study Report:** apd334-tox-002.pdf (statistical report on page 2784)
L2519: **SAS data:** SEND
L2520: The objective of this study was to evaluate the oncogenic potential of a test article, Etrasimod (referred to as APD334 hereafter), in male and female Sprague Dawley CD® [CrL:CD®(SD)] rats when administered daily for up to approximately 91 weeks (104 weeks originally intended) via oral gavage. In addition, steady-state systemic exposure to APD334 was evaluated on Day 364. The study design was as follows:
L2521: Group No.	Test Material	Dose Level (mg/kg/day)	Dose Volumea (mL/kg)	Dose Concentration (mg/mL)	Main Study
No. of Males	No. of Females
1	Water control	0	10	0	75	75
2	Control articleb	0	10	0	75	75
3	APD334 L-arginine	2	10/15.0c/1d	0.2c/2d	65	65
4	APD334 L-arginine	6	10/20.6c/3d	0.6c/2d	65	65
5	APD334 L-arginine	20	10	2	65	65
L2522: a Based on the most recent body weight measurement.
L2523: b The formulated control article was an aqueous solution of L-arginine hydrochloride at the concentration of L-arginine (0.92 mg/mL) in the high-dose formulation (20 mg/kg/day).
L2524: c Due to low recovery in the Week 8 low and mid-dose formulation samples, animals in Groups 3 and 4 were dosed on Days 52 and 53 with dose concentrations of 0.2 and 0.6 mg/mL, respectively, using dose volumes of 15.0 mL/kg and 20.6 mL/kg, respectively, to ensure the administration of the proper dosages.
L2525: d Beginning on Day 54 and for the remainder of the dosing phase, a dose concentration of 2 mg/mL was used for Groups 3 and 4, with dose volumes of 1 mL/kg and 3 mL/kg, respectively.
L2526: The following parameters and endpoints were evaluated in this study: mortality/survival, clinical signs and masses, body weight, body weight gains, food consumption, toxicokinetic parameters, and macroscopic and microscopic examinations.
L2527: ## 3.1 Sponsor's Analyses
L2528: ## 3.1.1 *Survival Analysis*
L2529: Intercurrent mortality data were analyzed using the Kaplan-Meier product-limit method. An overall test comparing all groups was conducted using a log-rank test (Allison, PD, 1995). If this overall test was significant (p &lt; 0.05) and there were more than two groups, then a follow up analysis was done where each treatment group was compared to the control group using a log-rank test.
L2530: **Sponsor's concluded results:** No definitive APD334-related effects on survival were observed.
L2531: ## 3.1.2 *Tumor Data Analysis*
L2532: Tumor incidence data were analyzed using both survival adjusted and unadjusted tests. The unadjusted tests were based on the incidence and number of sites examined for each tumor type. The Cochran-Armitage trend test (Agresti, A. 2002) were calculated, and Fisher's exact test (Zar JH, 1999) was used to compare each treatment group with the control group. Trend test was applied for all control and treatment groups that were able to be arranged in a strictly increasing dose level. The survival adjusted test was conducted according to the prevalence/mortality methods described by Peto et al. (Peto R, Pike MC, Day NE, Gray RG, Lee PN, Parish S, Pete J, Richards S, Wahrendorf J. 1980).
L2533: The Poly-3 method (Bailer AJ, Portier CJ. 1988) (Bieler GS, Williams RL. 1993) was used to assess prevalence of tumors. The survival-adjusted rates based on the risk weights were displayed. The tests of significance included both an overall trend and pair-wise comparisons of each treatment group with the control. All p-values were reported using upper-tailed tests, unless otherwise indicated.
L2534: Evaluation criteria (p-values of significance) was applied differently for rare tumors (background rate of 1% or less) and common tumors (background rate greater than 1%) (Haseman JK, 1983). The evaluation criteria (from the FDA) (CDER, 2001) are given in the following table.
L2535: Evaluation Criteria for Common and Rare Tumors
L2536: Test for Positive Trends	Control-High Pair-wise Comparisons
Common and rare tumors were tested at 0.005 and 0.025 significance levels, respectively using upper-tailed tests	Common and rare tumors were tested at 0.01 and 0.05 significance levels, respectively using upper-tailed tests
L2537: **Sponsor's concluded results:** There were no APD334-related oncogenic findings.
L2538: ## 3.2 Reviewer's Analyses
L2539: To verify the sponsor's analyses and to perform additional analyses suggested by the reviewing pharmacologist, this review analyzed the SAS data sets of these studies received on 10/14/2022 via NDA 216956/S0001. The vehicle control group was compared to the treated groups (pairwise and trend) and vehicle control group was compared to water control (pairwise only) for the survival analyses and the tumor analyses.
L2540: ## 3.2.1 Data Quality
L2541: The sponsor only submitted SEND datasets on 10/14/2022 via NDA 216956/S0001. The data quality was acceptable.
L2542: ## 3.2.2 Survival Analysis
L2543: The survival distributions of rates in all treatment groups were estimated using the Kaplan-Meier product limit method. For control, low, medium, and high dose groups, the dose response relationship was tested using the likelihood ratio test and the homogeneity of survival distributions were tested using the log-rank test. The Kaplan-Meier curves for survival rates are given in Figures 1A and 1B in the appendix for male and female rats, respectively. The intercurrent mortality data are given in Tables 1A and 1B in the appendix for male and female rats, respectively. Results of the tests for dose response relationship and homogeneity of survivals, are given in Tables 3A and 3B in the appendix for male and female rats, respectively.
L2544: **Reviewer's findings:** The termination started at Week 91 for male rats and 90 for female rats. This reviewer's analysis showed the numbers (percent) of death that occurred prior to termination of the group were 55 (73%), 50 (67%), 37 (57%), 40 (62%), or 47 (72%) in male rats and 55 (73%), 49 (65%), 36 (55%), 38 (58%), or 38 (58%) in female rats in the WC, VC, LD, MD, or HD groups, respectively. The survival analyses didn't show any statistically significant dose response relationship in mortality in males and females when compared with vehicle control. The death rates for the water control group were similar with that of vehicle control group for both male and female rats.
L2545: ## 3.2.3 Tumor Data Analysis
L2546: The tumor data were analyzed for dose response relationships and pairwise comparisons of control group with each of the treated groups. Both the dose response relationship tests and pairwise comparisons were performed using the Poly-k method described in the papers of Bailer and Portier [2] and Bieler and Williams [3]. In this method an animal that lives the full study period (w_{max}) or dies before the terminal sacrifice but develops the tumor type being tested gets a score of s_h = 1. An animal that dies at week w_h without developing the tumor before the end of the study gets a score of s_h = \left(\frac{w_h}{w_{max}}\right)^k &lt; 1. The adjusted group size is defined as \Sigma s_h. As an interpretation, an animal with score s_h = 1 can be considered as a whole animal
L2547: while an animal with score s_h &lt; 1 can be considered as a partial animal. The adjusted group size \Sigma s_h is equal to N (the original group size) if all animals live up to the end of the study or if each animal that dies before the terminal sacrifice develops at least one tumor of the tumor type being tested, otherwise the adjusted group size is less than N. These adjusted group sizes are then used for the dose response relationship (or the pairwise) tests using the Cochran-Armitage test. One critical point for Poly-k test is the choice of the appropriate value of k, which depends on the tumor incidences pattern with the increased dose. For long term 104-week standard rat and mouse studies, a value of k=3 is suggested in the literature. Hence, this reviewer used k=3 for the analysis of this data. For the calculation of p-values the exact permutation method was used.
L2548: The adjusted levels of significance for testing a positive dose response in the 2-year rat study are 0.005 and 0.025 for a common tumor and a rare tumor, respectively. The adjusted levels of significance for the pairwise comparison in the 2-year rat study are 0.01 and 0.05 for a common tumor and a rare tumor, respectively. A rare tumor is defined as one in which the tumor rate is less than 1% in the vehicle control group. The tumor rates and the p-values of the tested tumor types are listed in Tables 5A, and 5B in the appendix for male and female rats, respectively.
L2549: **Reviewer's findings:** Following table displays the tumor types showing p-values less than or equal to 0.05 either for dose response relationships or for pairwise comparisons of treated groups and control for the injection sites.

L2554: ## 4 Mouse Study- apd334-tox-003

L2556: **SAS data:** SEND database
L2557: The objective of this study was to evaluate the oncogenic potential of the test article, Etrasimod (referred to as APD334 hereafter), in male and female Crl:CD1® (ICR) mice when administered via daily oral gavage for up to 104 weeks. In addition, steady-state systemic exposure to APD334 was evaluated on Day 364. The study design was as follows:

No. of Males	No. of Females

L2562: The following parameters and endpoints were evaluated in this study: mortality/survival, clinical signs, body weights, body weight gains, food consumption, toxicokinetic parameters, and macroscopic and microscopic examinations.
L2563: ## 4.1 Sponsor's Analyses
L2564: ## 4.1.1 Survival Analysis
L2565: The sponsor used the same survival analysis methods for the rat study in this mouse study.
L2566: **Sponsor's concluded results:** Statistically significant decreases in survival were noted in males at 20 mg/kg/day when compared to both the water control and control article groups, but not in any of the other groups on study.
L2567: ## 4.1.2 Tumor Data Analysis
L2568: The sponsor used the same tumor data analysis methods for the rat study in this mouse study.
L2569: **Sponsor's findings:** Administration of with APD334 was associated with an increase in incidence of hemangiosarcomas and/or hemangiomas in males and females at \ge 6 mg/kg/day which resulted in increased mortality at these dose levels and early dosing cessation/termination of males at 20 mg/kg/day and females at all dose levels. The hemangiosarcomas were associated with the macroscopic findings of cysts in the liver and edema in the subcutis in males at 20 mg/kg/day and in red discoloration of some of the masses in the subcutis of the females at 20 mg/kg/day. Commonly affected tissues were the liver, the subcutis, the heart, the bone marrow, and the spleen. A few hemangiosarcomas /hemangiomas were also noted in the uterus, intestine, skeletal muscle, lung, thymus, adrenal gland, kidney, eye, mesenteric lymph node, mesentery/peritoneum, spinal cord, and
L2570: File Name: NDA216956Carcin.doc
L2571: epididymides. The hemangiosarcomas and hemangiomas in males and females at \ge 6 mg/kg/day occurred earlier in the study compared to those present in water control and control article groups. The number of hemangiosarcomas/hemangiomas present per affected animals was also increased in males at \ge 6 mg/kg/day and in females at 20 mg/kg/day.

L2657: Figure 3 displays two simulated heart rate (HR) plots over 21 days for subjects with UC, comparing 2 mg QD without titration (left) and 2 mg QD with titration (right). The Y-axis represents Heart Rate (bpm), ranging from 40 to 80. The X-axis represents Days 1 to 21. Both plots show a median HR (solid line) and shaded areas representing 50% (darker blue), 75%, and 90% (lighter blue) prediction intervals for data simulated under the model. In both scenarios, the median HR starts high (around 75-80 bpm) and gradually decreases, stabilizing around 60-65 bpm by Day 21. The titration group shows a slightly lower median HR and tighter prediction intervals compared to the non-titration group, particularly in the initial days.
L2658: Line graph showing Placebo-corrected Mean Change from Baseline (90% CI) in HR (bpm) versus Time (0 to 24 hours) for four treatment groups: APD334 2 mg (Day 1), APD334 2 mg (Day 7), APD334 3 mg (Day 12), and APD334 4 mg (Day 14). The Y-axis ranges from -20 to 5 bpm. The APD334 2 mg (Day 1) group shows the largest negative change (around -15 bpm) at 3 hours, while the APD334 4 mg (Day 14) group shows the largest positive change (around 5 bpm) at 24 hours.
L2659: Line graph showing Mean (SE) Percent Change from Baseline in Lymphocyte Count versus Study Week (Baseline to Week 12) for Placebo, Etrasimod 1 mg, and Etrasimod 2 mg groups. The Y-axis ranges from -70% to 40%. Placebo shows a slight increase in lymphocyte count percentage change over time, starting near 0% at Baseline and ending around 18% at Week 12. Etrasimod 1 mg shows a decrease, starting near 0% at Baseline and ending around -20% at Week 12. Etrasimod 2 mg shows the greatest decrease, starting near 0% at Baseline and ending around -40% at Week 12. Error bars represent standard error (SE).
L2660: Figure 6 displays four plots showing the Exposure-Response Relationship for Predicted and Observed Probability of Efficacy Responses at Week 12 in Adult Subjects With UC. The x-axis for all plots is C_avg,ss (ng/mL), ranging from 0 to 200. The y-axis is Probability of Response, ranging from 0.00 to 1.00. The plots are arranged in a 2x2 grid: Top Left: Clinical Remission; Top Right: Symptomatic Remission; Bottom Left: Endoscopic Improvement; Bottom Right: Mucosal Healing. Each plot shows observed black points (proportions of patients achieving improvement) and simulated data (solid black line and shaded blue region representing medians and 95% prediction intervals). Below the plots, boxplots show the distribution of C_avg,ss for Placebo, 1 mg, and 2 mg dose groups.
L2661: Scatter plot showing Dose Normalized AUC (ng*h/mL per mg) on the Y-axis versus Etrasimod Only Steady State and Etrasimod+Fluconazole on the X-axis. The plot compares observed exposure (black dots) with predicted exposure (red and blue box plots). The red box plot (Etrasimod Only Steady State) shows AUC values ranging roughly from 500 to 1500. The blue box plot (Etrasimod+Fluconazole) shows AUC values ranging roughly from 1000 to 2000, indicating a significant increase in exposure when co-administered with fluconazole.
L2662: The target subject population will include (approximately 50% of subjects in each category):
Subjects who have had an inadequate response to, loss of response to, or intolerance to conventional therapy and are naïve to biologic/Janus kinase (JAK) inhibitors
Subjects who have had an inadequate response to, loss of response to, or intolerance to a biologic or JAK inhibitor (subjects in this category may have received prior conventional therapy).
Flowchart Description:
The trial starts with Screening (28 days), followed by N=420; Randomization: 2:1 Etrasimod (2 mg):placebo.
This leads into the 12-Week Treatment Period.
Subjects who meet the predefined definition for UC worsening at Week 12 may enter the APD334-303 Open-label extension.
The main trial continues into the 40-Week Treatment Period. Subjects with predefined UC worsening during this period may enter the APD334-303 Open-label extension.
The trial concludes at Week 52, followed by 2 and 4-week follow-up visits for subjects not continuing into open-label.
Statistical Note:
2-group Chi-square test, 1-sided significance level of 0.025, 90% power
Endpoints at Week 12 and Week 52, respectively
L2663: Line graph showing LSM (95% CI) change from baseline for Stool Frequency Subscore over 52 weeks for Etrasimod 2 mg (red line) and Placebo (teal line). The Y-axis ranges from -2.0 to 0.0. The Etrasimod group shows a greater reduction in the stool frequency subscore compared to the placebo group, starting early in the trial.
L2664: Line graph showing LSM (95% CI) change from baseline for Rectal Bleeding Subscore over 52 weeks for Etrasimod 2 mg and Placebo groups. The Y-axis ranges from -2.0 to 0.0. The X-axis shows Week (0, 4, 8, 12, 16, 20, 24, 28, 32, 36, 40, 44, 48, 52). Both groups show a decrease in rectal bleeding subscore from baseline. The Etrasimod 2 mg group generally shows a greater reduction in the subscore compared to the Placebo group, although the difference is not statistically significant based on the confidence intervals.

L2666: Line graph showing LSM (95% CI) change from baseline for Stool Frequency Subscore over 12 weeks for Etrasimod 2 mg and Placebo groups. The Y-axis ranges from -2.0 to 0.0. The X-axis shows Week (2, 4, 6, 8, 10, 12). The Placebo group (blue line) shows a change from approximately -0.5 at Week 2 to approximately -0.7 at Week 12. The Etrasimod 2 mg group (red line) shows a change from approximately -0.5 at Week 2 to approximately -1.2 at Week 12. Error bars represent 95% confidence intervals.
L2667: Line graph showing LSM (95% CI) change from baseline for Rectal Bleeding Subscore (RBS) over 12 weeks for Etrasimod 2 mg and Placebo groups. The Y-axis ranges from -2.0 to 0.0. The X-axis shows Week (2, 4, 6, 8, 10, 12). The Placebo group (blue line) shows a gradual decrease in RBS change from baseline, starting around -0.5 at Week 2 and ending around -0.8 at Week 12. The Etrasimod 2 mg group (red line) shows a steeper decrease, starting around -0.5 at Week 2, dropping to approximately -1.0 at Week 4, and stabilizing around -1.2 to -1.3 from Week 8 to Week 12. Error bars represent 95% Confidence Intervals (CI).
L2668: eDISH Plot showing Same-Day Total Bilirubin (x ULN) on the Y-axis (log scale, 0.1 to 100) versus Maximum ALT (x ULN) on the X-axis (log scale, 0.1 to 100). Temple's Corollary Range (above 2 x ULN).

L2669: Diagram illustrating the Structural Population Pharmacokinetic Model of Etrasimod. The model consists of three compartments: Absorption compartment, Central (V_2), and Peripheral (V_3). The Dose input is characterized by F_1 and D_1. The Absorption compartment is connected to the Central compartment by a first-order absorption rate constant k_a. The Central and Peripheral compartments are connected by intercompartmental clearance Q. The Central compartment is connected to elimination by systemic clearance CL.
L2670: Figure 16: Visual Predictive Check of the Final PopPK Model for Etrasimod. The plot shows Prediction-corrected Etrasimod Concentration (ng/mL) on the Y-axis versus Time from First Dose (hour) on the X-axis. The X-axis ranges from 0 to 10000 hours. The Y-axis ranges from 30 to 100 ng/mL. Observed data (black lines) show the median (solid line), 5th (dashed line), and 95th (dashed line) percentiles. The shaded regions (blue and grey) represent the 95% confidence intervals of the corresponding percentiles.
L2671: Diagram illustrating the Base Lymphocyte Model, which consists of two main components: Pharmacokinetics (PK) and Pharmacodynamics (PD).
Pharmacokinetics (PK) Compartment: The PK model is a three-compartment model. A Dose input leads to the Absorption compartment, characterized by parameters F_1 and D_1. The Absorption compartment is connected to the Central compartment (V_2) via the absorption rate constant k_a. The Central compartment is connected to the Peripheral compartment (V_3) via the intercompartmental clearance Q. Systemic clearance (CL) acts on the Central compartment. The PK model is labeled "Pharmacokinetics".
Pharmacodynamics (PD) Compartment: The PD model involves Lymphocytes. The Central compartment output influences the Lymphocytes compartment via parameters I_{max} and IC_{50}. The Lymphocytes compartment is influenced by the egress rate constant k_{in} and the removal rate constant k_{out}. The PD model is labeled "Pharmacodynamics".
Additional parameters shown near the Lymphocytes compartment include Amp_{24} and Ph_{24}.

The observed data (black lines) shows the median (solid line), 5th (dashed line), and 95th (dashed line) percentiles. The blue and grey shaded regions represent the 95% confidence intervals for the corresponding percentiles. The observed data shows a sharp decrease in lymphocyte count starting around week 1, reaching a minimum around week 20 (median near 0.3), and then recovering towards baseline levels by week 60.

L2675: Diagram illustrating the PK-PD Model for Etrasimod Effect on Heart Rate, divided into Pharmacokinetics (PK) and Pharmacodynamics (PD) sections.
Pharmacokinetics (PK): The model consists of three compartments: Absorption compartment, Central (V_2), and Peripheral (V_3). Dose input is characterized by F_1, ALAG_1, and D_1. The Absorption compartment is linked to the Central compartment via absorption rate constant k_a. The Central and Peripheral compartments are linked by inter-compartmental clearance Q. The Central compartment is linked to the Tolerance Compartment via clearance CL and a parameter \theta.
Pharmacodynamics (PD): The Heart Rate compartment is influenced by the Central compartment via parameters \theta I_{max} and IC_{50}, and the Tolerance Compartment via parameter \theta_{tol,50}. The Heart Rate compartment is labeled with observed metrics: HR_{BL}, AMP_{24}, and PH_{24}. The Tolerance Compartment is linked to the Heart Rate compartment via parameter \gamma. The Tolerance Compartment is also linked to the Central compartment via input rate constant k_{tol,in} and output rate constant k_{tol,out}.

L2683: Flowchart illustrating the Gatekeeping Approach to Control the Type I Error Rate for APD334-301.
Family 1 (Primary)
\alpha=0.05
• The proportion of subjects achieving clinical remission at Week 12
• The proportion of subjects achieving clinical remission at Week 52
Proceed to F2 only if both null hypotheses in F1 are rejected
Family 2 (Higher-rank key secondary)
\alpha=0.05
Truncated Hochberg procedure within F2
• The proportion of subjects achieving endoscopic improvement at Week 52
• The proportion of subjects achieving endoscopic improvement at Week 12
• The proportion of subjects achieving symptomatic remission at Week 52
• The proportion of subjects achieving symptomatic remission at Week 12
Proceed to F3 if at least one null hypothesis in F2 is rejected
Family 3 (Lower-rank key secondary)
\alpha=\alpha(F3)
Traditional Hochberg procedure within F3
• The proportion of subjects in clinical remission at Week 52 and who had not been receiving corticosteroids for \ge 12 weeks prior to Week 52
• The proportion of subjects with mucosal healing at Week 52
• The proportion of subjects with mucosal healing at Week 12
• The proportion of subjects achieving clinical remission at both Weeks 12 and 52

Family 1 (Primary)
\alpha=0.05
• The proportion of subjects achieving clinical remission at Week 12
Proceed to F2 only if null hypothesis in F1 is rejected
Family 2 (Higher-rank key secondary)
\alpha=0.05
Truncated Hochberg procedure within F2
• The proportion of subjects achieving endoscopic improvement at Week 12
• The proportion of subjects achieving symptomatic remission at Week 12
Proceed to F3 if at least one null hypothesis in F2 is rejected
Family 3 (Lower-rank key secondary)
\alpha=\alpha(F3)
No adjustment necessary for single null hypothesis in F3
• The proportion of subjects with mucosal healing at Week 12

L2705: Figure 2A displays the Kaplan-Meier Survival Functions for Male Mice. The Y-axis represents Survival Probability (0.0 to 1.0) and the X-axis represents Survival time (week) (0 to 100). The legend indicates five dose groups:
0 mg/kg/day | Water Control
0 | Vehicle Control
2 mg/kg/day | Low Dose
6 mg/kg/day | Mid Dose
20 mg/kg/day | High Dose
The survival curves for all groups start at 1.0 survival probability. The curves generally decline over time, with the High Dose group showing the steepest decline, reaching approximately 0.3 survival probability by 100 weeks. The Water Control and Vehicle Control groups maintain the highest survival probabilities, around 0.4 to 0.5 by 100 weeks. The legend also indicates '+ Censored' data points.
L2706: Figure 2B displays the Kaplan-Meier Survival Functions for Female Mice. The Y-axis represents Survival Probability (0.0 to 1.0) and the X-axis represents Survival time (week) (0 to 100). The legend indicates five dose groups:
0 mg/kg/day | Water Control
0 | Vehicle Control
2 mg/kg/day | Low Dose
6 mg/kg/day | Mid Dose
20 mg/kg/day | High Dose
The survival curves for all groups start at 1.0 survival probability. The curves generally decline over time, with the High Dose group showing the steepest decline, reaching approximately 0.2 survival probability by 100 weeks. The Water Control and Vehicle Control groups maintain the highest survival probabilities, around 0.4 to 0.5 by 100 weeks. The legend also indicates '+ Censored' data points.
